# Port fidelity audit — 2026-09-09

Storage note (2026-09-14): bulk observations and before/after JSON baselines were
retired during the user-authorized generator migration. Paths below are historical
references; current tests use live native comparisons and retained failure seeds.

The initial audit found nine discrepancies. **F1–F9 are now repaired**, with permanent
comparative regressions in the ordinary property suite. The original isolated reproducer now
reports **51/51 matches**. Discovery details below are retained as historical evidence.

The follow-up also repaired the adjacent parent/coercion cases and the additional defects below.
Every repair has a shared Python/TypeScript comparison. The harness now checks exception classes and messages; initially, any two exceptions
counted as a match, including unrelated failures. Message checking was added in the
Gaussian constructor follow-up below.

| Repair | Upstream evidence / comparative coverage |
|---|---|
| Integer `.mod()` reduces by the ideal generated by the modulus; `%` remains signed | `structure/element.pyx:mod`, `rings/ideal.py:reduce`; signed/zero modulus cases |
| Zero integer valuation/ord and negative popcount/hamming weight return infinity | `integer.pyx:_valuation`, `popcount`; zero, invalid-base and negative boundaries |
| Integer reciprocals are Rational, including units; zero inversion/powers preserve the error message | `integer.pyx:7031-7055`, `_pow_long`; exact values, classes and zero error messages |
| Zero `ndigits` returns before checking the base | `integer.pyx:1833`; invalid-base zero controls |
| `xgcd(0,0)` returns `(0,0,0)` | Sage/GMP extended gcd; signed and 128-bit random inputs |
| Modulus-zero inverses of ±1 return the unit; zero prime-divisor factorization raises ArithmeticError | `integer.pyx:inverse_mod`, `prime_divisors`; unit, zero and invalid cases |
| CRT zero-modulus branches raise Sage exceptions instead of native RangeError | `arith/misc.py:3490-3499`; solvable/unsolvable and both-zero moduli |
| Negative Bell/partition indices raise the proper errors; Catalan(-1) is -1/2, earlier negative indices are zero | `combinat/combinat.py:342-344,451-455`, `combinat/partition.py:number_of_partitions` |
| Sigma's negative-exponent branch preserves the upstream AttributeError | `arith/misc.py:Sigma.__call__`; nonunits, units and zero |
| Quadratic class numbers use the existing PARI backend beyond the old lookup table | `integer.pyx:class_number`, `pari/buch1.c:quadclassno`; 33 discriminant/boundary cases |
| Invalid finite-curve points raise TypeError; curve equations omit zero terms and unit multipliers | `schemes/elliptic_curves/ell_generic.py:_repr_`, point construction; invalid points, valid controls and equation strings |
| Dependent lattice bases lose zero Gram-Schmidt rows, construct successfully, then raise IndexError when sampled | `matrix2.pyx:gram_schmidt`, `discrete_gaussian_lattice.py:584-587,862-865`; construction versus sampling cases |
| Natural integer logs handle zero and values beyond the JS Number range | `integer.pyx:log`; the existing documented floor adaptation, tested through 2048 bits |
| Quadratic residues modulo 2 are accepted | Finite-field `is_square` degree-one branch; both residue classes and signed lifts |
| Comparison harness rejects different exception classes and handles empty messages | `tests/property/compare.test.ts`; regression cases plus the newly exposed curve/lattice failures |

`integers` now covers all **107 implemented Integer methods** with 6,027 cases. Some methods are
port-only conveniences, some outputs intentionally choose a different root, and real logarithms
are approximate: their adapters are explicit in the oracle and `DEVIATIONS.md`. These counts
mean method dispatch coverage, not proof over all inputs. Known backend limits remain tracked.

The broader API audit remains in progress. [AUDIT-COVERAGE.md](AUDIT-COVERAGE.md) inventories all
production files, explicit/partial stubs, comparative areas and measured execution coverage.

## Scope and evidence

- Audited revision: `72b1e2524a7d6cd0f50e28d74d9e1b4ed2ecc491` (0.0.19).
- Bundled SageMath revision: `28a7d0422ba718b81d555a29ec02ef07cf6d92e9` (10.9.beta4).
- Executed oracle: SageMath 10.3; TypeScript executed with Bun. Each reported behavior was also
  checked in the bundled source. No finding depends on a known 10.3/10.9 difference.
- Read the existing July audit and the relevant accepted deviations/open gaps before classifying
  findings. These are additional paths, rather than repeats of the known Conway-table, RNG,
  backend-delegation or missing-API issues.
- Existing differential suite: **4673/4673 pass** (fast 539, slow 4134; zero errors).
- Public API documentation tests: **32 pass, zero failures**, 120 assertions. The new TypeScript
  audit scripts pass targeted Biome checks; `git diff --check` is clean.
- New targeted audit: **51 cases, 22 matches, 29 discrepancies**, grouped into the nine findings.
  Cases are deterministic, share a JSON input list and include passing controls. Exception text is
  printed, but only differences in result or exception class count as mismatches.
- This was a focused source audit of core paths, plus execution of all existing differential areas.
  It is not a line-by-line audit of every module or every PARI/FLINT/NTL routine, and passing tests
  do not establish equivalence outside their inputs.

Reproduce from the repository root:

```sh
bun tests/audit/2026-09/compare.ts
bun run test:property:fast
bun run test:property:slow
```

The standalone audit command **exits 1 while discrepancies remain**. It is deliberately outside the
ordinary passing test tiers, so the audit does not redefine incorrect behavior as expected. Its
shared cases and the Python/TypeScript probes are in `tests/audit/2026-09/`.

## Initial findings (resolved)

### F1 — High: custom finite-field moduli are never checked for irreducibility

**Port:** `packages/sagemath-ts/src/rings/finite_rings/finite_field_extension.ts:684-703`
(`FiniteFieldExtension` constructor, reachable through exported `GFpn`).

`GFpn(5n, 2, [4, 0])` builds a field modulo `a^2 - 1`, reports `is_field() === true`, and
has nonzero elements `a - 1` and `a + 1` whose product is zero. The port checks only the degree.
The coefficient list here correctly omits the implicit leading 1, as the port API requires.

**Sage:** `GF(25, 'a', modulus=[4, 0, 1])` raises
`ValueError: finite field modulus must be irreducible but it is not`.
Bundled `reference/sage/src/sage/rings/finite_rings/finite_field_constructor.py:846-847`
performs that check by default. Confirmed for characteristics 3, 5 and 7.

**Impact:** field-only algorithms can receive a ring with zero divisors under a false field
contract. Check irreducibility before exposing the parent; an unchecked construction must require
an explicit opt-out if one is added.

### F2 — High: prime-field arithmetic and equality ignore incompatible parents

**Port:** `packages/sagemath-ts/src/rings/finite_rings/finite_field_extension.ts:74-76`,
`:121-126`, `:150-158` (`PrimeFieldElement`, the class returned by the public `GF(p)`).

```ts
GF(5n).one().add(GF(7n).one())       // 2, Sage raises TypeError
GF(5n).one().div(GF(7n).__call__(2)) // 4, Sage raises TypeError
GF(5n).one().eq(GF(7n).one())        // true, Sage returns False
```

`_coerceValue` takes another element's residue without checking its characteristic. Division is
worse: it inverts the denominator in its original field, then multiplies in the numerator's field.
`eq` compares only the numeric residues.

**Sage:** binary operations go through canonical coercion before field arithmetic
(`reference/sage/src/sage/structure/coerce.pyx:1236-1242`, `:1286`); no canonical map exists
between these different characteristics. Explicit conversion `GF(5)(GF(7)(6)) == 1` **does** work
in Sage and is not the defect. Confirmed with field pairs (5,7) and (7,11), with (5,5) controls.

**Impact:** invalid arithmetic silently returns plausible values, and equality conflates elements
of incompatible fields. Validate compatible parents on arithmetic and implement cross-parent
comparison separately from explicit conversion. Comparing parent identity alone is insufficient:
this port intentionally constructs multiple instances of the same field.

### F3 — High: polynomial operations silently identify different variables

**Port:** `packages/sagemath-ts/src/rings/polynomial/polynomial_element.ts:138-148`, `:376-387`.

For `x = new PolynomialRing(GF(7n), 'x').gen()` and
`y = new PolynomialRing(GF(7n), 'y').gen()`, `x.eq(y)` returns `true`, and `x.sub(y)` returns zero.
The methods inspect coefficient arrays without checking polynomial parents or variable names.

**Sage:** generators of `GF(7)['x']` and `GF(7)['y']` compare unequal; subtraction raises `TypeError`.
Sage's coefficient-array subtraction (`polynomial_element.pyx:12404`) runs only after the
parent/coercion machinery (`structure/coerce.pyx:1219-1286`) has accepted the operands.
Confirmed over GF(5) and GF(7).

**Impact:** expressions involving distinct indeterminates can collapse to zero. Preserve ring
compatibility at the public operation boundary; explicit variable renaming is a separate operation.
This is distinct from the already documented absence of unique parent instances.

### F4 — Medium: square-free decomposition loses the leading unit

**Port:** `packages/sagemath-ts/src/rings/polynomial/polynomial_element.ts:1244-1245`, `:1298`.

Over GF(7), `f = 2*(x+1)^2` gives `[[x+1, 2]]`. Multiplying every returned factor with its
multiplicity reconstructs `(x+1)^2`, not `f`. The routine makes `f` monic and does not retain the
leading coefficient in its result. Confirmed for the same family over GF(3), GF(5), GF(7);
unit-1 controls pass.

**Sage:** returns `(2) * (x + 1)^2`, including a unit of 2. Bundled
`reference/sage/src/sage/rings/finite_rings/finite_field_base.pyx:624` records
`unit = f.leading_coefficient()` and returns it in the `Factorization`.

**Impact:** the returned decomposition represents a different polynomial. The port's documented
array adaptation for `factor()` preserves nontrivial units as degree-zero factors; this routine
needs equivalent preservation. The probe compares reconstructed polynomials, not display or container shape.

### F5 — Medium: CRT returns negative, noncanonical representatives for negative moduli

**Port:** `packages/sagemath-ts/src/arith/misc.ts:1209-1213`.

`crt(1n, 0n, -3n, 5n)` returns `-20n`; Sage returns `10`. Two further examples return `-25`
instead of `5`, and `-19` instead of `11`. All are congruent answers, but deterministic output
parity requires Sage's canonical representative.

**Cause:** `(_m / g) * _n` is a signed product, not Sage's nonnegative lcm. When the JS remainder
is negative, adding this negative product makes it even more negative.

**Sage:** `reference/sage/src/sage/arith/misc.py:3498-3499` reduces modulo `lcm(m, n)`.
The audit exhausts all 15 residue pairs for moduli (-3,5): 3 mismatches, 12 matches.
Use the existing nonnegative `lcm` or its equivalent before normalizing.

### F6 — Medium: negative Frobenius powers fail instead of applying the inverse automorphism

**Port:** `packages/sagemath-ts/src/rings/finite_rings/finite_field_extension.ts:425-428`.

`GF(9n).gen().frobenius(-1)` raises a native `RangeError`. Sage returns `2*a + 1`
(integer representation 7). GF(4) with power -1 and GF(25) with power -2 also fail in the port.

**Sage:** `reference/sage/src/sage/rings/finite_rings/element_base.pyx:943-975` explicitly supports
negative powers and computes `self**(p**(k % n))`. The port computes `p ** BigInt(power)` directly.

**Impact:** inverse Frobenius is unavailable through an implemented API. Reducing the power modulo
the extension degree also avoids constructing unnecessarily huge exponents for positive powers.
Zero and positive-one controls pass.

### F7 — Medium: generic additive discrete logs crash on extension-field elements

**Port:** `packages/sagemath-ts/src/groups/generic.ts:732` and
`packages/sagemath-ts/src/rings/finite_rings/finite_field_extension.ts:353-355`.

With `a = GF(9n).gen()`, `discrete_log(a.add(a), a, 3n, '+')` throws a raw `TypeError`
while Sage's `discrete_log(2*a, a, 3, operation='+')` returns 2. Reproduces over GF(25) as well.

**Cause:** generic group code implements additive scalar multiplication by calling `x.mul(n)`
with a bigint, whereas extension-field `mul` assumes its argument has a `.lift` polynomial.
The resulting undefined operand fails inside polynomial multiplication.

**Sage:** `reference/sage/src/sage/groups/generic.py:237-241`, `:1056` implements the additive operation through
integer scalar multiplication and the normal coercion/action machinery. The corresponding
finite-field operation is valid. This is separate from the documented missing extension-field
`.log()` method: the reproducer uses the already exported generic function and supplies the order.

**Impact:** the public group and finite-field implementations cannot interoperate on this valid
input. Supply a working scalar action, or implement generic integer multiples through the existing
doubling algorithm instead of assuming every `.mul` accepts integers.

### F8 — Medium: integer powering rejects every negative exponent

**Port:** `packages/sagemath-ts/src/rings/integer_ring.ts:525-530`.

`new Integer(2n).pow(-3n)` raises `ValueError`; Sage returns the exact rational `1/8`.
`(-2)^(-3)` should be `-1/8`; even `1^(-1)` is rejected.

**Sage:** `reference/sage/src/sage/rings/integer.pyx:2384-2414` constructs a `Rational`
for a negative exponent, rejecting only zero to a negative power.

**Impact:** exact reciprocal powers supported upstream are unavailable. This is an additional
entry point to the already documented missing rational inversion behavior, not a requirement
for symbolic arithmetic. The port already has a `Rational` class. A fix must update the exported
return type and `LLM.md` together. The positive-exponent control passes.

### F9 — Medium: `Integer.exact_log(0, base)` rejects an upstream-defined value

**Port:** `packages/sagemath-ts/src/rings/integer_ring.ts:697-699`.

`new Integer(0n).exact_log(2n)` raises `ValueError: self must be positive`.
Sage returns negative infinity. The same discrepancy occurs for base 10; the `(8,2)` control passes.

**Sage:** `reference/sage/src/sage/rings/integer.pyx:2803-2805` handles a zero receiver before
validating signs or the base. This is not covered by the documented `valuation(0,p)` exception
adaptation, which concerns another API.

**Impact:** zero inputs fail where callers expect an extended numeric result. Choose and document
an infinity representation consistent with the public return type, and preserve the upstream
zero-input branch ordering.

## Initial repair order (completed)

The initial plan was to fix F1–F3 first: they violate object invariants and can contaminate downstream calculations without
an exception. Then address F4–F7, and the extended return types in F8–F9. Move the relevant standalone
cases into the ordinary area-based property suite as each defect is fixed. Keep the oracle assertions
intact; the current passing suite missed these inputs.

## Rational repair batch (0.0.22)

The `rationals` area contains 5,757 shared comparisons and dispatches all 84 public Rational
methods, accessors and construction paths. The source review covered `rational.pyx` coercion,
rounding, arithmetic, roots, valuations, heights, polynomials and numeric conversion, plus
`rational_field.py` coercion and rational entry conversion in `matrix_operations.ts`.

| Discrepancy | Repair and comparative regression |
|---|---|
| `toNumber`, `n`, `numerical_approx` overflowed numerator and denominator independently, lost subnormal values and rounded ties incorrectly | Transcribe `mpq_get_d_nearest` from bundled `rational.pyx:3898-4096`; compare exact binary64 encodings at both signs, underflow/overflow boundaries, sticky remainders and large random fractions |
| Matrix rational conversion discarded a nonzero remainder | Use the shared corrected conversion; a 1×1 norm of `(2^65+2^12+1)/2^65` now rounds to `1+2^-52` |
| Global and local heights overflowed on huge exact integers | Use the existing overflow-safe integer logarithm; compare 400- and 1,000-digit inputs |
| Zero `ord` and numerator valuation rejected infinity | Preserve Sage branch order and the existing infinity sentinel; test invalid and valid bases |
| Valuation errors differed from upstream | Compare exact class/message and use Sage's message separately for `val_unit` and ordinary valuations |
| Gamma at integral poles threw | Return the existing unsigned-infinity object; compare nonpositive and positive integers |
| Tuple/constructor zero denominators raised the wrong class | Raise Sage's `ValueError`; retain explicit division-by-zero guards in reciprocal root operations |
| Floating coercion used decimal strings instead of simplest rationals | Delegate to the existing real field coercion; compare `1/3`, tiny fractions, nonfinite values, Rational.from and QQ |
| String parsing accepted decimal strings/empty input/invalid syntax and missed base-zero grammar | Match Sage/GMP integer and quotient grammar; cover each string via fromString, from and QQ |
| Gaussian lattice numeric bases/centers used decimal-string coercion | Delegate to Rational.from(number), with six shared constructor comparisons; tiny centers now use Sage’s simplest rational |
| Negative decimal rounding precision was ignored | Scale exactly by rational powers of ten, round to even and unscale |

All rational comparisons pass. The complete suite passed 16,506 comparisons before the last
56 added boundary cases; the final focused run passes all 5,757 rational cases. Those last
cases additionally repaired local-height overflow and exact valuation error text. The executed
public API examples pass. Detailed unit/line coverage appears in the coverage inventory.

Dispatch coverage does not mean the whole rational input domain is implemented: symbolic
square roots and nonintegral gamma remain explicit gaps. The source review also identified
separate duplicated binary-search integer-root algorithms; those are queued for the continuing
algorithm-fidelity review rather than described as GMP delegation.


Rational-batch validation: 5,886 fast units passed (32 existing skips). The 12 affected slow
files contain 734 tests: the first run found eight Gaussian coercion failures and one
five-second timeout; the repaired Gaussian file passes all 84 tests and the isolated
hyperelliptic file passes all 31 tests, completing those 734 tests. `rand_stats` passes
288/288 after its six added coercion cases. Merged current LCOV covers 80,454/101,207 lines
(79.49%); edited source files are excluded from stale reports before merging. Type checking
still reports 1,373 sagemath-ts diagnostics, matching the prior batch after source-line and
union-order normalization; the workspace command also reports 161 consumer diagnostics.


## RationalField and integer-root repair batch (0.0.23)

All 38 public RationalField members now have comparative dispatchers. Source review found
five discrepancy families, reproduced by 61 mismatches before repair: `some_elements` stopped
at 48 and used the wrong denominators from n=10; `gen(1)` raised ValueError instead of IndexError;
tuple coercion divided nonintegral components rather than coercing each to Integer; fractional
prime bounds were rejected instead of rounded up; random generation skipped ZZ's unconditional
draw, used a bounded default, and rejected Sage's zero-bound normalization. Six additional
nonfinite-tuple cases pin Integer's OverflowError/ValueError behavior.

The root review found duplicated full-size binary searches where Sage delegates to GMP's
precision-doubling Newton routine. The shared replacement follows GMP 6.3.0's size schedule,
Newton correction and padded-limb optimization over native BigInt. It uses an exact one-bit
seed, documented in DEVIATIONS.md. The exponent-interface review reproduced 22 mismatches
at C int boundaries before repair. Comparative cases include roots with 8,193-bit magnitudes,
exact powers and both neighboring inputs, negative operands and maximum valid exponents.
The formerly queued root-algorithm issue is now repaired.

The inventory now distinguishes actual class members from nested local functions, so a local
Cartesian-product generator is not incorrectly reported as a missing public method. The
Integer/Rational member totals remain 107 and 84; RationalField adds 38.


Validation: the full comparative suite passes 17,259/17,259. After completing GMP's padded-limb
optimization, the final affected integer/rational run passes 12,451/12,451. Final fast units:
5,888 passed, 32 existing skips; affected slow units: 734 passed. Colocated root regressions
exercise the padding threshold, exactness and maximum valid exponent. The workspace typecheck
still fails with the same diagnostic kinds as the previous batch. Biome and public examples pass.

The inventory also recognizes dependency stubs carrying `FLINT_NOT_IMPLEMENTED` /
`NTL_NOT_IMPLEMENTED` / `PARI_NOT_IMPLEMENTED` markers even when they throw plain Error.
Those were missing from the earlier NotImplementedError-only count; this is an inventory
correction, not newly unimplemented behavior.


## Integer and scalar constructor repair batch (0.0.24)

Integer and ZZ construction now use the shared transcription of Integer.__init__ and
`mpz_set_str_python` in `integer.pyx:698-804,7402-7537`. Comparative cases cover both wrapper
modes, all implemented input families, prefixes/whitespace/underscores, invalid strings and
bases, exact Python-style diagnostics, nonfinite numbers, arbitrary-precision list bases,
4,097-bit string values in non-power-of-two bases, conversion hooks and modular lifts.

The initial constructor sweep reported 616 mismatches. The oracle initially called ZZ for
both wrapper modes; reviewing Q_to_Z clarified that an explicit base on a Rational is rejected
by the ring's conversion map but accepted by Integer.__init__. The oracle now dispatches the
two constructors separately and the port preserves that distinction. String-base C int
boundaries reproduced four further mismatches before repair. The corrected Integer area has
6,934 passing comparisons.

Factory follow-up reproduced 14 discrepancies in 15 added cases: Rational's omitted value and
Integer-wrapper inputs, boolean/null coercion and QQ tuple components. QQ(None) returns zero,
whereas QQ([None]) enters Rational.__set_value and raises TypeError; a failed comparison caught
and corrected an initially over-broad shared None guard. QQ tuple strings now use the Integer
grammar through the same coercion path. The final Rational area has 6,302 passing comparisons.

The public reference and executable examples document the widened signatures. Structural
conversion interfaces and the preserved upstream conversion-map behavior are described in
DEVIATIONS.md and DESIGN.md.

Validation: all 18,044 configured comparisons pass. Fast units: 5,888 pass and 32 existing
skips; affected slow units: 734 pass. Executable public examples: 32 pass. Type-check
diagnostic kinds are unchanged from the existing baseline. Current merged execution
coverage is 80,525/101,343 lines (79.46%); this is separate from comparative coverage.

## Finite-field member and coercion repair batch (0.0.25)

The finite_fields area now compares all 99 public members of the two prime-field
implementations, the extension parent and their element classes. The expanded tests execute
actual field cardinality and generator-order operations; two old dispatchers had returned
formulas without exercising the implementation. The source inventory checks the explicit
member-to-dispatcher mapping so a newly added member cannot silently count as covered.

Repairs with comparative regressions:

- Integer/Rational/boolean/None and integer-string construction, direct element construction,
  Integer/boolean arithmetic and equality, and the difference between explicit Rational
  conversion and forbidden implicit Rational arithmetic.
- fromInteger bounds; incompatible extension-parent conversion; foreign polynomial inputs
  changing coefficient ring before reduction, including low-degree polynomials.
- Zero inverse/division errors, nonsquare same-field sqrt errors, multiplicative-order-of-zero
  errors and direct prime-parent validation errors.
- Seeded finite-field draws through Sage's Python random stream, with the newer bundled
  extension default transcribed explicitly into the installed-10.3 oracle.
- Prime-field multiplicative order delegates to PARI Fp_order, whose word-sized Fl_order and
  larger-modulus rec_order algorithms now live in ff.ts and are shared with elliptic/group.ts.
  Comparative cases include moduli above 2^64 and nonminimal known order multiples.
- Matrix change_ring: a stale unit assertion required Z/8 -> GF(7) to fail. Sage converts
  each entry successfully; twelve live matrix comparisons now protect this behavior.

The paired tests explicitly select the PARI polynomial-basis backend and spell its option
`impl` for Sage 10.3. They use Sage's vector conversion for coefficient lists. This avoids
mistaking backend/version interface differences for numerical port defects. Root-extension,
polynomial-string parsing, random keyword options and factory-option gaps remain open in
DEVIATIONS.md; 99/99 member dispatch is not a full-input-domain equivalence claim.

Validation: 29,103 full-suite comparisons pass, followed by 925/925 matrix-area comparisons
including twelve new cases (29,115 now configured). Finite fields: 11,137/11,137. All 734
affected slow tests and 109 focused PARI tests pass. The final instrumented fast run had
5,886 passes, 32 existing skips and two timeouts; isolated reruns passed the CM comparison

## Finite-field factory repair — 0.0.26

The name/modulus overloads now reach the implementation instead of being ignored or
stringified. Shared cases cover full polynomial coefficients, nonmonic normalization,
wrong degree, reducibility, linear roots, algorithm choices and invalid field orders.
Name tests compare exact errors, including comma-separated duplicates, Unicode, control
characters and the Python/JavaScript whitespace difference. The eleven existing Conway
characteristics now include their verified degree-one polynomials. Order decomposition
uses PARI `Z_isanypower`; explicit controls cover composite perfect powers and large inputs.

The finite-field area grew from 11,137 to 12,465 comparisons. All 30,437 cases in the full
run passed before the final six whitespace regressions; the final 12,465-case field rerun
passed. Fast units: 5,888 pass and 32 existing skips. Focused field/PARI/public-doc/deviation
checks: 291 pass; affected slow tests: 946 pass. Measured line coverage is 80,618/105,177
(76.65%). Type checking adds no diagnostics and removes two against the prior batch.

The installed Sage 10.3 oracle ignores prime-field names and has an upstream perfect-power
bug already fixed in the bundled source. The harness explicitly executes Sage's name
validator and PARI decomposition for those newer branches. The zero-modulus FLINT exception
mapping on Sage 10.9 remains unverified; see DEVIATIONS.md. Broader factory options,
polynomial-expression input, root-extension options and the repository audit remain open.

## Prime-field square roots — 1.0.0

A 1,560-case option/default sweep reproduced 928 mismatches. Both prime-element classes
now match `integer_mod.pyx`: a nonsquare's default root lives in the quadratic extension
with modulus x^2-a and generator sqrt<a>; `extend:false` keeps the original error; `all:true`
returns sorted base-field roots. The specialized small-modulus search can return [] before
attempting extension, whereas the general extension/all branch raises NotImplementedError.
Twelve additional tests verify unknown-keyword errors. The Sage 10.3 oracle explicitly
adapts the newer source's added NotImplementedError explanation.

Full comparison run: 32,003 pass before the final twelve keyword tests; final field area:
14,037 pass. Fast units: 5,888 pass and 32 existing skips; focused field/public-doc/deviation
checks: 232 pass; affected slow tests: 946 pass. Measured line coverage is 80,628/105,138
(76.69%). The type-check diagnostic set is unchanged after normalizing union order.

The default return type now includes an extension element. Elliptic point enumeration uses
`extend:false` to remain over its original field. The major version increment follows the
repository's breaking-change rule; it does not assert complete audit coverage or implementation.

## Polynomial-string construction and parser — 1.1.0

The initial string sweep reproduced 578 mismatches. Extension strings now go through their
polynomial ring and Sage's recursive-descent arithmetic grammar. The implementation preserves
integer exponents before field reduction, implicit multiplication only before names, unary and
exponent precedence, factorials of numeric expressions, and comparison expressions. Fractions
can cancel before final polynomial coercion; a field expression such as the string `a^-1`
is rejected by that intermediate polynomial constructor, as Sage does.

Shared regressions cover coercion from QQ, scalar versus polynomial division by zero, the FpT
versus generic fraction-field errors, characteristic-two exponent errors, and GF2X/NTL parent
labels. Larger field presentations explicitly use Sage's PARI modulus to match the documented
fallback outside the port's Conway table. The arithmetic reuses the existing rational-function
numerator/denominator code; it does not add a public fraction-field representation.

The new parser area directly compares tokenizer state, all implemented grammar entry points,
lookup callbacks, Unicode Digit/whitespace classification and character/C-int bounds. It has
566 cases; the finite-field area has 14,904. The full suite passed 33,443 comparisons before
five final parser-boundary cases, and the final parser area passes all 566. Fast units: 5,895
pass and 32 existing skips; affected slow tests: 946 pass; focused parser/field/polynomial/docs:
184 pass. Measured line coverage: 81,019/105,652 (76.68%); parser callable dispatch: 23/23,
excluding seven explicit stubs. Type-check diagnostic kinds are unchanged after normalizing the added string union.
General parser function calls, sequences, matrices and broader symbolic/coefficient promotions
remain explicit gaps in DEVIATIONS.md; this is not full repository or input-domain coverage.


## Modular-integer coercion, random stream and backend repair

The new shared modular area initially reproduced 1,758 mismatches in 3,389 cases. Fixes cover
exact scalar constructors (Integer, Rational, boolean, default/null and strings), arithmetic
coercion and equality, result-parent selection, seeded ring randomness with the optional
symmetric bound, generic-ring order validation and native/GMP negative-power diagnostics.
Negative powers now invert the powered residue, preserving its value in an inversion error.

The canonical quotient pushout uses the gcd of moduli and rejects a newly formed trivial
quotient, as Sage's QuotientFunctor does. Compatible prime-field operands promote to that
field. Comparisons record full result-parent displays, so a matching residue in the wrong
ring fails. Mod now gives elements a complete IntegerModRing parent. Multiplicative order
now delegates to PARI's znorder prime-power/valuation/lcm algorithm; 92 direct backend cases
cover powers of two, odd prime powers, mixed composites and a 127-bit prime.

Final area: 3,517 pass, including 36 final string controls; all 34 public element/ring members
have dispatchers (existing logarithm cases remain in groups_modn). Full suite: 36,929 pass
before those final controls. Fast units: 5,895 pass and 32 existing skips; affected slow units:
946 pass; final focused units/docs: 112 pass. Current instrumented lines: 81,166/105,683 (76.80%).
Type-check primary diagnostic kinds are unchanged: overload declarations repeat 18 existing
RingElement diagnostics, while broader input signatures remove 149 prior diagnostic instances.
The type check remains failing. Zero/negative factory inputs and broader symbolic/coercion
frameworks remain explicit open gaps; dispatch coverage does not close those domains.


## Modular factories and zero-modulus return types

Added 373 comparative cases for Zmod/Integers/IntegerModRingFactory, signed-key parent identity,
Mod zero-value identity and explicit modular/prime parents. The initial 321 cases reproduced
240 mismatches; four identity comparisons and 32 explicit-prime-parent failures exposed the
remaining branches. Integral JavaScript numbers are represented by Sage Integers in the
zero-Mod identity oracle, matching the project's existing number-type mapping.

Factories now normalize negative orders and return ZZ for zero/default orders. Mod returns
the caller's original input at modulus zero, before inspecting its optional parent; otherwise
an explicit parent overrides the modulus. Prime-parent dispatch preserves the corresponding
prime-element API. WeakRef/FinalizationRegistry implements Sage's weak signed-order cache.
Conditional types narrow literal orders and expose the union for a dynamic possibly-zero
order. Group exponents and rational periods construct their proved-positive finite rings;
LWE retains its existing nonzero-modulus precondition. These return-type changes require the
major version 2.0.0 under repository policy; they do not mark full audit coverage.

All 37,338 comparative cases pass, including 3,890 modular cases. Final fast suite: 5,902 pass
and 32 existing skips. An old test incorrectly required Zmod(0) and negative orders to fail;
it now asserts Sage's outputs and separately tests the direct class's positive-order rule.
All 946 affected slow tests pass, 454 focused tests pass, and 102 corrected legacy/docs tests
pass. No type-check diagnostics were added or removed relative to 1.2.0. Measured instrumented
lines: 81,217/105,513 (76.97%). Category/is_field factory options, general symbolic/coercion
frameworks and LWE over ZZ remain tracked open domains.


## Prime-field power dispatch and original exponent types

Added 672 prime-field comparisons and 225 modular-exponent comparisons. The initial expanded
field run had 266 differences: six required the existing integral-JavaScript-number oracle
mapping, and the remaining discrepancies involved native/GMP power errors and exponent
coercion. A subsequent modular sweep reproduced 24 additional cases where Rational/string
exponents convert to small negative integers but must retain the GMP error path.

Both prime-element classes now reuse IntegerMod.pow, matching Sage's inheritance. The shared
path retains the original exponent type, then coerces through Integer, raises exact errors
for non-integral exponents, performs modular exponentiation and finally inversion. Public
pow signatures include the constructor-coercible scalar inputs exercised by the comparisons.
Results retain their original prime parent; there is no linear exponentiation algorithm.

All 38,235 comparisons pass (15,576 field and 4,115 modular). Fast suite: 5,902 pass and 32
existing skips before the final colocated regression; final focused units/docs: 119 pass.
All 946 affected slow tests pass. Type-check diagnostic instances are unchanged from 2.0.0.
Current instrumented lines: 81,201/105,665 (76.85%). Mixed-parent arithmetic, extension backend
architecture and further finite-ring/polynomial input domains remain under review.


## Mixed finite-ring arithmetic and sequence multiplication — 2.1.0

The 7,665-case mixed-operand sweep reproduced 3,647 mismatches: 837 multiplication,
822 addition, 822 subtraction, 822 division and 344 equality results. It crossed both
prime implementations, PARI extension fields, quotient rings, integers, rationals,
booleans, null, strings, lists and finite/nonfinite JavaScript number inputs. Each
comparison records the result parent as well as the value or exact exception.

All four element classes now share canonical maps rather than using explicit constructors
for implicit arithmetic. Compatible quotient rings, prime subfields and extension parents
promote before the operation; different named extensions remain incompatible. Division by
a promoted extension zero uses its own backend error. Equality rejects inputs that only
explicit constructors can convert. Named extension compatibility retains the documented
structural-parent adapter for independently constructed TypeScript fields.

This sweep also found an error in the older modular comparative oracle: Python string/list
multiplication can return a native sequence, so calling result.parent() raised AttributeError.
The oracle now uses Sage's global parent(result), and the port returns the actual repeated
sequence. Another 314 comparisons cover Unicode strings, list repetition, empty sequences,
backend sizes and the signed 64-bit index boundary. Colocated tests additionally verify
shallow list identity and the exact common parent. Runtime allocation limits remain an
explicit host-language boundary rather than a claimed error-equivalence result.

All 46,214 comparisons pass, including 23,555 finite-field and 4,115 modular cases. Fast units:
5,907 pass with 32 existing skips; final focused field/API tests: 251 pass. Type checking
still fails: 763 primary diagnostic instances versus 1,397 before this batch. Wider overloads
and equality signatures remove many prior incompatibilities and change the manifestation
of some existing recursive RingElement/unknown-vector constraints. This is not a clean
TypeScript build. Finite-field constructor options and extension backend delegation remain
under active source/input review; this batch does not establish full behavioral coverage.


The affected slow suite passed 945 tests with one five-second timeout. Both matching
point-counting cases pass in isolation; the timed-out case takes 2.76 s. After the final
union overload declarations, 252 focused/API/replay tests pass, including all 7,979 new
cases replayed against the generated Sage transcript. Modified production files were removed
from older LCOV inputs and measured again at their current line numbers. The merged report
records 81,148/105,804 instrumented lines (76.70%); this is not behavioral/branch coverage.


## Generator indices, prime parent coercion and integer hooks — 2.2.0

The retained constructor/index cases reproduced 528 discrepancies: 351 generator-index
and 177 parent-constructor results. The original sweep included 48 setup-only cases which
never invoked the target method; these were removed after identifying mismatched setup
constructors. Prime/PARI field gen uses Python truthiness, while quotient gen delegates
to ZZ.gen's equality-to-zero check. Both prime constructors now coerce the order with ZZ
before validating it; legacy check:false still rejects nonpositive orders and permits one.

Constructor cases involving field elements exposed missing _integer_ hooks on modern
prime and extension elements. These now return the integer representative of prime-subfield
values and reject nonconstant extension values with Sage's ValueError. Another 178 cases
exercise the hooks directly and through both integer constructors. Following the same index
rule into QQ reproduced eight further mismatches in 43 cases; QQ.gen now accepts zero
Integer/Rational/finite elements and false without constructor-coercing strings or lists.

Added 1,209 cases in total. All 47,380 full-suite comparisons passed before the final QQ
addition; the final field/rational run passes 31,066 (24,764 field and 6,302 rational).
Fast suite: 5,909 pass and 32 existing skips before final QQ/API additions. Final focused
units/docs: 269 pass, plus a successful replay of 1,613 new-parent/hook and existing QQ
cases against generated Sage transcripts. All 946 affected slow tests pass with a 15-second
per-test timeout to avoid the prior five-second scheduling flake. Existing type-check
diagnostic instances remain unchanged at 763. Current instrumented coverage, after removing
stale reports for all five edited production files: 81,193/106,081 (76.54%).

Finite-field dispatch coverage is now 101/101 members, including the two new conversion
hooks. This does not close unchecked nonprime-parent arithmetic, other conversion protocols,
extension backend delegation or general category/coercion input domains.


## PARI quotient kernels and extension arithmetic — 2.3.0

A 3,850-case sweep reproduced 1,478 mismatches. FpXQ_pow rejected negative exponents;
FpXQ_powBig silently returned one for them; the existing inverse accepted only its port's
four-argument Hensel signature. Extension powers bypassed Sage's exponent comparison and
Integer-coercion ordering. Ninety additional C-kernel cases verify that the four-argument
lifting behavior remains identical to PARI ZpXQ_inv after adding the original three arguments.

Both quotient-power entry points now share inversion-first signed power and bb_group.c's
binary/sliding-window algorithm, including the unsigned-word and large-exponent thresholds.
The extension's add/sub/neg/mul/div/inv/pow paths now use parigp-ts FpX/FpXQ routines; the
local polynomial xgcd and field exponentiation loops were removed. Exponents are compared
with zero before Integer conversion, preserving fractional/nonfinite error precedence and
Python ordering errors for string/list/null values. The shared oracle's negative-infinity
fixture was corrected to retain the sign; the resulting Sage errors are compared exactly.

Direct kernel tests install and invoke PARI's exported C routines with actual polynomial
GEN values. They include zero, reducible/nonmonic moduli, nonunits, zero exponents and
binary/sliding-window boundaries through 1,024 bits. PARI e_INV and the existing port's
PariInvError are explicitly normalized to one low-level error kind; Sage-facing exception
messages are compared independently. Dense bigint coefficient representation and native
packed/multiplication optimization gaps remain documented, so this is not claimed as
complete algorithm/performance parity with every PARI backend.

All 51,363 comparative cases pass (28,704 in finite_fields). Fast units: 5,914 pass with 32
existing skips before the final API example. All 946 affected Sage slow tests pass, as do
62 direct Galois tests, 219 focused source tests and 35 API-example tests. A final replay
of all 3,940 new comparisons also passes against generated Sage/PARI transcripts. Existing
type-check diagnostics remain at 763. Instrumented line coverage: 81,254/106,262 (76.47%),
using fresh reports for the three edited production files. The broader audit continues.


## Polynomial constructors and coefficient conversions — 2.4.0

The initial 954-case constructor/index sweep reproduced 821 mismatches against Sage.
Polynomial arrays bypassed coefficient conversion, leaving raw integers or foreign field
objects in the result. Generator indices were ignored. Follow-up cases exposed QQ FLINT's
recursive singleton-list constructor and GF2X's recursive polynomial-list accumulation;
12 nested-list mismatches and 12 identity/base-parent mismatches were recorded separately.
The repairs preserve exact-parent identity and embed elements of the exact polynomial base
parent as constants before attempting coefficient conversion.

Coefficient conversion also exposed missing `_rational_` hooks on both prime-element classes
and IntegerMod, missing compatible modular/legacy-prime inputs in extension constructors,
and incorrect nonconstant extension-to-prime errors. The shared case list includes direct
conversion/hook controls, composite moduli, large residues, nested lists, nonfinite numbers,
QQ and six finite/quotient base-parent representations. A full run caught 15 Hilbert-class-
polynomial regressions: the old caller supplied primitive-returning ZZ where a coefficient
ring of Integer objects was required. Its zero/one/coercion factories now consistently wrap.

Added 1,626 comparisons; all 52,989 full-suite cases pass, including 1,671 polynomial cases.
All 1,626 new cases also pass a saved-Sage transcript replay. Fast tests: 5,920 pass with
32 existing skips before the final API example. All 946 affected slow tests pass with a
15-second default per-test timeout (some tests define longer individual timeouts). Focused
source/replay: 1,920 pass; final API/constructor tests: 41 pass. Existing type-check failures
remain, decreasing from 763 to 609 diagnostics after aligning polynomial input interfaces;
one existing incompatibility message now includes the new rational hook.

Fresh execution data replaces stale reports for all six edited production files. Measured
coverage is 80,543/106,155 lines (75.87%); the decrease reflects discarded old line mappings,
not a claim of complete branch coverage. Finite-field member dispatch is 103/103 and modular
member dispatch is 35/35. Polynomial arithmetic/coercion maps, negative powers, dependency
algorithm dispatch and the remaining production files still require review. Two existing
ZZ-polynomial comparative adapters were found to execute test-local algorithms rather than
the production implementation; replacing those adapters is the next audit step.


## Integer-polynomial comparison adapters and zero GCD — 2.4.1

The old `poly_gcd_zz` and `poly_eval_zz` adapters implemented their own algorithms instead
of calling production Polynomial methods. The Python GCD side also removed the content,
which hid part of Sage's observable result. With 216 new zero/sign/content/trailing-zero/
large-value cases and the normalization removed, the old adapter failed 118 comparisons.
Replacing both algorithms with public-method calls exposed 12 production mismatches:
`gcd(0, f)` and `gcd(f, 0)` normalized negative leading coefficients.

Sage's polynomial_integer_dense_flint.pyx:822-825 returns the actual operand before calling
FLINT on these branches; the port now preserves both its sign and identity, including the
one shortcuts. An older unit test labeled the opposite result as a Sage observation; that
assertion was corrected to the reproduced upstream behavior. The nonzero/nonunit algorithm
is unchanged. It still uses a local primitive remainder sequence rather than FLINT's
subresultant/heuristic/modular dispatch, which remains a separate open architecture and
performance discrepancy for the next repair.

All 53,205 full-suite comparisons pass, including 246 polynomial_ops cases; all 216 new
cases also pass a saved-Sage replay.
Fast tests: 5,921 pass with 32 existing skips; focused polynomial tests: 46 pass. Type-check
diagnostics are unchanged at 609. Current measured execution coverage is 80,781/106,358
lines (75.95%); old polynomial_element.ts line mappings were discarded.

## FLINT integer-polynomial GCD delegation — 2.5.0

The preceding GCD repair left an algorithm/dependency discrepancy: Sage selected FLINT's
subresultant, packed heuristic and modular algorithms, while TypeScript always ran a
Sage-local primitive pseudo-remainder loop. Integer GCD and squarefree factorization now
delegate to the new dense-array FLINT kernels. The former local GCD/pseudo-division code
has been removed.

The implementation follows `fmpz_poly/gcd*.c`, `pseudo_rem_cohen.c`, `divides.c`,
`divrem_divconquer*.c`, `nmod_poly/gcd.c` and `gr_poly/hgcd.c`. It preserves content,
positive backend normalization, valuation removal, heuristic success/failure, prime order,
unlucky-degree rejection, symmetric CRT reconstruction and divisibility checks. Large modular
GCDs use recursive half-GCD; polynomial products use BigInt Kronecker substitution, modular
division uses Newton inversion and integer exact division uses divide-and-conquer.
Native packed dispatch and word-instruction differences remain explicit in DEVIATIONS.md.

Tests now invoke FLINT's public C GCD entry points through Sage's loaded shared library,
including the heuristic's failure status. Modular raw remainders are made monic on both
sides; this deliberately bypasses Sage's zero-operand shortcut for the backend comparison.
Separate Sage-facing cases still preserve that shortcut. Added **896** comparisons cover
zero/sign/content cases, degree changes, skipped and discarded primes, degrees through
3,500, coefficients through 4,300 bits and prime generation beyond FLINT's initial table.
The initial 484 backend cases failed against the stubs; all final cases pass.

Validation:
- Full comparative run: **54,060/54,060**, followed by the final expanded polynomial area:
  **1,142/1,142**. Together these cover the current **54,101** configured cases.
- Saved Sage/FLINT transcript replay: **896/896** new comparisons.
- Fast unit suite: **5,926 passed**, 32 existing skips; affected slow suite: **946 passed**.
- Final docs/deviation-reference/backend unit checks: **43 passed**.
- Type-check diagnostics: **609**, unchanged after normalizing line shifts and an existing
  union-type display-order difference. The new FLINT files add no diagnostics.
- Current execution coverage: **81,857/106,906 lines (76.57%)**, across 177 production files;
  6,953 callables, 556 explicit stubs and 236 partial stubs. This does not establish full
  behavioral or branch coverage. The broader audit continues.

Source comparison of `polynomial_element.pyx:6323-6374` and

is already monic. The monicity predicate compares with the coefficient-ring one. Word-modular
normalization checks for a unit and delegates to FLINT's new dense-array make-monic kernel,
including its distinct allocation behavior.

errors now match Sage's IntegerMod backend, and powers delegate to the already-tested

the native/GMP cutoff. The old unit assertion accepting a zero GF(2) monic result was corrected.

updated. Arbitrary categorical base extension and the newer bundled FLINT zero-error runtime

## Polynomial indexing, shifts, truncation and reversal — 2.6.1

Compared production methods across QQ, wrapped ZZ, both prime-element classes, the
specialized GF2 class, composite/zero modular rings, PARI extension fields (including
binary extensions), large NTL primes and nested QQ polynomial coefficients. Added
9,608 shared cases with scalar integers/wrappers, rationals, booleans, strings, lists,
null, nonfinite floats, modular/prime elements and constant/nonconstant extensions.
Checks include coefficients, parent strings, identity and exact exception classes/messages.

The first safe sweep exposed 3,189 mismatches; finite scalar, long coefficient-list,
extension constant and final unsigned-increment sweeps exposed another 404, 16, 55
and 22 mismatches respectively. Repairs preserve exact indexing versus Cython's
fraction-truncating conversions, native signed widths, QQ degree-plus-one conversion,
zero shortcuts, generic negative truncation slices, native unsigned increment wrap,
and scalar-parent arithmetic during generic reversal. The extension backend allocates
for zero shifts; large-prime NTL checks the original zero argument before conversion.

Sources reviewed: `polynomial_element.pyx` scalar `__getitem__`, generic `shift`,
`truncate`, `reverse` and generic-dense overrides; `polynomial_template.pxi` shift and
truncate; `polynomial_rational_flint.pyx`; `polynomial_integer_dense_flint.pyx`;
`polynomial_zmod_flint.pyx`; `polynomial_modn_dense_ntl.pyx`; `polynomial_zz_pex.pyx`;
`arith/long.pxd`; PARI extension element comparison/integer protocols; FLINT polynomial
reverse kernels. Source storage and allocation limits remain explicit limitations.

The Sage 10.3 oracle required narrowly scoped empty-list repetition for failed generic
sequence operations: repeated compiled failures otherwise decrement cached coefficient
references and eventually segfault. These checks retain the original scalar dispatch;
isolated direct Sage calls verified their errors. Only the old "non-negative" reversal
message spelling is normalized to the bundled source's "nonnegative". Valid shifts that
construct enormous monomials are excluded from execution; the audit does not claim
resource-exhaustion equivalence. See DEVIATIONS.md for the exact compatibility boundary.

## Native integer/modular division and coefficient exactness — 2.7.0 (2026-09-10)

The bundled `polynomial_integer_dense_flint.pyx:quo_rem` returns partial integer
quotients and can leave high-degree terms in the remainder. The port incorrectly used
generic exact coefficient division. Added 5,418 quotient/remainder comparisons across
implemented coefficient parents, both operand orders and incompatible variables, zero
inputs, positive/negative constant divisors, nested exact/inexact polynomial coefficients,
and packed GF2 degrees up to 1,000. The initial sweep exposed 4,790 mismatches; a nested
coefficient extension exposed another 24. All final 24,824 polynomial comparisons pass.

Implemented the FLINT dense integer division backend with its length-6 dispatch,
recursive cutoff 16, unbalanced blocks, small-magnitude leading skip and signed floor
quotients. Preserve uncancelled high remainder coefficients across the final unbalanced
block. The previous exact-only GCD division implementation now calls this shared kernel.
Added 1,360 direct C-library comparisons, including raw `exact=1` calls, signed constant
quotients, cutoff boundaries, long/unbalanced shapes and 1,100-bit packed coefficients.
A temporary refactor regression (a missing private multiply helper in heuristic GCD)
was detected by its existing comparative tests and repaired before validation.

Exposed the shared modular classical/Newton division kernel through a validating dense
array adapter; added 224 comparisons covering composite unit-leading divisors and word
sizes/cutoffs. Binary Sage polynomial division delegates to `ntl-ts/GF2X.DivRem`.
Sage-facing coercion errors, zero identities, nonunit checks and NTL errors were repaired.
Nested coefficient division accepts exact polynomial quotients and raises the original
generic ArithmeticError for fractional polynomial coefficients. Corrected the old unit
assertion requiring native ZZ division to reject every nonintegral leading quotient.

Sources: `fmpz_poly/divrem.c`, `divrem_basecase.c`, `divrem_divconquer.c`,
`divrem_divconquer_recursive.c`; the existing reviewed modular Newton division kernel;

extension fast division and broader scalar protocols remain open, as recorded in DEVIATIONS.md.

JSON-encodes coefficient pairs to bypass the generic formatter's factorization heuristic;

Added 9,790 permanent shared comparisons for scalar equality across 12 coefficient
bases, both directions of coefficient-polynomial embedding, arithmetic/division parents
and zero identity. The initial scalar sweep found 1,037 incorrect false results. The
expanded float/embedding sweep exposed 1,311 failures before repair; a later 12-case
zero-action identity failure prompted 144 additional controls.

Equality now selects the supported scalar's canonical parent. ZZ/QQ comparisons with
nonintegral JavaScript numbers convert coefficients to binary64 before checking degree,

## Polynomial GCD canonical parents and native backends — 2.8.0 (2026-09-10)

Added 5,654 mixed-parent GCD comparisons across 16 base selections, both variable modes,
zero/equal/nonmonic inputs, nonunit leading coefficients, quotient reductions and rational
coefficient promotion. The initial sweep exposed 5,393 discrepancies. Sixty subsequent
identity failures were repaired by preserving original operands across mutually coercible
TypeScript parent instances and allocating after nontrivial extension GCD calls.

QQ now delegates to the new FLINT `_fmpq_poly_gcd` numerator-array kernel. It removes
content, reuses the existing integer backend and returns the monic rational normalization.
Added 301 dependency comparisons covering zero, signs, trailing zeros, common factors,
length boundaries and 350-bit coefficients. Word-modular GCD delegates to the existing
FLINT Euclidean/half-GCD port; the Euclidean kernel now stops at a nonzero constant without
inversion, as the source requires. Binary GCD delegates to NTL GF2X. Template zero/equal
shortcuts and native errors are preserved; large-prime/extension fast backends remain open.

`polynomial_template.pxi:gcd`, `nmod_poly_linkage.pxi:celement_gcd`, and Sage's rational

## Finite extended GCD and tuple identity — 2.9.0 (2026-09-10)

Added 6,556 shared Sage comparisons covering extended GCD across supported parent pairs,
incompatible variables, nested coefficient embeddings, nonconstant extension GCD/XGCD,
zero/nonmonic inputs and composite inversion failures. The initial sweep found 6,180
mismatches. Canonical parent normalization, finite-template zero shortcuts, native NTL
both-zero convention and mixed overloads now agree with the reference.

Implemented FLINT modular extended GCD with the source bit-size cutoff table, classical
extended Euclid, recursive half-GCD and final cofactor division. The shared HGCD matrix
is the inverse of FLINT's matrix, as already documented for GCD. Reuse packed fast products
and Newton division. Binary Sage XGCD delegates to NTL GF2X. Added 1,328 direct Cython/native
FLINT comparisons, including exact divisors, constant remainders, cutoff boundaries,
unbalanced inputs, long common factors and composite errors. These bypass Sage's template
shortcuts; original native code computes every expected triple.

Strengthened all new Sage XGCD results with the full three-output alias matrix. This
exposed three QQ zero-input failures: the port returned one shared zero object rather
than three separate native results. Repaired that allocation and retained exact identity
assertions. Installed Sage composite XGCD uses the same narrow signal guard as GCD,
translating its old native abort as the bundled nmod linkage ValueError.

`gr_poly/hgcd.c` matrix convention; `polynomial_template.pxi:xgcd`,

Integer resultant/modular reconstruction, QQ native XGCD and remaining NTL fast backends
are still open; no claim of complete polynomial or repository audit is made.

## Native integer and modular resultants — 2.10.0 (2026-09-10)

Ported the FLINT resultant dependencies required by integer extended GCD. Integer
resultants retain content/sign, the exact length/bit-cost dispatch, subresultant
pseudo-remainders, norm bound, skipped word primes and balanced CRT reconstruction.
The norm-bound bit length is computed exactly with a squared power-of-two comparison.
Modular resultants use inverse-free small formulas and Euclidean/recursive half-GCD
arithmetic. The existing HGCD implementation optionally accumulates resultant factors,
including deferred degree drops and parity offsets across truncated recursive inputs.

Added 1,597 comparisons against actual native FLINT C calls: zeros, signed content,
constant and pointer-alias cases, composite inverse errors, 130-bit coefficients,
exact dispatch boundaries, leading coefficients divisible by reconstruction primes,
common factors and large-degree HGCD. A Cython array-conditional compile issue in the
new oracle was repaired and preflighted before the completed comparison run; this was
oracle scaffolding, not a production discrepancy. All 5,952 final backend cases pass.

The modular native same-pointer shortcut returns zero even for nonzero constants;
distinct constant arrays return one. This is preserved explicitly and tested. Source:
`fmpz_poly/resultant.c`, `resultant_euclidean.c`, `resultant_modular.c`,
`pseudo_rem_cohen.c`; `nmod_poly/resultant.c`; `gr_poly/resultant_small.c`,
`resultant_hgcd.c`, and resultant-state branches of `gr_poly/hgcd.c`.
Sage-facing integration and integer extended GCD continue separately.

Validation: 1,597 new native transcript replays pass (40.33s); the full saved-Sage/native
suite passes 104,064 comparisons. Fast: 5,967 pass/32 existing skips; affected slow:
946 pass (170.68s). Type diagnostics remain 609. Current-source coverage:
82,884/108,031 instrumented lines (76.72%).

The next integer-XGCD probe confirms that FLINT orders inputs by length before computing
the resultant and does not reverse its sign afterward: over ZZ, `(x+2).resultant(x^3+3)`
is -5 but `(x+2).xgcd(x^3+3)` starts with 5. The prose guarantee in Sage's docstring is
therefore too strong; the implementation and its coefficients are the regression oracle.


## Integer and rational extended GCD — 2.11.0 (2026-09-10)

Implemented the previously missing ZZ polynomial XGCD and replaced generic QQ XGCD with
native FLINT delegation. The integer dependency uses the length-ordered resultant, skips
bad word primes, reconstructs centered Bezout coefficients, verifies stabilized residues
and stops at the source coefficient bound. Rational XGCD removes content/common factors
and restores all numerator/denominator factors. The Sage wrapper retains Integer returns
for constant and zero cofactors, original polynomial zero shortcuts, and the QQ fallback
with a common denominator cleared when the integer resultant vanishes.

Before integration, 373 newly added Sage cases failed: 361 compatible ZZ pairs and 12
self-input cases. All now pass. The scalar-return overload exposes the newly implemented
branch while preserving existing field overloads. Standalone native constant/constant
inputs are guarded because original FLINT explicitly leaves them undefined; the Sage
wrapper uses Integer.xgcd and never enters that unsupported native case.

Added 863 native FLINT/QQ-kernel and 928 Sage comparisons, including signed content,
large coefficients, reconstruction-prime skips, shared factors, mixed-parent errors,
self-input identities and the complete output-alias matrix. Native Cython compilation
was preflighted before the area run. Every expected triple comes from original native
FLINT or Sage; no test-local extended-GCD algorithm replaces the reference.

Sources: `fmpz_poly/xgcd_modular.c`, `fmpz_vec/set_nmod_vec.c`, `fmpq_poly/xgcd.c`,
Sage `polynomial_integer_dense_flint.pyx:xgcd` and `polynomial_rational_flint.pyx:xgcd`.
The existing native resultant and modular-XGCD dependencies are reused. Fast NTL
large-prime/extension delegation and Sage-facing resultant integration remain open.

## Sylvester matrices and explicit scalar construction — 2.12.0 (2026-09-10)

Audited Sylvester matrix layout and all existing coefficient-parent combinations,
including scalar operands and optional variable arguments. The first 12,049 cases exposed
10,043 mismatches; all 4,992 subsequent scalar-operand cases failed in the original port.
Repaired canonical parent selection before degrees, exact zero-input errors, conversion
of the optional variable and its replacement on mixed-parent calls. Sage reads the left
variable tuple after coercion, so a constant left polynomial can raise IndexError first.
Those observable error paths are retained rather than simplified.

The remaining cases exposed explicit constructor discrepancies: finite-ring elements can
expand through their prime-field polynomial representative, while QQ singleton lists stay
inside the element constructor and can reject the same input. Repaired specialized GF2
wrapper coefficients, foreign finite zero conversion, and the finite-versus-integer
conversion distinction for extension coefficients. Added 1,248 direct scalar constructor
comparisons so ignored variable results cannot hide bad coefficients.

Original matrix selection also matters. Small PARI-FFELT fields lack the Givaro `_cache`
required by the default Meataxe/M4RIE implementations. Their AttributeError is preserved;
characteristic-two 0-by-0 matrices still succeed. Degree-one extension wrappers must use
the prime-field path; added 64 direct comparisons for GF(2), GF(3), GF(7) and GF(257).
The API still returns arrays of coefficients rather than full Matrix objects, as recorded
in DEVIATIONS.md. Resultant/discriminant retain the extracted raw entry builder until their
separate native algorithm integration; public Sylvester validation is not used as that
internal fallback.

Sources: `polynomial_element.pyx:sylvester_matrix`, `variables`; `polynomial_ring.py:280-440`;
`polynomial_rational_flint.pyx:__init__`; `element_pari_ffelt.pyx:polynomial`;
`integer_mod.pyx:polynomial`; `matrix_space.py:get_matrix_class`, `matrix_gfpn_dense.pyx`
and `matrix_gf2e_dense.pyx` construction and element conversion. No oracle adapter replaces
these paths: expected entries and errors come directly from original Sage.

## General real resultants — 2.14.0 (2026-09-10)

Ported PARI's single-word real addition and division and its real determinant path.
Addition truncates aligned mantissas before adding and preserves 64-bit storage after
cancellation. Division follows the native one-word quotient/remainder branch. The
Sylvester matrix distinguishes exact padding zeros from finite-accuracy real zeros.
Elimination preserves the native 2-by-2 formula, first maximal-exponent pivot, reference
column approximate-zero check, column update order, early return and accumulated sign.

All initial 1,119 new Sage RDF resultant cases passed. Direct PARI comparisons exposed
260 further mismatches because native `Polrev` retains inexact leading zeros while Sage
normalizes them before conversion. Corrected that dependency boundary without changing
Sage normalization. Native all-zero arrays still have zero polynomial sign. Cypari2's
zero accuracy exponent is -53, distinct from raw PARI `dbltor`'s -1023.

Added 7,062 comparisons: 4,764 primitive/composed native arithmetic cases, 1,149 native
resultants and 1,149 Sage resultants. Arithmetic comparisons use exact rational values
and stored zero exponents; resultant comparisons use binary64 bits. Original values are
computed by libpari/Sage, with no test-local elimination algorithm. New native add/div
helpers are internal one-word APIs; the older approximate QFB/Buchmann APIs remain
separate. SciPy overflow and nonconstant nested RDF coefficients remain explicit gaps.

## Derivative protocol and coefficient arithmetic — 2.15.0 (2026-09-10)

The first derivative sweep found 17,431 discrepancies: the old method ignored every
argument, lacked aliases/gradient, allocated a new generic zero and used repeated addition
for scalar coefficient multiplication. Implemented the source variable/count parser,
`multi_derivative`, `_derivative`, aliases and gradient. Variables can recurse through
nested polynomial coefficients; zero counts preserve the original object. QQ derivatives
delegate to FLINT's rational kernel, with its distinct fresh-zero allocation.

Generic derivatives now multiply the degree by the coefficient once in the base ring.
For example, the degree-11 RDF coefficient 6392024.733746229 differentiates to
70312272.07120852; repeated addition produced 70312272.0712085. Added isolated RDF and
nested RDF controls alongside random bit-pattern coefficients. Native integer/rational
derivative kernels normalize dense arrays without mutation and cancel denominators.

After repair, 810 comparisons differed only in the older installed Sage's hyphenated
negative-count error. The bundled source uses `derivative counts must be nonnegative`;
a narrowly scoped oracle adapter translates that one historical message. Other errors,
including C-int conversion before negativity checking, are compared without normalization.

Added 21,025 comparative cases: 19,793 polynomial/parser/utility cases and 1,232 native
FLINT kernel cases. Direct utility comparisons preserve the singleton-list parser identity.
The package now exports `sagemath-ts/misc/derivative`; LLM examples exercise the new import
and public polynomial protocol. General Python iterable and symbolic protocols remain
outside the explicitly documented subset.

Sources: Sage `misc/derivative.pyx`, generic `polynomial_element.pyx:4187-4386`, QQ
`polynomial_rational_flint.pyx:1508-1555`; FLINT `fmpz_poly/derivative.c` and
`fmpq_poly/derivative.c`. Native comparisons invoke QQ._derivative and its actual FLINT
call chain, including denominator-one inputs for the integer kernel.

## Pseudo-division, scalar protocols and fraction parents — 2.16.0 (2026-09-10)

The first 9,778-case sweep found 6,464 discrepancies. The port required polynomial
operands, replaced coefficient-base membership with degree zero, multiplied coefficients
in the original parent without scalar coercion, discarded negative final exponents and
always returned polynomial quotients. Restored the source's ordered zero/membership
checks, scalar multiplication actions, original-operand powers and cancellation loop.

For QQ polynomials, pseudo-dividing 1 by 2*x^3+2 returns (0, 1/4); over Z/14Z the same
degree gap raises `inverse of Mod(4, 14) does not exist`. A zero dividend divided by a
nonzero constant polynomial can return a fraction-field zero quotient, while an Integer
scalar follows its own power/coercion path. Native/generic zero identities remain distinct.

Added mirrored fraction-field parent/element modules for constant polynomial denominators,
including normalized numerator/denominator data, arithmetic, negation, equality and parent
access. Eight comparative controls caught sign changes from reducing an intermediate
integer-fraction inverse; division now preserves the source's unreduced inverse and
cross-cancels through multiplication. Nonconstant denominators, complete fraction coercion,
FpT/native multiplication dispatch and the public polynomial power protocol remain open.

An additional scalar-equality control found that wrapping a zero-ring scalar as a polynomial
introduced a quotient-parent coercion absent from Sage's scalar comparison. Repaired that
case while retaining the separate polynomial-to-polynomial behavior.

Added 15,994 comparative regressions in total: 9,778 original pseudo-division cases,
6,048 direct fraction cases and 168 zero-ring equality/zero-protocol controls. Tests compare
values, errors, parents, numerator/denominator data and operand/result identities.

## General polynomial fractions and native modular products — 2.17.0 (2026-09-10)

The first 10,800-case fraction sweep found 10,500 mismatches, mostly unsupported
nonconstant denominators, missing inverse/power/reduction methods, wrong subclass selection
and missing cached-object behavior. Implemented the generic and univariate-field source
protocols and the separate native FpT class. Generic arithmetic preserves its cross-factor
cancellation and unreduced intermediate signs; FpT preserves stored-pair equality, raw
inversion and fresh polynomial accessors. An unreduced native `0/x` intentionally compares
unequal to zero, while checked inversion still rejects its zero numerator.

Ported native FLINT modular addition, subtraction, power and portable multiplication
dispatch, including classical squaring and KS/KS2/KS4 packing/recovery. FpT delegates its
arithmetic to these kernels and the existing native GCD/division ports. Direct native
comparisons exercise carry-heavy coefficients, aliases, zero/constant inputs and full-word
moduli/exponents. General polynomial multiplication and power still need their own audit.

The wider suite caught an unbound characteristic-method call in mixed polynomial
coercion and the missing Python ArithmeticError/ZeroDivisionError relationship in fraction
construction. Both are repaired with comparative controls. Constructor checks cover native
characteristic overflow, the bundled polynomial-class restriction and generator indices.
The existing free-module vector-space-span path now obtains the mirrored fraction parent;
its representation matches Sage instead of adding parentheses around a monomial denominator.
Added live free-module integration comparisons and corrected that stale unit expectation.

This batch adds 16,017 cases: 10,800 arithmetic/reduction/subclass comparisons, 165 parent
constructor cases, 160 polynomial free-module integrations and 4,892 direct native-kernel
cases. Python adapters are limited to the documented crashing zero-negative-power input,
runtime category suffixes and installed-versus-bundled constructor checks/messages.

## Fraction sections, construction and coefficient towers — 2.18.0 (2026-09-10)

The initial 15,312 conversion cases found 9,088 mismatches: 7,252 polynomial conversions
and 1,836 fraction constructor conversions. Restored the distinction between canonical
embeddings and partial sections, generic unit-denominator checks, exact numerator identity,
native FpT normalization side effects and cross-characteristic fallback errors. Fraction
construction now follows the source's ordered resolver, including clearing QQ polynomial
coefficient denominators when converting into a fraction field over ZZ. Non-domain
polynomial method calls preserve their missing-category-method error; factory errors remain
separate. The new `has_coerce_map_from` query has 242 original parent-pair controls.

Three isolated import comparisons exposed a fraction-field module initialization cycle
masked by aggregate imports. Structural predicates and type-only imports remove the cycle
without changing the public subpaths. These comparisons spawn a new process for each import.

The 1,380 nested-coefficient cases caught truncated inverse coefficients, missing constant
polynomial sections back to coefficient rings and polynomial/fraction equality in the wrong
parent. These fixes also restore zero pseudo-division result identities. Nested comparisons
construct the original noninterned Sage polynomial classes with the supplied base parent;
the reason, cached-factory difference and remaining limitation are explicit in DEVIATIONS.md.

All bugs have permanent shared comparative cases that check values, result parents, errors,
source representation after conversion and numerator/denominator identity. This batch adds
16,937 cases: 15,312 conversion protocols, three isolated imports, 242 parent queries and
1,380 nested coefficient operations. Direct scalar constructor fraction hooks, general
fraction coercion maps and full polynomial power/native multiplication remain open.

## Fraction and polynomial scalar conversion — 2.19.0 (2026-09-10)

The initial 3,828 fraction-to-scalar cases found 3,132 discrepancies. Generic fractions
lacked `_conversion`, `_integer_` and `_rational_`; native FpT needed its own section map,
including normalization before integrality/constancy errors. QQ's structural fraction
convenience incorrectly accepted numerator/denominator methods as numeric properties.
Scalar constructors now preserve section/hook precedence, native mutation and the source's
Integer-to-QQ fallback ordering for exact modular inputs.

The new paths exposed zero-identity loss during reduction: native ZZ/QQ floor division
allocates a zero numerator, while the port's `quo_rem` fast path retains its operand.
The fraction reduction adapter now preserves the original floor-division allocation.
GF2's separate element class was also missing integer/rational hooks and object coercion.
Those paths now share the audited prime-field constructor and residue-lift protocols.

An additional 1,596 direct polynomial/hook cases exposed missing modular rational fallback,
a nonconstant polynomial evaluated in a finite extension when an earlier constant section
should reject it, and missing GF2 coefficient embeddings. A further 1,914 direct generic
fraction hook cases found the distinction between generic ring unit-inverse ArithmeticError
and direct modular inverse ZeroDivisionError. All have permanent original comparisons.

Source review also found that the installed Sage 10.3 constant-section error lacks the
operand prefix present in the bundled source. The oracle adapts only that exact old message,
using the offending original polynomial; earlier nested section controls are corrected too.
Scalar comparison frames use the requested parent for primitive/wrapper scalar results,
as their TypeScript representations lack Sage's universal parent member.

## Polynomial integer powers and native kernels — 3.0.0 (2026-09-10)

The first 3,234 integer-power comparisons found 1,008 discrepancies: all negative
polynomial powers were rejected, backend-specific zero and overflow rules were missing,
and generic binary multiplication replaced Sage's FLINT/NTL dispatch. The port now uses
FLINT's small/binomial/multinomial/binary ZZ powers and common-denominator QQ powers;
word-modular powers use FLINT, binary powers use NTL GF2X and prime/extension powers use
NTL ZZ_pX/ZZ_pEX. Source review confirmed that Sage's PARI finite-extension coefficients
still use the NTL polynomial class. Generic scalar/generator/Frobenius branches and original
power-one identity are retained. The public return type now includes fractions for negative
and dynamic exponents; checked nonnegative literals retain polynomial chaining.

Direct native comparisons exposed two additional defects. Rational power must preserve its
raw numerator/common-denominator pair, even when noncanonical. Shared packed integer products
could exceed JavaScript's BigInt temporary limit while every output coefficient fit. The
FLINT and NTL product boundaries now split those products with their native Karatsuba schemes;
NTL also retains its unbalanced split. Nine of 16 dedicated large NTL regressions failed
before the fix and all pass afterward. Native coefficient comparisons hash every exact signed
hexadecimal coefficient to avoid storing huge decimal transcripts. The oracle avoids NTL's
C-int-truncating Python conversion and catches the original GF2X LONG_MIN abort with a
Cython signal guard. These adapters and remaining FFT/SS/remainder performance differences
are explicit in DEVIATIONS.md.

A further 90 original QQ comparisons and the existing strict coefficient-ring units guard
against returning coefficients from the wrong TypeScript class or requiring a broader
constructor than the coefficient-ring contract. Reconstruction uses integer construction
and field division in the supplied ring. Colocated type tests prevent dynamic or mixed-sign
literal exponents from silently narrowing to polynomial-only results.

This batch adds 7,856 permanent original comparisons: 3,234 integer-power protocols, 90
strict rational coefficient controls, 3,518 native FLINT powers, 998 NTL power/product controls
and 16 large NTL regressions. Every observed bug has a shared original comparison. Fractional
exponents, modular powers, root-series dependencies and wider polynomial multiplication
remain open; this batch does not establish complete polynomial or repository coverage.
