## Polynomial roots and truncated series — 3.1.0 (2026-09-10)

The exponent-type matrix found 1,655 discrepancies before fixes: exact rational powers
were rejected and native versus generic conversions were conflated. Root dependencies
were missing, so this batch implements exact polynomial roots, characteristic/valuation
extraction, Newton root series, truncated multiplication/powers and reciprocal series.
The integer/rational/word kernels live in FLINT; extension inversion lives in NTL with
its high-half Newton algorithm and recursive ZZ_pX half-GCD coefficient inversion.
Permanent native tests exercise the 24/25/64 cutoffs, unbalanced degrees, arbitrary-size
coefficients, noncanonical rational inputs and exponents beyond native words.

The comparisons also caught distinct single-root choices in prime finite fields versus
integer-modulus rings. The positive coefficient-root bridge now follows Johnston, the
source's category-specific root-of-unity choice, prime-power p-adic log/exp and CRT.
Large roots exposed a separate factor-order bug: printed strings were sorted instead of
numeric polynomial coefficients and multiplicities. This is fixed in finite, integer and
rational factor lists. Zero/composite factorization errors now match the original.
A large-prime p-adic parent also stalled in a trial-division primality check; parent
validation now delegates to the existing prime predicate. Every discovered discrepancy
has a permanent shared case; standalone original runs confirmed each failing input.

The final typed series matrix found 254 additional differences in validation order,
identity shortcuts and conversion errors. The source deliberately compares an untyped
root exponent before coercion, so zero/one polynomial identities accept some inputs that
nonconstant polynomials reject. Extension inverse precision handling also differs from
the generated native wrappers. These branches and the original guarded PARI conversion
SystemError are covered explicitly. Narrow installed-version message adapters and remaining
portable multiplication/category-state limitations are documented in DEVIATIONS.md.

New shared comparisons: 3,520 polynomial exponent protocols; 17,526 root/series operations;
2,460 finite coefficient roots; 5,878 native FLINT series; 4,840 large/generic truncated
powers; 75 large prime-power roots; 28 p-adic parent validations; 77 factor-order controls;
4,400 typed series protocols; 1,122 native NTL half-GCD/extension inverse controls. Total:
39,926. Mixed-parent truncated products, inexact coefficients, modular powers and the wider
repository audit remain open. This batch does not claim complete polynomial coverage.

finite_fields.py:zeta; structure/factorization.py:sort; padics/factory.py and FM_template.pxi;

## Bounded native division and modular products — 3.1.1 (2026-09-10)

A duplicated packed multiplication kernel in integer divide-and-conquer division exceeded
Bun's BigInt temporary-size limit, despite valid quotient and remainder coefficients.
All six native division regressions failed before the repair. Division now calls the
shared bounded FLINT product kernel through a lazy function wrapper, preserving standalone
module initialization across the existing GCD/division dependency cycle.

The same limit affected modular KS/KS2/KS4 products and the public multiplication dispatcher.
Twenty-two of 32 new product/square comparisons failed before the repair. Each packed
boundary now checks its output-bit budget and uses the existing bounded integer product,
followed by modular reduction, when necessary. All 38 new native comparisons pass.
Coefficients above 2^32768 and modular product degrees through 65,534 are included.
Native FFT/SS performance remains an explicit portable-backend limitation.

Sources: FLINT fmpz_poly/divrem.c and divrem_divconquer_recursive.c; nmod_poly/mul.c,
mul_KS.c, mul_KS2.c and mul_KS4.c; the previously transcribed shared FLINT Karatsuba kernel.

## Truncated multiplication and inexact products — 3.1.2 (2026-09-10)

The first mixed-parent/scalar matrix found 7,082 discrepancies in 10,980 cases. The public
method used explicit coefficient construction instead of canonical coercion, admitting
strings/lists and incompatible modular scalars, retaining QQ for real inputs and reporting
precision errors before parent failures. It now selects the source's canonical parent,
including aligned nested QQ/RDF coefficient promotion and the scalar zero-ring restriction.
Native/internal typed errors and nullable operands match the compared original wrappers.
Integer adapter parents remain intact when canonical coercion encounters another ZZ adapter.

Bit-level real-polynomial checks found 318 discrepancies in 1,800 truncated products and
160 in 600 full/aliased products. Initializing sums with zero lost signed zeros; the generic
term and square paths were also missing. The shared schoolbook product now starts with the
first actual product, while inexact full multiplication follows term shortcuts and the
original aliased-square order. Scientific notation and infinity coefficients no longer gain
spurious parentheses. NaN payloads are normalized in both oracles; all other coefficient
bits are compared. Another 632 comparisons protect shared parent-promotion callers and
precision-conversion ordering. All 14,012 new original comparisons pass in their focused runs.

## Full exact polynomial multiplication — 3.2.0 (2026-09-10)

Public exact products used a quadratic coefficient loop for every backend, bypassing
SageMath's native multiplication and generic Karatsuba. An 8,192-coefficient GF(2)
aliased square did not finish within a two-second pre-fix bound; the repaired native
GF2X square completed in approximately 3.5 ms in the focused probe. Permanent comparisons
retain this large input alongside all exact coefficient backends and unequal degrees.

Integer/rational/word products now delegate to FLINT, binary/large-modulus/extension
products to NTL, and generic exact products use the source's balanced/unbalanced
Karatsuba recursion and coefficient-ring thresholds. Noncommutative matrix comparisons
protect operand ordering and both term-action directions. Native rational full products
preserve cross-content cancellation and raw aliased-square denominators; they intentionally
use a different adapter from canonicalized truncated products. The documented portable
BigInt/FFT trade-offs remain explicit rather than claiming native platform performance.

## Polynomial modular powers, RDF powers and bounded modular GCD — 3.3.0 (2026-09-10)

The polynomial power method previously ignored its modulus. The port now preserves the
original backend validation, canonical parent selection, native/generic exponent dispatch,
zero/unit shortcuts, signed-word boundaries and fraction-field reciprocal behavior.
ZZ/QQ reject the modulus as bundled Sage does. FLINT modular powers use binary/preinverse
algorithms and the generator-specific window path; extension powers use NTL sliding windows
and reciprocal reduction with an extension half-GCD/XGCD adapter.

Real coefficients lacked the native integer-power/inverse operations needed by polynomial
powers. The new three-function GSL package follows original inversion and binary64 operation
order and Sage's large-exponent log/exp path. Native comparisons cover subnormals, signed
zeros, infinities, NaNs, overflow and underflow. Libm portability limits are documented.

Real polynomial division incorrectly rejected rounded/NaN/infinite inverses and retained
leading cancellation terms. One modular-power regression over RDF caused NaN remainders
to grow without bound instead of remaining below the modulus degree. Division now uses
Sage's descending subtraction order and explicit remainder truncation. Unchecked quotients
preserve raw trailing zeros and their sometimes surprising original string representation.

The modular half-GCD/Newton helper also retained unbounded packed products. A direct
4,096-coefficient product raised a BigInt out-of-memory error. Of 64 new original product,
division and GCD comparisons, 16 failed before the repair; all pass after dispatching through
the existing bounded FLINT multiplication implementation.

polynomial_zmod_flint.pyx:__pow__, polynomial_element.pyx:__pow__/quo_rem, generic dense

nmod_poly/powmod_binexp.c,powmod_binexp_preinv.c,powmod_x_preinv.c,inv_series.c and
gr_poly/inv_series_newton.c; NTL ZZ_pEX.cpp and the GF2X/ZZ_pEX Sage linkage; GSL 2.8
sys/pow_int.c,specfunc/log.c,specfunc/exp.c. GSL archive provenance is in its package README.

An installed Sage 10.3 overflowing-list error damages cached GF(2) values after repeated

## Polynomial evaluation and composition (3.4.0)

This pass compares the reachable evaluation wrappers and their native kernels against the
bundled SageMath, FLINT and NTL source. It adds **34,922 shared original comparisons**.
The initial RDF matrix found 74 operation-order differences in 1,032 cases; the scalar/
polynomial input matrix found 8,522 differences before public dispatch was repaired.

| Repair | Original evidence and regression domain |
|---|---|
| Sparse compiled evaluation replaces unconditional Horner order at degrees 4–50,000 | `polynomial_element.pyx:__call__`, `polynomial_compiled.pyx`; raw IEEE coefficients, graph representations, gap algorithms, repeated calls, rational underflow and cutoff stress |
| Validate/coerce before zero and constant shortcuts | Generic `__call__`; 13 coefficient domains plus large prime/composite modular rings, incompatible parents, strings, booleans, primitive floats/RDF, nested lists and polynomial arguments |
| Preserve nested coefficient evaluation and generator substitution | Generic argument unpacking/map_coefficients path; extra arguments, None/empty argument lists, scalar and polynomial result parents |
| Delegate native scalar evaluation and QQ/word composition through the proper backend | Integer/rational FLINT wrappers, word FLINT wrapper, ZZ_pEX and composite NTL wrappers; native evaluation/composition pairs and cross-parent public composition |
| Keep large prime-field evaluation on the generic compiled path | `Polynomial_dense_mod_p` inherits generic `__call__`; its native power wrapper does not override scalar evaluation |
| Cache and distinguish generators, including zero-ring formatting and native integer predicates | `polynomial_ring.py:gen`, `polynomial_element.pyx:is_gen/_repr`, template/NTL overrides; constructors, arithmetic, powers, composition and identity flags |
| Reconstruct large-prime first powers as the bundled native wrapper does | Bundled `Polynomial_dense_mod_p.__pow__`; original NTL power plus reconstruction, including the non-generator inputs whose installed Sage 10.3 generic path returned self |
| Keep arbitrary-size scalar arguments out of word-sized index conversion | FLINT integer/rational scalar wrappers; existing 10²⁰ points and new signed 1,000-bit points across zero/constant/quadratic inputs |

New FLINT routines mirror integer/rational Horner and binary-splitting evaluation, native
unreduced rational denominators, Horner/Taylor/divide-and-conquer composition, integer Taylor
shift cutoffs and modular monomial-plus-constant composition. NTL prime and extension scalar
adapters mirror native Horner evaluation. The compiled evaluator uses an iterative instruction
graph and releases values at their final consumer; it preserves coefficient truth tests before
coercion and repeated-call cleanup.

The oracle compiles the exact bundled FLINT evaluation C bodies where installed FLINT's raw
rational denominator convention differs. Narrow Sage 10.3 adapters execute original native
power/reconstruction for bundled first-power allocation and apply the bundled composite scalar
result's base constructor. They do not substitute expected values or identity flags. All
adapters and unsupported domains are explicit in DEVIATIONS.md. Custom minimal coefficient
adapters remain usable, and native rational reconstruction uses the supplied parent.

The public API reference and executed examples cover all new exports, mixed evaluation and
composition signatures, compiled calls, generator flags and raw native array results.
Matrix/symbolic/complex evaluation actions, general multivariate dispatch, remaining polynomial
methods and the broader repository audit remain open; measured line execution is not a claim
of full behavioral coverage.

## Specialized binary matrix evaluation and M4RI multiplication (2026-09-10)

This pass adds **5,823 permanent original comparisons**. Polynomial evaluation previously
rejected the specialized binary matrix class. Matrix products also used scalar cubic loops
where the original delegates to M4RI. The port now delegates to a separate `m4ri-ts` package,
with packed row arithmetic, eight Gray-code tables, Strassen-Winograd/Bodrato multiplication,
a separate squaring schedule and word-aligned rectangular tails.

| Repair | Original evidence and comparative domain |
|---|---|
| Evaluate polynomials at specialized GF(2) matrices | `polynomial_element.pyx:__call__`, matrix scalar actions; 2,592 comparisons across coefficient parents, empty/rectangular/square dimensions and coefficient patterns |
| Preserve matrix possibilities in public evaluation types | Mixed scalar/matrix arguments and argument lists; strict virtual TypeScript compilation and executed public API examples |
| Delegate public/default and explicit multiplication to M4RI | `matrix_mod2_dense.pyx:_multiply_,_multiply_m4rm,_multiply_classical,_multiply_strassen`; 853 public comparisons, including a 2,048-square product, signed C-int conversion and dimension/empty/error order |
| Port native multiplication algorithms and helpers | M4RI 20251207 `strassen.c`, `brilliantrussian.c`, `graycode.c`, `mzd.c`; 1,858 comparisons, including 5,504-square/default-cutoff and large rectangular products |
| Preserve small-cutoff native errors without terminating JavaScript | 520 isolated native comparisons around 64/128-bit splitting boundaries; zero-width windows can cause original aborts or segmentation faults |

The isolated oracle builds the exact release pinned by the bundled Sage package metadata,
checks its SHA-256 and executes the unmodified static library in child processes. Most
ordinary kernels run installed M4RI through Cython. Native row words are serialized directly;
zero-width rows are not dereferenced. Explicit undefined-input exclusions and representation,
cache-tuning and ownership differences are documented in DEVIATIONS.md.

A comparison against the pre-change scalar implementation also verified every output entry
at sizes 256, 512 and 1,024; observed times improved from 26.69/458.63/2,222.58 ms to
11.51/29.05/92.69 ms respectively. These are local measurements, not performance guarantees.

## Binary matrix indexing and native slice extraction (2026-09-10)

This pass adds **19,522 permanent original comparisons**. Of the initial 8,176 distinct
row/entry/slice boundary cases, 7,660 disagreed before repair. After those bounds repairs,
a further 1,781 of 3,156 scalar-conversion cases still disagreed. All now match; another
8,190 cases compare aligned/unaligned word slices against both Sage and native M4RI.

| Repair | Original evidence and comparative domain |
|---|---|
| Negative entry indices and exact bounds errors | `matrix0.pyx:__getitem__/__setitem__`, `matrix_mod2_dense.pyx:row`; empty/rectangular matrices, valid negative indices and both bounds |
| Negative submatrix sizes mean remaining extents | `matrix_mod2_dense.pyx:submatrix`; start/count combinations, empty slices and column-before-row extent validation |
| Preserve signed C-index conversion and validation order | C-int reads versus Py_ssize_t row/write/slice arguments; bigint, Integer, Rational, booleans, numeric-index compatibility, fractional/nonfinite numbers, strings and null |
| Preserve original rational index distinction | `rational.pyx:__int__/__index__`; reads truncate while rows/slices/writes require integral rationals |
| Delegate row and slice extraction to M4RI | `mzd.c:mzd_submatrix`; packed aligned words and shifted windows, padding, zero dimensions and independent result allocation |

Only the input words intersecting a requested high-level slice are packed. Unrelated rows
and columns are not scanned. The public signatures and the new low-level submatrix export
are documented with an executed example. Native vector/cache identity, arbitrary index
protocols, JS numeric-index compatibility and undefined oversized native endpoints remain
explicit in DEVIATIONS.md.

## Binary density and native real-literal conversion

This pass adds **24,161 permanent original comparisons**: 3,208 binary density cases and
20,953 MPFR/real-literal conversion, formatting and state-transition cases. The initial
density comparison disagreed in 944 cases. Native parsing probes also exposed malformed
NaN handling and 75 repeated-setter sign discrepancies; real-literal probes exposed two
incorrect invalid-precision exception classes. Each defect has a shared regression.

| Repair | Original evidence and comparative domain |
|---|---|
| Exact density returns Rational, with integer zero for empty matrices | `matrix_mod2_dense.pyx:density`; empty, rectangular, sparse and dense matrices |
| Approximate density delegates and preserves native sampling | `mzd.c:mzd_density`; 64-bit boundaries, final full-word omission, automatic/explicit strides and isolated zero-column native faults |
| Real literals preserve native precision and original text | `real_mpfr.pyx:RealLiteral/create_RealNumber`; factory precision shortcut, padding/minimum precision, precision changes, signed zeros, infinities and NaN |
| Exact decimal conversion and IEEE boundaries | MPFR 4.2.1 `set_str/strtofr/get_d/get_str`; ties, subnormals, overflow/underflow, 4096-bit/digit limits and formatting options |
| Preserve mutable parser behavior and Sage precision errors | MPFR `strtofr` resets sign before parsing; empty setter retains state; invalid Sage precision raises `ValueError` |

The installed MPFR oracle is version 4.2.1, matching the bundled pin. Installed Sage 10.3
lacks the bundled `RealLiteral.__float__` override: its float observation explicitly executes
the bundled `float(x.numerical_approx(53))` expression. This avoids mistaking an original
version difference for a port defect. Exact literal observations use native state, while
inherited real arithmetic retains its documented binary64 limits. Unsupported conversion
domains and the safe native-fault exception are explicit in DEVIATIONS.md.

## Integer Gaussian validation, center rounding and tail conversion

This pass adds **806 permanent original comparisons**: 356 constructor combinations and
450 scalar/algorithm/seed cases. Sigma and tau validation now precede algorithm, center
integrality and precision dispatch. Missing sigma, explicit None sigma/center, invalid
precision and combinations of invalid inputs preserve original exception classes/messages.
The installed older precision message has a narrowly scoped bundled-source spelling adapter.

Sage's `Integer(tau)` rejects fractional Python floats; the port previously sampled with
fractional bounds. Integer centers now undergo the original 53-bit nearest-even conversion,
rather than being rejected above the safe-integer range. Bounds add/subtract integers
exactly: previous double arithmetic lost offsets even for the accepted center `2^53-1`.
The 450 scalar probes exposed 370 constructor/stream discrepancies; extending those probes
to native-derived bounds exposed 46 further mismatches after the first fixes.

Original evidence: `discrete_gaussian_integer.pyx:346-406`, `integer.pyx:718-724` and
`dgs_gauss_mp.c:111-165`. All five algorithm selections are covered, with signed centers

## Native real predicates and integer rounding

This pass adds **23,484 permanent original comparisons**: 3,600 Sage observations,
18,300 direct MPFR classification/comparison/rounding cases, and 1,584 extreme-exponent
rounding cases. The initial 576 Sage probes exposed 100 discrepancies, all repaired.

Real signs, infinity/integer/square predicates and multiplicative orders now inspect native
values; finite `1e400` no longer appears infinite, and `1e-400` no longer appears zero.
Floor/ceil/round/trunc delegate to native integer rounding and extraction, preserving
precision and the original singular-value error messages. Native comparisons with ±1
prevent high-precision values near a unit from acquiring an incorrect finite order.

The MPFR port implements classification, signed-integer comparison, rounding wrappers and
integer extraction from the bundled 4.2.1 sources. Rounding supports nearest-even,
nearest-away, toward zero, directed infinities and away from zero, including destination
aliasing and native inexact magnitudes. Its significand work is bounded by input/output
precision even at binary exponents ±100,000,000. Only extraction constructs the full
integer result. Direct MPFR header declarations correct omissions in the installed Sage
Cython bindings without adapting native output.

## Arithmetic prime iterators and successive differences

Added 1,228 permanent original comparisons. `primes()` now exhausts immediately,
`primes(stop)` starts at 2, and two-bound iteration advances lazily through next-prime
calls with proof selection. Explicit Infinity and Integer bounds are supported.
Large finite-bound tests draw a prefix without allocating the entire interval.

`differences` now rejects all nonpositive orders with Sage's message and returns an empty
list once the requested order reaches the input length. The iterative recursion-limit
adaptation is explicit in DEVIATIONS.md. `subfactorial` uses the factorial validation
message and supports Integer wrappers. Its prior Python oracle duplicated the recurrence;
it now invokes Sage's actual implementation. Evidence: bundled `arith/misc.py:primes`,
`differences`, `subfactorial` and `factorial`.

## PARI real addition and subtraction — 2026-09-11

While tracing the remaining PARI factorial backend, direct native checks found that
`addrr(1,1)` grew a 64-bit real to 65 bits. Exact-sum rounding at a fixed ulp also
lost whole-word precision during cancellation, mishandled zero accuracy, and gave
mixed integer operands the wrong conversion precision. The port now follows
`kernel/none/add.c:addrr_sign` and `addir_sign`, including word alignment, extension
thresholds, carry truncation and final guard rounding.

- Added 57,825 shared native comparisons across all six addition/subtraction entry
  points. The initial 57,820 expose 23,993 old mismatches; five additional controls
  execute final rounding overflow and simple carry/cancellation boundaries.
- The original C whole-word extension can read one word past a real operand's
  logical length. A controlled native probe demonstrates a one-bit output change
  when that padding changes from 0 to 2. The oracle allocates a zero guard and the
  port uses that deterministic value; DEVIATIONS.md records this upstream issue.
- Shared Python/TypeScript output frames agree exactly for all new cases, including
  sign, exponent, normalized mantissa and precision. Four colocated regressions
  preserve representative native frames.
- Arithmetic replay/public examples: 279,088 pass, plus all five new boundary cases.
  Fast: 6,163 pass, 32 skips, zero failures. Dependent qfb/buch: 96 pass; live Sage
  quadratic forms: 82/82 pass. All 588 type diagnostics are unchanged; bundles pass.
- All instrumented lines of the repaired addition kernels execute. Combined current
  execution is 92466/117153 (78.93%); configured comparisons total 907,350 in 29 areas.
  This remains an execution inventory, not proof of whole-port equivalence.
- Versions synchronized to 10.0.2. PARI real factorial and the broader source audit
  continue; existing real multiplication/division/transcendental gaps remain open.

## PARI real factorial and dependency kernels — 2026-09-11

The free PARI selector returned a sequentially computed bigint, although Sage calls
cypari2's real `mpfactr` backend. It now returns `{s,e,m,p}`, preserving native rounded
mantissa bits and a bigint exponent. GMP/default calls retain bigint results. The
signed-C-long conversion and native exponent overflow are restored.

The dependency implementation follows PARI's exact/precision-aware factorial product
thresholds and positive-integer gamma branch, including the original Bernoulli zeta
cache, Chudnovsky pi cache, exponential and reciprocal Newton iterations, AGM log,
and binary/sliding-window powering. A trace comparison caught depth-first product
callback evaluation; it now evaluates complete levels in native order. Two exp-near-1
comparisons require the native reciprocal iteration to preserve its final bit.

- Added 10,085 shared original comparisons: 9,404 real/dependency cases and 681 exact
  product/power value-and-trace cases. All match. Coverage includes 0/410 and the
  medium/gamma thresholds, signed errors, >2^53 exponents, and 64–8,192-bit precision.
- Original-selector baseline: 1,012 mismatches in 1,032 safe calls (all nonnegative
  calls return the wrong type). Another 580 original log controls match numerically;
  the AGM repair restores the missing algorithm branch rather than changing those
  rounded outputs.
- The installed PARI ABI uses word precision; the oracle explicitly translates bits
  and temporarily sets the exported exponential/log/reciprocal tuning constants to
  the bundled GMP values, then restores them. Direct C calls preserve native kernels
  when the high-level wrapper would choose a different operation.
- Final arithmetic replay, public examples and focused units: 289,183 pass. After

  Fast: 6,168 pass/32 skips. Qfb/buch: 96 pass; live quadratic forms: 82 pass;

- Combined execution: 92966/117649 (79.02%), with 917,435 configured seeded cases.
  New factorial/product/Bernoulli kernels execute all their instrumented lines;

before normalization. `divru` accepts bigint for the complete unsigned-word domain.

## Allocated zero precision — 2026-09-11

PARI integer/real conversions allocate a destination even when the input is zero.
The port now keeps that precision and `affrr`'s accuracy clamp. It also follows
allocation-based integer division and reciprocal validation: zero divided by an
allocated zero returns inexact zero, while nonzero inversions fail in the actual
nested division kernel. Zero `rdivii` retains the allocated destination and returns
before denominator validation, matching the original code.

- Added 2,052 native comparisons: 1,089 old mismatches across conversions, subsequent
  arithmetic and inverse origins. Four focused units and a public example also pass.
- Found that the older native serializer erased zero allocation. It now preserves
  the native length; regeneration from PARI exposes 90 existing `rdivii` mismatches
  repaired by this change. All 10,085 factorial/dependency controls match again.
- The Cython oracle uses cypari2's signal handling to safely observe native failures.
  Zero payload words remain intentionally unspecified; only their meaningful
  allocation/exponent is compared. Existing inverse error-class/GEN-rendering
  mappings remain explicit, and large uninitialized-zero reciprocal inputs retain
  a deterministic port error rather than reading unspecified memory.
- Full arithmetic/public examples/units: 302,611 pass. Fast: 6,178 pass/32 skips;
  qfb/buch: 96 pass. Adjacent quadratic/number-field/core replay: 195/195 pass.
  All 588 existing type diagnostics unchanged; all workspace bundles pass.
- Combined execution: 93153/117821 (79.06%); 930,861 configured seeded cases.
  Versions 11.0.2. Native multiplication/square-root and direct huge exponential
  audit work continues; full source/branch coverage is not claimed.

operands retain the general multiplication algorithm, which can produce a different bit.

## Function-field valuation and zero factorization — 2026-09-11

Valuation now converts the complete fraction argument through the existing fraction
and polynomial parents, rather than dropping its denominator. Polynomial valuation
rejects nonzero constants and evaluates both numerator and denominator before returning
infinity, preserving invalid-divisor errors even for zero inputs.

  polynomial. Bundled `polynomial_zmod_flint.pyx:820` and generic polynomial guards
  reject zero. Installed Sage 10.3 omits the word-modular guard, so the Python oracle

## Function-field fraction integration — 2026-09-11

Function-field `field()` and `element()` now return the actual fraction parent and
underlying element. The wrapper preserves identity and normalization state, observes
later fraction normalization, and delegates arithmetic and display to the chosen
backend. Negation follows generic multiplication by -1; binary powers start at the
least set bit, preserving the operand for exponent one.

  fraction string rows, 949 unreduced wrapper rows and 240 string-parent checks.
  Initial bridge results exposed 373 discrepancies. Subsequent probes exposed 159

- String construction attempts polynomial conversion before evaluating in the
  fraction generators, preserving numerator/denominator evaluation order and
  converting Boolean comparison results through fraction resolution. Syntax is
  checked before unknown-name lookup. General Sage/Python evaluation remains an
  explicit parser limitation, not a claimed implementation.

## Function-field inversion and orders — 2026-09-11

Modular inversion preserves the original empty assertion errors for invalid generators
and nonintegral inputs. It returns the Bezout coefficient divided by a constant gcd,
without a redundant remainder operation; constants can therefore be inverted modulo
the zero ideal, including native zero-modulus paths whose gcd is not monic.

- Added 2,556 ordinary inversion rows exposing 1,547 discrepancies: 1,478 assertion
  class/message mismatches and 69 valid zero-modulus inverses. Removing the remainder
  exposed 36 of those same cases to a nonmonic gcd, repaired by the source's unit
  scaling. Another 96 unreduced-fraction rows test exact denominator-one assertions.
- Added 1,314 order rows exposing 556 discrepancies: constructor error propagation,
  conversion between finite and infinite ideals, existing-element and unique-parent
  identities in ideal monoids, and native empty `is_subring` errors. Constructors

## FLINT word dependencies for square roots — 2026-09-11

Ported `n_sqrtmod` and its word dependencies: binary unsigned/signed Jacobi symbols,
the parity-accumulating Jacobi core, exact square testing, limb preinversion and binary
modular powers. This prepares the original polynomial-root route; function-field
square roots are not yet switched to it in this batch.

attributed this route to NTL; the GF2X linkage has no factor override. Standalone
binary convenience helpers and other finite-field factor routes remain open.

  squaring, division, valuations and unreduced initial power entries. F2v.c kernel

  preserve native degree dispatch, factor order and exact global RNG consumption.
- 11,139 shared comparisons exercise exhaustive small inputs, degree 20/21
  dispatch, repeated factors, large products, matrix dependencies, guard boundaries
  and Sage GF(2)/Zmod(2) callers. A spy regression verifies actual backend routing.

## Quotient powers and binary automorphism composition — 2026-09-11

The bundled FpX.c:2196-2209/2322-2337 leaves the first quotient-power entries
and the first automorphism-vector entries unreduced. The old helpers reduced
these entries. FpXQ_autpow also used a linear substitution loop instead of PARI's
left-to-right binary fold (FpX.c:2354-2365, bb_group.c:191-223). Reduction makes
these composition orders observably different for general substitutions.

- The initial 3,699 direct comparisons found 845 mismatches. All are repaired.

  moduli and explicit guards for invalid/unrepresentable native counts.

## PARI matrix multiplication dispatch — 2026-09-11

The existing Buchmann ZM_mul always used the classical loop. The bundled ZV.c
switches to a 2x2 seven-product kernel, size-dependent Strassen–Winograd products,
and modular reconstruction for large balanced inputs. Those routes now live in
the shared ZV.ts implementation, with the existing Buchmann entry point delegating
to it. FpV.ts and F2v.ts supply the modular-composition matrix dependencies.

- 585 integer comparisons exercise every word-length cutoff, odd rectangular
  shapes, dimension 69/70/71, 2x2 kernel boundaries, asymmetric coefficients and
  zero products. Another 666 compare word, binary and arbitrary-modulus dispatch;
  18 compare explicit portable guards and valid boundary controls.
- Native testing confirmed modulus one is valid; the initial new guard was
  corrected before integration and now has permanent comparisons. Word matrices
  use the native 140/40/70 thresholds, packed binary products select/XOR columns,
  and integer reconstruction uses native high-bit primes and a balanced CRT tree.
  A spy regression confirms large products actually call the modular backend.
- Live: 208,296/208,296; caller/docs/units: 254,384 pass; Buchmann/Galois: 97 pass;
  fast: 6,298 pass/32 skips. Native mat/linear suites pass under static/dynamic GP;
  all 588 type diagnostics are unchanged; bundles pass. Versions: 20.7.0.
- Inventory: 350 production files, 7,539 named callables, 1,267,875 configured
  comparisons, 557 stubs and 251 partial callables. Combined execution is
  95090/119396 (79.64%). Remaining uncovered entries in the matrix kernels are
  instrumentation-only braces/type declarations, retained in the denominator.
  Full behavioral coverage remains open; Brent–Kung composition is next.

power callers delegate to these kernels, including the original square steps.

## PARI modular square roots and fused powering — 2026-09-11

The original square-root entry delegates unsigned-word moduli to Fl_sqrt and
uses signed-small/Gauss-sum shortcuts, Atkin, Cipolla or Tonelli-Shanks for large
primes. The port previously used a uniform Euler precheck and Tonelli/Atkin path.
The restored kernels preserve the native cost test, candidate-prime order,
minimal root, supplied-generator semantics, and observable composite/zero errors.

- 10,022 baseline comparisons found 402 discrepancies, including eight hangs
  for residues modulo 9/25/49/81. The original Euler precheck accepted these
  residues but its nonresidue search never terminated. A worker-isolated replay
  retained each timed-out baseline and the native expected error. The initial
  untraced runner was stopped; its empty JSON output was not used as evidence.
- Added 21,551 permanent shared comparisons: 10,022 baseline, 11,308 word/
  supplied-generator/fused-schedule controls and 221 cost/prime-search boundaries.
  These include every residue for small primes, signed reductions, zero and
  safe native composite controls, all Gauss-sum shortcuts, the signed-word
  boundary, e=23/24 Cipolla cutoff and primes with v2(p-1) through 256.
- Fused callback controls compare native ordered execution using deliberately
  noncommutative callback arithmetic, through 2,048-bit positive and negative
  exponent magnitudes. Zero is an explicit adapter guard. Word nonresidues use
  the existing null failure convention instead of C's ULONG_MAX sentinel.
- Routing tests verify word delegation, the no-power signed-small shortcut and
  the exact Cipolla boundary. Public Fp_sqrt_i, Fl_sqrt and gen_pow_fold examples
  execute; docs/routing tests: 141 pass. Bundles pass, all 588 type diagnostics
  remain unchanged. Bundled native arith/sqrtn suites pass.
- Full cached-native caller replay: 323,203 pass, zero failures; fast: 6,328
  pass and 32 existing skips; extended arithmetic/elliptic callers: 329 pass.
  The latter include native SEA regression vectors, the 128-bit point-order
  proof, P-256 and Curve25519. The previously verified native prefix was reused;
  the 21,551 newly generated rows bring the function-field transcript to 277,097.
- New helper execution: 177/182 lines. The next-prime fallback after one million

## PARI extension-polynomial arithmetic dependencies — 2026-09-11

Twelve native product, square, coefficient-reduction and normalization entry points
now mirror FpXX.c, FlxX.c, F2x.c and polarit3.c. These are required dependencies for
the extension-factor backend, whose quotient/GCD/factor routing remains unfinished.

- Generic coefficients retain native integer versus polynomial tags. Integer-only
  generic squares return unreduced ZX_sqr results; mixed products transpose by
  inner degree. Other generic and word products use native Kronecker packing of
  width 2*deg(T)-1 and delegate to ZX/Flx products. Binary products pack width
  2*deg(T)+1, while binary squares square coefficients and insert odd-power zeros.

  Generic monic inputs can retain unreduced lower coefficients, while word/binary
  monic inputs still invert and reduce. Native extended GCD computes only the
  required Bézout output. Inverse failures preserve their original polynomial
  payloads, with the documented canonical inner variable y.

  and eight large coefficient-array comparisons under Node and Bun. All compare

## PARI extension-polynomial division and reciprocal dependencies — 2026-09-11

Fifteen division, remainder, quotient, inverse-Barrett and cached-reduction entry
points now mirror FpXX.c, FlxX.c and F2x.c. The shared coefficient context also
supplies the existing normalization routines.

- Native basecase, Newton and chunked Barrett paths retain distinct thresholds,

  445 guard/error controls, 4,914 binary packing boundaries and 960 reciprocal
  raw-input controls. Expected values and exception classes/messages come from

  although native packing has space for them. Relaxing that guard fixes 4,613

  that native basecases preserve; 12 comparative failures exposed this. Generic
  basecases retain their separate final-reduction schedule. Focused unit checks
  accompany the permanent native comparisons.

## PARI extension GCD, Bézout and recursive half-GCD — 2026-09-11

Nine native GCD, extended-GCD and half-GCD entry points now mirror FpXX.c,
FlxX.c and F2x.c. The results retain native scaling, coefficient tags and
row-major matrix entries.

- The extension basecase tracks V on every Euclidean iteration and derives U
  through quotient division. This differs from the existing prime-field helper.
  The private V-only mode omits U, including its native zero-first-input error.
  Generic word-size primes delegate to word kernels and collapse constant
  polynomial coefficients. Half-GCD retains its separate raw-input entry path.
- Recursive half-GCD follows native thresholds, low/high polynomial splitting,
  seven-product Strassen matrix multiplication and reverse Bézout replay. The
  three GCD and extended-GCD thresholds are independently compared.
- Added 24,532 permanent shared cases: 4,872 basecases, 6,144 cutoff cases,
  3,168 raw-coefficient cases, 9,664 binary packing cases, 672 guard/inverse
  controls and 12 isolated termination comparisons. Native results and exact
  exceptions come from the compiled bundled PARI source.
- Raw half-GCD exposed 12 inputs rejected by the previous binary packing guard.
  Native allocation permits additional coefficients beyond deg(T), including
  overlapping blocks. The port now XORs shifted 64-bit words for those inputs,
  retaining the existing string-packing path for nonoverlapping coefficients.
  Direct packing comparisons fix 6,696 previously rejected cases. The guard now
  models the actual native word allocation and still excludes out-of-bounds writes.
- Fourteen prior binary allocation-boundary expectations were regenerated against
  native C; the old 352,314-row transcript is retained unchanged as a baseline.
  Appending the new comparisons gives 376,846 function-field transcript rows.
  Large integer fixtures preserve decimal strings through the shared argument
  format, avoiding the temporary probe's initial JavaScript-number rounding.
- The bundled binary remainder quirk makes GCD cycle at degrees 100, 102 and
  103 in the tested X^n+1 versus X² family over T=7. The port preserves this
  native behavior. Both implementations run in isolated processes under a
  two-second operation deadline; nearby terminating degrees and paired-division
  extgcd controls distinguish the cycle from ordinary initialization/runtime cost.
- Final arithmetic/division/GCD/native/docs replay: 62,634 pass in 121.43 seconds.

## PARI extension random projections and truncated products — 2026-09-11

Eight missing dependencies now mirror FpXX.c, FlxX.c and Flx.c: extension random
sampling, generic/word dot products, and truncated products and squares.

- Random sampling visits outer coefficients in ascending order and delegates
  each coefficient to the existing native random_FpX/random_Flx port. Constant
  T gives empty coefficient polynomials; zero outer length does not consume the
  random state. Sampling reads T's canonical degree without reducing its values.
- Generic dot products accumulate exact tagged integer/polynomial products,
  then perform one field reduction. Word dots accumulate Flx products and modular
  sums before one remainder. Tests preserve polynomial zero after cancellation,
  scalar zero for scalar-only inputs, and the absence of an inverse error when
  unreduced terms cancel before final reduction.

  truncates the packed product, then unpacks/reduces. Word arithmetic performs
  the full reduction before outer truncation. Tests distinguish the resulting
  n=0 inverse-error behavior. Generic integer-only squares retain unreduced ZX
  coefficients; an equivalent multiply reduces them modulo p, as in the source.

## PARI prime-polynomial linear boundary repairs — 2026-09-11

Direct comparison of the existing FpX_red/add/sub/neg/Fp_mul exports found 5,280
mismatches in 18,408 preserved prepatch probes. All are repaired.

- Negative modulus adjustment previously added the negative divisor to a negative
  remainder, producing incorrect residues. Reduction now uses the absolute
  modulus, matching the observed native integer primitives. There were 3,868

and TS output but still violated PARI's explicit no-overflow precondition.

- perm_powu now applies native itou magnitude conversion before even the empty

  zv_prod explicitly assumes no overflow; the port guards each product prefix,
  and the oracle certifies that same precondition with native arbitrary-precision
  multiplication before entering the unchecked routine. Guard records are labeled
  separately and do not attribute a RangeError to PARI itself.
- vecsmall_lexcmp returns the native sign for unequal-length prefixes; uniq uses
  native counting when the vector's nonnegative entries fit the counting cutoff,

## PARI fixed-field symmetric-polynomial evaluation — 2026-09-12

Native galconj.c:883 reduces each Newton sum with signed remii, then sympol_eval
combines weighted columns over the integers without a final modular reduction.
The old implementation normalized every weighted combination, losing signs and
values above the modulus. It also returned a zero column where PARI returns
scalar zero when there are no nonzero weights. Cancellation of nonzero weights
still yields a column. The return signature is now bigint[] | bigint; LLM.md and
executed examples explain caller narrowing. Fixed-field constructors select a
nonzero weight and preserve their existing column invariant. This API correction
uses a major version bump, 21.0.0.

Newton powering now delegates to arith1.Fp_powu and the existing shared scalar
power engine: native word casts, small-exponent shortcuts and word/Montgomery/
Barrett schedules replace the linear loop and unreduced exponentiation. The
first-power shortcut for a multiword modulus preserves raw signed inputs before
the Newton sum's signed remainder. Separating-weight and orbit-selection controls
match native outputs. Additional direct unsigned-power controls exercise zero,
word-width casts, unreduced bases and modulus sizes through 8,101 bits.

## PARI perfect-power helper fidelity — 2026-09-12

A direct 7,460-case probe exposed 1,016 discrepancies: 670 negative-input
Z_isanypower results, 171 is_357_power exponent/root/mask results, and 175
is_pth_power failure records. Native Z_isanypower has a signed wrapper around its
magnitude-only internal search. The port previously exposed the internal search
under the public name. It now keeps only the odd part of a negative input's
maximal exponent and returns the negative base, or [0, original input] on
failure. For example, -64 returns [3, -4], whereas -16 is not an integer perfect
power with exponent greater than one.

The native single-word 3/5/7 search prefers smaller exponents; the multiword
search prefers larger ones. Its residue sieve and word valuation checks also
change the returned mask. Those checks are restored with the native 106-entry
mask table, while retaining the existing documented exact-Newton-root
substitution for verified floating-point guesses. The p-th-power search now
returns exponent zero on failure, and both internal consumers use that sentinel.
The original C comment about higher-power priority does not describe its
single-word dispatch. All probe results now agree; the old unit expectation for
3^21 was corrected to the native word path.

The original probe also recorded native iterator T.p beyond its end bound. The
TypeScript iterator facade exposes exhaustion as null, not a native p field.
The comparator now maps an out-of-bound native p to the test's zero exhaustion
observation; in-bound cutoff primes remain observable. This adapter resolves
39 state-only differences after the actual return-value fixes. All 175 affected
failure records already differed in their exponent, so the 1,016 bug-record
count does not include any representation-only records.

older finite-field magnitude adapters: Python explicitly used abs(n), whereas

## Signed power and MPQS helper fidelity — 2026-09-12

All 2,732 new native comparisons match, repairing 957 previously mismatching
records. The 1,680 negative k-th-power cases exposed 285 discrepancies: native
modular filters can reject negative nonpowers, but successful filters reach the
unsupported-negative sqrtnr error. The port returned roots instead. Sixty-eight
additional exponent-threshold controls exposed 14 differences: twelve more of
those errors and two BigInt allocation failures for input 2 with exponents
17,886,697 and 17,886,698. An exact bit-length guard now establishes root one
before Newton iteration constructs an enormous intermediate power. Native zero
inputs to this private helper exhibited unsafe stack behavior; they were not
used as a deterministic error oracle. Valid documented inputs are nonzero
integers and positive exponents.

The 276 relation comparisons exposed 212 signed-discriminant discrepancies.
MPQS's shared class-group relation combiner now computes inverses modulo |D|.
Class-group fixtures satisfy Y^2-D = 4*q*product(p_i^e_i); factor-mode fixtures
satisfy Y^2 = q*product(p_i^e_i) modulo N. The latter 64 controls matched before
and after. The class-group entry points themselves remain unported. Packed
exponents and indices stay within both native and TypeScript representations.

The 708 reduced quadratic-residue comparisons exposed 446 noncanonical MPQS
square roots. Native Fl_sqrt_pre_i always selects the smaller root, including

## Sparse binary kernel algorithm fidelity — 2026-09-12

The first 426 comparisons exposed 83 mismatches, including 63 different ordered
bases. Another 340 comparisons exposed 337 mismatches, including 194 different
bases. There are 420 mismatching baseline records in total. All 766 shared
comparisons now agree on the singleton-elimination map, ordered kernel vectors,

verifies the MPQS one-based Uint32Array adapter against the same result and seed.

F2v.ts now follows F2v.c:671–1077 and alglin1.c:4851. It keeps dense elimination
through 640 rows, then removes singleton columns and uses 64-bit block Lanczos.
The port preserves eight 256-entry byte tables, nonsingular block selection,
all three recurrence terms, failed-start retries, final independent-column
selection, and inflation to original columns. MPQS delegates to this backend.
Its former dense-only substitution changed observable results and random state;
its claim of preserving the same full kernel space was also incorrect because
native Lanczos may return a proper subspace. For a zero 130-column matrix, the
seeded native result has 64 vectors rather than the dense basis's 130.

86 failed starts, with up to 35 iterations in one case. The 65-column cycle at

random matrices, long singleton chains, repeated columns, and matrices through

## Order-from-multiple factor lists and valuations — 2026-09-12

The source treats an empty factorization or prime list as absent. JavaScript

empty/nonempty combinations, explicit-factor precedence, reversed lists, extra
nondividing entries, results and complete custom-operation traces.

Prime-list valuations now delegate to arith.valuation/Integer.valuation and GMP

and 3^600. At M=0 the source produces infinite valuations: single-base prime-power
cases terminate through its helper, while multi-factor costs yield native
phase/sign/power errors. These specific branches are implemented without claiming
a general symbolic infinity ring. SignError preserves ArithmeticError inheritance
and the native name. Prime-list oracle entries are Sage Integers, matching the
IntegerLike adapter; raw Python integers can produce different infinity-power
error wording.

Native nontermination was probed separately with an interruptible harness and
is excluded from permanent executable cases. For example, with additive value 1

## Generic multiple-iterator state and copying — 2026-09-12

Sage validates and copies at iterator construction, advances the stored state
before returning each value, and advances its index even if the operation fails.
The previous JavaScript generator delayed validation until next(), retained
mutable inputs, advanced only after yielding, and closed permanently on callback
errors. The repair preserves the public Generator interface while implementing
Sage's construction and next() schedule. Standard identities now come from the
parent when available, including a callable parent() as used by number fields.

The 608 new native comparisons contain 576 prepatch mismatches: 480 mutable-state
controls (456 discrepancies), 48 second-copy failure controls (48), 48 parent and
identity controls (40), and 32 quadratic-field parent/power controls (32). Cases
observe construction, external mutation, each next(), operation errors and
recovery, custom copy hooks, indexed values, empty and invalid counts, and both
standard operations. The oracle loads the bundled generic.py in installed Sage.
Host __copy__/copy hooks and descriptor copying are documented adapters, not an
implementation of Python pickle/reduce semantics. JavaScript return()/throw() and
for-of closing behavior remain explicit host conventions.

example pass. Frozen tests: 34,709 focused with 46,753 assertions (3.97 seconds);

## Number-field scalar coercion and zero division — 2026-09-12

Number-field addition, subtraction, division and equality previously required a
field element even for rational scalars accepted by Sage. The field constructor
also rejected Integer wrappers and truncated JavaScript number inputs with floor.
The repair coerces RationalLike inputs through the field, retains a field-operand
equality overload for structural compatibility with generic GroupElement, and
converts binary64 inputs through the existing RR.simplest_rational kernel. This
matters even for large integral-looking real inputs: the native conversion of
2^100 as a real is 1267650600228229331127959027712. Bigint/Integer inputs stay exact.
Nonfinite conversion diagnostics are compared on unembedded fields, matching the
port's field representation; this does not claim new embedding support.

Native inversion of a field zero raises "number field element division by zero".
Quadratic rational scalar division first inverts in QQ and instead reports
"rational division by zero". General-degree fields coerce scalars into the field
first. Both routes and negative powers of zero now preserve these distinctions.
Existing number-field backend delegation work remains open.

There are 1,530 new native comparisons across five fields, signed/fractional and
constant/nonconstant operands, bigint/Integer/Rational/field operand variants,
finite reals over exponent scales -100 through 100, and nonfinite reals. They
repair 850 mismatched records: 563 arithmetic/construction, 215 real conversion,
and 72 zero-operation cases. The full 1,554-record area and all 6,508 group records
match. The original 24-record native prefix is preserved.

## Number-field coefficient indexing — 2026-09-12

The previous __getitem__ returned zero for out-of-range indices. Sage's general
number-field element checks degree bounds and raises IndexError, while its
quadratic square-root/gaussian representation indexes a tuple directly and accepts
-2 and -1. Error messages differ between representations. Nonintegral indices
also expose the native branch order: general elements check their trimmed
coefficient length before list indexing, so a zero element can return zero for
an in-bounds fractional index; square-root elements always perform tuple indexing.

Representation selection uses the original defining polynomial, including its
leading sign and scaling, and the source's discriminant squarefree_part(bound=10000).
A module-private WeakMap preserves that metadata and caches the selection lazily.
The original wheel and exact GMP divisibility/division semantics are reproduced
with BigInt. Full factorization would be wrong here: x^2-2*10007^2 uses the native
square-root representation, whereas x^2-2*9973^2 uses the general representation.
Negative leading coefficients and large factors in polynomial scaling also matter.

The 1,638 new cases include fourteen defining polynomials/scalings, zero/constant/
nonconstant elements, negative and oversized indices, bigint/Integer/host-number
indices, fractions and nonfinite numbers. Integral host-number indices explicitly
adapt to Python integers; the public index API also accepts exact IntegerLike
inputs. There were 1,332 prepatch discrepancies. All 3,192 number-field records
and 6,508 group records match; the original 1,554-record native prefix is preserved.
Eight regressions and an executed public example pass.

## Integer/rational arithmetic and generic-group interoperability — 2026-09-12

Integer-left addition, subtraction and multiplication previously applied JavaScript
BigInt operations directly to Rational objects. Comparisons likewise lacked the
native QQ coercion. These calls now delegate to the existing Rational kernels;
IntegerLike overloads retain Integer results and Rational operands retain Rational
results even when integral. Signed and near-equal 256-bit controls verify exact
comparison without floating-point coercion. Integer.div remains floor division.

BSGS on Integer(2), Integer(8), [0,10] failed because generic inversion required
inv(), whereas Integer exposes the native __invert__(). Standard inverse dispatch
now recognizes both names. Neutral powers/actions supply identities for numeric
host classes without a parent member. Generic GroupElement equality is typed
against this rather than an unrelated abstract group element. Overloads expose
Integer | Rational for standard Integer multiplicative multiple and
GroupOps<Integer | Rational> for standard parsing, preserving the native QQ
completion and avoiding false Integer-only result assumptions.

The 1,296 arithmetic comparisons contained 225 prepatch mismatches; 384 group
comparisons contained 108. Cases check arithmetic result parents, Boolean results,
signed/large exact operands, both BSGS branches, finite and infinite-order controls,
identity bases, multiple/order/discrete-log APIs and parsed operation components.
The full 8,230 Integer and 6,892 group areas match, with old native prefixes of
6,934 and 6,508 records preserved. The 3,192 number-field records also pass replay.

## Rational equality with real inputs — 2026-09-12

Rational.eq explicitly accepted JavaScript numbers but converted them with BigInt.
Fractions and nonfinite values therefore threw, while large integral-looking values
and underflowed rationals compared exactly instead of following Sage's real coercion.
The method now delegates to RDF.__call__(this).eq(other). The existing RDF rational
conversion uses the audited nearest-binary64 kernel; its native equality handles
NaN and signed zeros. Exact Rational/IntegerLike comparisons retain their path.

The 1,170 new comparisons use raw binary64 patterns: fixed boundary/special values
and 66 seeded random words, paired with exact, nearby, extremely large/small and
ordinary fractional rationals. They expose 812 prepatch mismatches, including
QQ(1)/10 == float(0.1), rounded 2^100+1, underflow to zero, overflow to infinity,
and NaN comparisons. Both oracle sides use the same explicit bit patterns.
The full 7,472-record rational area matches; its 6,302-record native prefix is
preserved. Five focused regressions and an executed public example pass.

## RDF scalar coercion and binary multiple — 2026-09-12

RDF arithmetic read operand.value directly, producing NaN for primitive scalars
and rationals and TypeError for Integer wrappers. Scalar operands now use the
existing RDF constructor and native double arithmetic/equality. Python integer
and bool zero addition/one multiplication preserve ModuleElement's object reuse;
ZZ, QQ, RDF and float operands follow ordinary arithmetic. Concrete-this
overloads preserve structural compatibility with polynomial RingElement.

Generic multiple incorrectly bypassed the source's binary group algorithm for
standard operations. Removing that bypass repairs additive zero repetitions of
NaN/infinity, signed zero, inverse handling and binary rounding. For example,
31 additive repetitions of RDF(1e-200) have bits 0x16b7ba9d84a47fee, while native
scalar multiplication rounds differently. Multiplicative repeated -0 retains
-0 through the original idempotence shortcut. Algorithms using _power_func
intentionally retain native standard powers; their separate behavior is tested.

The 2,922 permanent cases comprise 1,925 scalar, 385 bool, 216 object-alias and
396 group-multiple records. They expose 1,789 prepatch mismatches. All new cases
match live Sage, using bundled generic.py for group operations. The polynomial
comparison uses its preserved 276,371-record native prefix plus 2,526 live new
records; all 278,897 TypeScript records match. All 7,288 group records match a
fresh native run, and both local canonical prefixes are preserved.

Frozen validation: 57,779 focused tests, 69,914 assertions (8.88 seconds); 6,680
fast tests, 32 existing skips, 1,864,236 assertions (44.18 seconds); 355 callers,
160,650 assertions (50.96 seconds). Eight regressions and the public example pass.
Build passes. Removing obsolete casts eliminates four existing TS2352 diagnostics;
all other 584 normalized diagnostics are unchanged. All 400 production hashes
remain unchanged. Combined line execution: 101670/125647 (80.92%). Version 21.0.27.
The broader audit remains open; BSGS and order-testing operation schedules are next.


## Generic group operation schedules and hash equality — 2026-09-12

BSGS used native standard powers and recomputed the giant factor rather than
reusing the final baby step. It now obtains the parent identity, preserves
inverse-before-power evaluation, uses generic binary multiple and multiplies the
giant factor on the left. has_order also uses binary multiple and computes both
recursive inputs eagerly before its short-circuit tests. order_from_bounds uses
binary divisor scaling while preserving native order_from_multiple powers.
Discrete logarithms retain _power_func's native standard operation but restore
its zero-power identity checks and direct negative powers.

BSGS also confused equal string keys with equal elements. A colliding-string
modular group returned 8 instead of 35; distinct NaN intermediates could return a
false logarithm such as 17. Hash buckets now compare object identity, then element
equality, and preserve duplicate-key index updates. The remaining consistent-key
requirement and collision-heavy performance trade-off are documented. RDF error
formatting now distinguishes Sage's ordinary NaN/+infinity/-infinity strings
from the nan/inf/-inf spellings used by its float-backed empty format method.

There are 3,604 new permanent comparisons: 2,460 exact modular operation traces
and 1,144 RDF algorithm/error cases. They expose 1,575 prepatch mismatches.
The modular cases cover six algorithms, standard/custom operations, identity and
nonidentity elements, composite orders, both BSGS branches, nonzero lower bounds,
known order divisors and deliberately colliding string keys. The native oracle
executes bundled generic.py. All 10,892 group records match a fresh native run,
with the existing 7,288-record canonical prefix preserved. Ten regressions pass.

Frozen validation: 61,393 focused tests, 73,529 assertions (8.01 seconds); 6,690
fast tests, 32 existing skips, 1,864,247 assertions (44.05 seconds); 355 callers,
160,650 assertions (50.93 seconds). A test helper was kept inside the unit-test
file to respect the package's TypeScript rootDir; no production change was
needed for that validation repair. Build passes, all 584 normalized diagnostics
are unchanged, and all 400 production hashes match the freeze. Combined line
execution: 101640/125592 (80.93%). Version 21.0.28. Parent-identity and equality dispatch are
next; the broader audit remains open.


## Generic group parent identity and equality dispatch — 2026-09-12

Order reduction substituted is_zero/is_one predicates for the source's equality
against the parent identity. Discrete logarithms skipped the initial parent
lookup, then repeated it in nested BSGS calls. Bounds-based order searches also
repeated lookups. Private helpers now carry the parsed identity and chosen native
power function through nested calls, including automatic exponential bounds.
This preserves _power_func's native standard powers after normalization and
propagates parent/equality exceptions at the original evaluation point. No public
signatures or options changed; stored-parent and parentless numeric adapters remain.

The 3,870 new comparisons trace parent identities, equality, predicates and group
operations through seven algorithms. Standard/custom modes cover exact modular
groups, identities, nonidentities and automatic bounds. Parent ValueError and
equality TypeError sentinels test exception ordering without changing arithmetic.
An exploratory equality ValueError with automatic bounds intentionally triggers
the original's retry loop; those probe processes were stopped, and the permanent
sentinel uses TypeError so both original and port terminate. This is a harness
constraint, not an unreachability claim or a production behavior change.

There are 795 prepatch discrepant records. All new cases and all 14,762 group
records match live bundled generic.py, preserving the 10,892-record native prefix.
Seven direct regressions cover identity reuse, explicit equality, automatic bounds,
parent-first errors and native powers. Frozen validation: 65,270 focused tests,
77,420 assertions (9.09 seconds); 6,697 fast tests, 32 existing skips, 1,864,268
assertions (44.42 seconds); 355 callers, 160,650 assertions (50.80 seconds).
Build passes; 584 normalized type diagnostics and 400 production hashes are
unchanged. Combined line execution: 101621/125550 (80.94%). Version 21.0.29. Pollard parent and
mutability handling, collision equality and singleton bounds are next; the broader
audit remains open.


## Pollard mutability, stored-point equality and singleton bounds — 2026-09-12

Both Pollard routines skipped parent parsing and set_immutable calls. They now
parse the target parent before validation and preserve the native mutation
positions: rho freezes each walk point; lambda freezes tame and wild points and
can freeze its input target. A reused binary power may freeze the base before an
error. The small-order rho fallback remains the original BSGS path without added
mutation calls. Native standard powers are retained after operation normalization.

Rho's memory confused string-key equality with group-element equality. With a
509-element additive group printed modulo four, it exhausted retries instead of
returning 17. Bucket lookup now compares identity/equality; eviction removes only
the specific remembered element and keeps other colliding keys. Partition hashing
retains the documented host adapter. Lambda's forced k>=1 workaround is removed:
single-value bounds invoke the hash callback and then raise ZeroDivisionError,
with no random step-size draws. The former deliberate deviation and LLM reference
are updated to reflect the restored behavior.

The 692 permanent comparisons comprise 656 modular Pollard cases and 36 binary
matrix cases; they expose 504 prepatch mismatches. They compare full operation and
mutation traces, parent/freeze exceptions, selected logs, retry exhaustion, input
immutability flags and the next 64 CPython bits. Rho uses the same documented DJB2
partition hash on both sides; lambda uses an explicit shared hash. Cases cover
standard/custom operations, small and large prime orders, invalid bounds/orders,
singleton intervals, constant and partially colliding keys, and mutation hooks
that require immutability or raise an exception. The original algorithm is loaded
from bundled generic.py; actual Sage and port GF(2) matrices verify the flags too.
All 15,454 group records match a fresh native run and preserve the 14,762-record
canonical prefix. Nine direct regressions and an executed LLM assertion pass.

Frozen validation: 65,972 focused tests, 78,146 assertions (9.67 seconds); 6,707
fast tests, 32 existing skips, 1,864,302 assertions (44.33 seconds); 355 callers,
160,650 assertions (51.06 seconds). Build passes; all 584 normalized type
diagnostics and 400 production hashes are unchanged. Combined line execution:
101598/125518 (80.94%). Version 21.0.30. Remaining number-field element methods and polynomial
kernels are next; MPQS defensive paths and the broader source audit remain open.


## Number-field coefficient reduction, defining equations and powers — 2026-09-12

NumberFieldElement truncated coefficients beyond the field degree instead of
reducing the polynomial. Degree-one generators incorrectly returned zero; public
polynomial getters and field display discarded the original equation's scaling.
The integer-power loop allocated a replacement for exponent one and used a
different multiplication schedule. Reduction now delegates through the FLINT
rational-remainder and pseudo-division ports, retaining the native recursive splits
and thresholds. Original equations are stored separately from their monic arithmetic
model. The mirrored Sage arith/power helper preserves integer inverse errors,
least-significant-set-bit scheduling, left multiplication and object reuse; pow's
signature now accepts IntegerLike, with a compiled unit and executed LLM example.

The 4,864 element cases expose 1,218 prepatch mismatches. They cover 16 field
presentations, degrees one through five, nonmonic and fractional defining equations,
large and over-degree coefficient vectors, invariants, predicates and signed powers.
Another 250 comparisons exercise native generic_power operation traces and aliasing,
including exponents beyond 64 and 100 bits. Six hundred direct bundled-FLINT C
comparisons cover basecase/divide-and-conquer pseudo quotient/remainder/exponents
and rational remainder across native degree thresholds, degree drops, leading
coefficients of both signs and multiprecision leading coefficients. New backend
functions have no prepatch implementation to count as a mismatch.

Caller validation exposed assumptions that the public equation was always monic.
Quadratic unit conversion, exact signs, approximate logarithms, the existing
equation-order different and Frobenius filtering now normalize explicitly. The
288 scaled-caller comparisons exposed 236 failures of the intermediate getter
change (200 unit and 36 other caller records); these are integration regressions
caught before commit, not additional failures of the original baseline. All pass.
Unit-generator, regulator-rounding and port-alias adapters are explicit in the
fixtures and DEVIATIONS.md. Different-ideal controls here have a monogenic maximal
order; the older derivative shortcut is invalid more generally and is queued next.

All 8,594 number-field records match a fresh Sage run and preserve the previous
3,192-record canonical prefix. The 278,897 existing polynomial records were replayed
against their cached native transcript; 600 newly registered records match a fresh
bundled-FLINT run, giving 279,497 polynomial records. Temporary JSON fixtures store
large coefficients as strings: an early numeric JSON probe rounded them on the
JavaScript side and is excluded from discrepancy counts. Twelve new unit regressions
and one executed API-reference example supplement the permanent comparative cases.

## Number-field ideal trace duals and powers — 2026-09-12

The old two-generator quadratic inverse negated the nonconstant coefficient,
which is not conjugation for shifted equations. General inverse cases were stubs;
the Order different shortcut used the defining-polynomial derivative even when its
power basis was not the maximal order. The field different itself was a stub.
Ideal multiplication accumulated every pairwise generator product and exponentiation
lost exponent-one object identity; repeated nonprincipal squaring expanded generator
lists exponentially. These operations now use PARI's ideal HNF products and trace-dual
inversion, cached field ideal metadata, and Sage's generic positive-power schedule.
The bridge keeps original integral-basis coordinates and rational denominators.

The 2,080 new Sage comparisons cover degrees one through sixteen, shifted and scaled
equations, nonmonogenic maximal orders, fractional/redundant generators, zero ideals,
positive and negative powers, bigint/Integer dispatch, and cached result identity.
Of 2,050 bounded prepatch cases, 866 differed: 278 wrong-behavior records and 588
NotImplementedError records. Thirty large nonprincipal positive powers are new native
controls; they were excluded from prepatch execution to avoid the old exponential
generator expansion. These counts distinguish missing implementation from bugs and
do not count intermediate prototype errors as original discrepancies.

Another 1,494 comparisons run the bundled PARI C sources directly: 350 word matrix
adjoints, 300 integer-matrix inverses, 384 half-GCD reductions, 432 rational lifts and
28 integral HNF triangular solves. They include rectangular and singular matrices,
CUP recursion thresholds, word primes near 2^64, 4,096-bit matrix entries, and
16,384-bit half-GCD inputs around the native 66-word recursive cutoff. All helper
outputs match, including native zero-denominator singular inverse results. New
backend helpers have no original implementation to count as a discrepancy.

The higher-degree smooth-part ideal-approximation specialization remains open:
base4 currently uses PARI's certified randomized two-generator route there. HNFs
match through degree sixteen; selected generators, RNG consumption and specialized
performance are not claimed equivalent. Exact Hadamard bounds, expanded Lehmer
basecase batches, matrix orientation adapters and ambient-order conveniences are
explicit in DEVIATIONS.md. Ideal integral_basis/free_module and gens_two still have
older shortcuts and are the next audit targets.

Fresh full-area results are 10,674 number-field and 2,419 matrix-extended records,
with both prior canonical prefixes preserved. Twelve new unit regressions and one
executed LLM example pass. Frozen production validation: 76,499 focused tests /

## Ideal bases and two-generator representations — 2026-09-12

The old integral_basis multiplied principal generators by the equation power basis,
which can span a proper suborder of the maximal order. Its two-generator quadratic
shortcut could return a nonbasis, and general nonprincipal ideals were stubs.
free_module inherited those defects and did not cache its result. Zero ideals
returned zero vectors instead of an empty basis. All three basis paths now map the
ideal HNF through the maximal integral basis; free_module caches its existing record.

The old gens_two selected the first nonrational input without checking that it and
the chosen integer regenerated the ideal. Its bigint first entry lost fractional
QQ intersection generators, neither entry pair nor cache behavior matched Sage,
and rational ideals had a nonzero second entry. It now delegates to PARI's two-element
HNF reduction, handles rational/zero ideals specially, and returns a cached tuple
of two ambient field elements. The return-type correction is breaking, so all
workspace versions move to 22.0.0; LLM.md includes the migration and an executed
fractional example. Cached tuple mutability and free-module record limitations are
explicit in DEVIATIONS.md, alongside the existing higher-degree generator-search gap.

The 1,782 initial comparative cases expose 1,556 prepatch mismatches: 814 wrong
behaviors and 742 missing-implementation records. They cover equation scaling,
nonmonogenic maximal orders, zero/rational/fractional/redundant generators, degrees
one through eight, exact basis rank and rational lattice, regenerated ideal equality,
field parents, zero second-generator predicate and cached tuple identity. Another
96 principal-ideal cases expose the unchanged free_module cache defect after the
basis fix; all were executable in the original implementation too. Thus 1,878 new
cases cover 910 wrong-behavior and 742 missing-implementation records. Early zero-basis
probes let the fixture HNF helper reject zero rows; the final oracle instead records
basis length and removes zero rows before canonicalization, preserving the actual
rank defect without confusing a harness exception with a port exception.

All 12,552 full-area records match a fresh Sage run, preserving the previous 10,674
canonical records. Six focused regressions and one executed LLM example pass.
Frozen validation: 75,965 focused tests / 88,233 assertions (15.37 seconds),
6,740 fast / 1,864,430 assertions with 32 existing skips (44.12 seconds), and 417
caller tests pass. The unchanged matrix dependency transcript and coverage from
21.0.32 remain valid. Build passes, all 584 normalized type diagnostics are unchanged,
and all 409 production hashes remain frozen. Combined execution: 102483/126020 (81.32%).
The audit remains open: fractional intersections and coprimality are next.


## Fractional ideal arithmetic and zero dispatch — 2026-09-12

The old denominator returned the LCM of generator coefficient denominators instead
of Sage's denominator ideal. Numerator multiplication consequently failed to cancel
common ideal factors, and is_coprime tested only I+J=O_K, which is invalid for general
fractional ideals. Numerator/denominator now cache coprime integral ideals D=(I+O_K)^-1
and N=I*D; fractional coprimality follows Sage's four sums. The first result is fresh
even when the numerator ideal equals the input. The denominator return-type repair
requires version 23.0.0; LLM.md documents migration to smallest_integer() when a
scalar clearing denominator is needed. Four old unit assertions now compare the
actual native denominator ideals instead of the stale scalar API.

Intersection discarded fractional rational generators by truncating them to integers.
It now preserves the existing Dedekind identity with a zero shortcut and no catch
that masks other errors. Its native PARI LLL-kernel route remains an explicit open
architecture item. Division now respects Sage's single-generator field division,
general PARI division and zero-left monoid paths. The PARI helper inverts the divisor
before handling zero dividends. Zero ideals lack fractional-only methods in Sage;
the shared port class raises matching AttributeErrors. Multi-generator zero products
preserve Sage's empty-PARI-matrix conversion failure. Field construction normalizes
empty/all-zero inputs to one zero generator and returns a fresh ideal each time.

The original 6,480 binary/unary cases expose 1,445 mismatches across intersections,
coprimality, divisibility, division, numerator/denominator values and cache identity.
Another 1,215 product cases expose 60 old zero-dispatch errors. The 180 construction
cases expose 90 empty/all-zero generator errors. These 1,595 discrepancies are all
wrong behaviors, not missing-implementation records. An additional 1,215 direct
Sage/PARI idealdiv comparisons exercise the new backend on zero and nonzero rational
HNFs; the helper has no prepatch implementation to count as a discrepancy. Together
9,090 new records cover fifteen field presentations through degree six, fractional
and scaled equations, redundant generators, ideal lattices, errors and result reuse.
The original multiply baseline was replayed in an isolated worktree at 8c5e116.
A temporary constructor prototype incorrectly cached zero ideals; the native identity
comparison rejected it, and it is not counted as an original bug. The final combined
numerator/denominator fixture evaluates numerator first, matching Python expression
order, so zero-ideal failures reflect the implementation rather than an adapter error.

All 21,642 full-area records match a fresh Sage run and preserve the previous 12,552
canonical prefix. Eight new unit regressions and an executed LLM example pass.

tests / 161,917 assertions (54.47 seconds). Build passes; all 584 normalized type

102507/126010 (81.35%). The audit remains open; PARI LLL dependencies for intersection are next.

build with contraction disabled matched all 1,594. The normal native oracle was

products, fully fused square loops and a separate final diagonal product. Explicit
software FMA and those boundaries reproduce every completed probe under both -O2
and the bundled build's -O3 profile. The new binary64 helpers also handle exact
subnormal rounding and exceptional values; scalar comparisons caught the distinction
between an infinite addend and an overflowing finite product. These are dependency
implementation/prototype findings, not inflated counts of pre-existing port bugs.

The permanent stage suite adds 376 completed probes with dimensions through 17 and

join the separate 94-probe inventory; none are counted as successful comparisons.
A further 3,375 direct native comparisons test FMA, ldexp and frexp bit patterns,

NaN payloads are normalized while all non-NaN bits remain exact. The live stage
oracle includes the unmodified original lll.c, records compiler identity in its cache
key and rejects non-AArch64/non-Clang builds. Cross-profile transform equivalence
