## PARI DPE LLL stage — 2026-09-12

The next native LLL dependency follows lll.c:1430-2022: normalized double significands
with separate signed-word exponents, iterative Babai reduction, exact Gram updates,
deep insertion and returned squared norms. BigInt exponents retain the -LONG_MAX zero
sentinel and its adjacent values. The pure column adapter supports a supplied or
incremental Gram matrix, absent B, optional identity U and native real norm records.
It returns partial G/B/U on failure; incremental failure returns G=null. Only PARI's
updated Gram triangle is authoritative, and stale entries in the other triangle are
preserved. Integer/real conversions delegate to the existing native primitive ports.

The first 2,520 exploratory probes produce 2,433 matching ordinary value results,
18 native errors and 69 native timeouts. Nine errors arise from dbltor of NaN/Infinity
when norms are requested; native cypari2 was run directly to confirm its error name
and punctuation. The other nine request temporary storage with shifts near LONG_MAX.
The first prototype returned status -1 in six of those cases because a zero multiplier
avoided an allocation; three instead hit the JavaScript BigInt allocation limit.
A checked unrepresentable-shift guard now raises RangeError before allocation.
This is an explicit resource-boundary deviation, not exact reproduction of PARI's
configurable stack, error class or diagnostic. A separate comparison dispatcher maps
only reached native stack failures and the port's specific guard to resource_failure;
these nine records are reported separately from exact comparisons. They are prototype
findings in a newly added dependency, not counts of pre-existing port bugs.

An additional 1,152 native probes cover optional transforms, four delta/eta pairs and
larger dimensions. Of these, 1,146 return matching values and six exceed the native
two-second exploratory bound. The permanent suite therefore adds 3,588 exact records
(including nine ordinary error records) and nine resource-boundary controls. The
75 timeout inputs and nine raw native stack diagnostics are retained in a separate
inventory; timeouts are not passing comparisons. Coefficients reach 4,096 bits and
dimensions reach 17. Twenty-five captured native fixtures verify state, input copying,
B_original*U=B_result and the authoritative Gram triangle; a shape-guard regression
and an executed LLM example complete the unit checks.

## PARI real LLL stages — 2026-09-12

The heuristic and proved arbitrary-precision stages now follow lll.c:1129-1425 and
2025-2314, delegating arithmetic to the audited PARI real kernels. The heuristic
stage preserves independently selected approximate Gram and Gram-Schmidt precision;
the proved stage preserves exact Gram updates, optional basis/transform/norms and
partial failure state. Private helpers retain native mantissa truncation, low-word
rounding wrap and the literal leading-word/zero-tail shortcut. Native assignments
copy into destination precision instead of accidentally introducing operand identity.
New gen3.roundr_safe rounds ties toward positive infinity, allowing precision loss;
abscmprr ignores zero accuracy exponents. The adapters require positive 64-bit-aligned
precision, matching the native adaptive caller's sequence, and document this boundary.

The first exploratory C harness mistakenly passed the older word-count precision ABI.
Bundled PARI 2.18.1 uses bit counts directly. Correcting only that harness eliminates
464 false discrepancies; the wrong-oracle baseline is preserved and these are not
reported as port bugs. Broad and boundary probes then produce 3,024 heuristic and
4,908 proved stage matches, plus 5,406 exact scalar matches. Native divrr-zero errors
retain their real-accuracy payload and cypari2 punctuation. Twelve proved-stage probes
exceed a two-second bound in both implementations and remain in a separate inventory,
not passing comparisons. New dependency prototype findings are not counted as bugs
in pre-existing port behavior.

## PARI real Cholesky and triangular inversion — 2026-09-12

The real-input branches of qfgaussred_positive/RgM_Cholesky (alglin2.c:1566-1623)
and RgM_inv_upper (alglin1.c:2665-2688) now preserve native upper-triangle selection,
row-update order, real square-root precision and backward substitution. The numeric
helpers implement gen1's MpReal/exact-0n branches, including exact zero multiplication,
finite real-zero accuracy and errors at the generic numeric boundary. These typed
adapters cover the real domain used by FLATTER; generic rational/complex matrix
arithmetic remains outside their documented scope.

Of 4,536 initial native probes, nineteen invert large allocated-zero diagonal cells.
In native invr's Newton branch those zeros have undefined mantissa payload: four
probes return zero records and fifteen raise errors. All nineteen are retained in a
separate inventory, not counted as passing comparisons. The existing port guard
remains deterministic and has unit regressions. The remaining 4,517 matrix cases
match. Adding 5,928 direct numeric probes exposes two prototype dispatch mistakes:
54 exact-0/0 cases originate in dvmdii, and 36 unallocated-real-zero inverse errors
include a native real-accuracy payload. Both are corrected and included permanently.
These are new dependency prototype findings, not pre-existing port bug counts.

All 10,445 new records compare exact values or errors. They cover bit precision
through 8,192, dimension through nine, disparate input/working precision, empty and
nonpositive matrices, exact/inexact/allocated zeros and positive/negative shifts.
Thirty-seven captured native fixtures plus empty/shape/precision and deterministic
zero-guard checks yield 39 unit tests. The LLM example executes both matrix interfaces.

Fresh native/TypeScript full-area runs match all 39,896 records and preserve the
29,451-record canonical prefix (including nine older separately normalized DPE
resource-boundary records). Frozen validation: 40,137 focused tests / 40,896 assertions
(170.23 seconds), 6,901 fast / 1,865,477 assertions with 32 existing skips (46.15 seconds),
successful builds and the same 584 normalized type diagnostics. All 414 production
hashes are frozen. Changed files execute 154/154 emitted lines; combined execution
is 103529/127335 (81.30%). Full behavioral coverage remains open. Matrix products/rescaling,
FLATTER, adaptive LLL and ideal-intersection routing are the next dependencies.


## PARI integer/real matrix products and rescaling — 2026-09-12

RgV_dotsquare, RgV_dotproduct, gram_matrix and RgM_mul now follow RgV.c's integer/real
branches. Dot products preserve sequential summation and pointer-identity squaring;
generic matrix products skip exact-zero left entries after the first term. Entirely
integer matrices delegate to the existing ZV.ZM_mul implementation, converting at its
unused-slot-zero interface. Mixed operands retain native mulir and exact-zero behavior.
RgM_rescale_to_int follows polarit2.c:2355-2397, selecting real low-mantissa-bit and
integer exponent bounds before scaling and rounding. It retains negative half-integer
ties and integer rounding in mixed matrices. For all-zero real matrices the native
HIGHEXPOBIT sentinel shift overflows when a zero has negative accuracy; this error is
preserved without attempting a huge shift.

All 5,184 initial probes match the source-based prototype; another 719 boundary probes
match after integration. The 5,903 permanent exact records cover empty/rectangular
matrices, exact/inexact zeros, real precision through 8,192, coefficients through 4,102
bits and integer products through 72 by 72, crossing multiplication backend cutoffs.
Thirty-eight captured native fixtures plus shape/empty checks yield 39 unit tests.
An executed LLM example covers every exported operation. These are new dependencies
for FLATTER, not counts of pre-existing port bugs; generic non-integer/non-real
coefficient domains remain explicitly outside their typed contracts.

Fresh native/TypeScript full-area runs match all 45,799 records and preserve the
39,896-record canonical prefix (including nine older separately normalized DPE
resource-boundary records). Frozen validation: 46,041 focused tests / 46,800 assertions
(171.64 seconds), 6,941 fast / 1,865,605 assertions with 32 existing skips (46.83 seconds),
successful builds and the same 584 normalized type diagnostics. All 415 production
hashes are frozen. Changed files execute 117/117 emitted lines; combined execution
is 103644/127430 (81.33%). Full behavioral coverage remains open. Adaptive Gram-Schmidt, rank,
FLATTER, adaptive LLL and ideal-intersection routing remain the next dependencies.


## PARI adaptive Gram-Schmidt precision — 2026-09-12

The lll.c FLATTER dependencies now preserve drop/potential/spread, upper/lower
condition bounds, required extra precision, the upper conversion shortcut and
adaptive QR/Cholesky. BigInt exponent arithmetic keeps the native integer-zero
HIGHEXPOBIT sentinel distinct. Real zero accuracy contributes to gexpo, and absolute
diagonal comparison delegates to abscmprr. Dynamic routines begin at n+31 bits,
double on failure and otherwise take the maximum of 4/3 growth and the condition
bound, rounding real allocations to whole 64-bit words. They delegate arithmetic
to the previously audited QR/Cholesky implementations.

The first 810 exploratory probes and 420 signed/reversed/boundary cases match exactly.
All 1,230 are registered permanently, with native long statistics encoded as decimal
strings rather than lossy JavaScript numbers. They cover square/tall bases, dimensions
through 17, maximum basis coefficient length 8,198 bits, output real precision through
16,512 bits and statistic exponent shifts through +/-10^9. Thirty-one native fixtures
plus shape/type/unrepresentable-allocation checks yield 32 unit tests. Full rank and
positive definiteness remain native caller preconditions; the precision resource guard
is explicitly documented. These are new dependency tests, not counts of existing bugs.
The previous matrix-product audit's measured coefficient maximum is clarified as
4,102 bits (its generator multiplied a 4,096-bit shift by a small signed coefficient).

Fresh full-area native/TypeScript runs match 47,029 records and preserve the 45,799
canonical prefix, including the nine older separately normalized DPE resource checks.
Frozen validation: 47,265 focused tests / 47,986 assertions (167.53 seconds), 6,974
fast / 1,865,684 assertions with 32 existing skips (47.03 seconds), successful builds
and the same 584 normalized type diagnostics. All 416 production hashes are frozen.
The new helper executes 107/108 emitted lines: the remaining entry is the closing
brace of the Cholesky retry loop, while its retry, update and return statements

Word pivots/solve, integer rank, FLATTER, adaptive LLL and ideal routing remain open.


## PARI modular pivots, rank and rational matrix solves — 2026-09-12

The alglin1.c and Flv.c dependencies now preserve native word-prime Gaussian/CUP
selection, pivot profiles, integer rank retries with exact kernel certificates,
large-prime solve batches, balanced CRT, centered residues and column rational
reconstruction. Integer solves select the same independent rows and certify the
reconstructed numerator/denominator by exact matrix multiplication. Public adapters
retain the established unused slot zero on both axes; shared row helpers have
explicit dimensions. Matrix right-hand sides and rows >= columns are the typed
solve domain. Parallel native workers execute sequentially while preserving batch
sizes and balanced arithmetic. Shape/word-range restrictions are documented.

All 5,418 new shared cases match native PARI (2,802 word and 2,616 integer), including
failed provisional rank certificates, multiple trial primes, singular reductions,
zero-column right-hand sides and rational lifting. Forty-one captured native fixtures
plus adapter guards yield 42 unit tests / 514 assertions, with input preservation
and square-system identities. Native behavior for inconsistent extra RHS rows is
preserved. A separate native probe confirms the original dimension error when the
first trial primes divide every matrix entry; it is not counted as a port bug.
These new dependencies close routing prerequisites, not 5,418 pre-existing bugs.

Fresh full-area runs match all 52,447 records and preserve the 47,029 canonical

## PARI adaptive LLL, FLATTER and shifted products — 2026-09-12

The typed lll.c wrapper now follows the original two-column shortcut, modular rank,
upper/lower/knapsack tuning tables, QR/Cholesky estimates, recursive block overlap,
rank augmentation and stopping statistics. It composes transformations from partial
failed stages, preserves the heuristic retry count and advances proved precision
by 64 bits. Flags, kernel/image pairs and INPLACE behavior are compared exactly.
The qfb module's new row-oriented redimagsl2 entry preserves the native negative-b
Schoenhage behavior; the wrapper transposes it at its column-matrix boundary. The
existing corrected public qfbredsl2 remains unchanged. Threshold cases exercise
both signs around 9,000 bits of excess form size.

Profiling a five-column basis with 50,100-bit shifts exposed an algorithmic porting
error: all four existing stages expanded shifted coefficients before multiplying.
Native PARI multiplies by a word/truncated mantissa and shifts the product. Restoring
that order preserves exact partial states and avoids large dense products. The
captured end-to-end probe fell from 15.227 to 2.135 seconds and still exactly matches
native output. Its permanent comparative unit fixture has a 10-second execution
budget, with a wide margin above the corrected measurement. A separate 864-case
native suite exercises the four stages with signed shifts through 50,100 bits,
basis/Gram inputs, transform/norm options, and real precisions of 64/128 bits.

All 3,935 new shared comparisons match: 2,651 initial wrapper cases, 420 threshold/
shape/real/form cases and 864 direct stage regressions. Wrapper dimensions reach
100; integral Gram entries reach 100,201 bits. Seventy-four native fixtures, three
separately recorded resource controls and adapter guards yield 78 unit tests.
An isolated C probe confirms that native ZM_lll_norms crashes when its two-column
shortcut is asked for norms; that wrapper is not exported. Three rank-deficient
Gram calls time out natively at five seconds because the augmentation loop requires
a null transform that its inner routine does not return on this domain. The port
executes native arithmetic, preserves errors and applies a documented 64-iteration
resource boundary. These are not counted as exact comparison successes. General
mixed exact/inexact Gram coefficients and generic GEN domains remain restricted.

Frozen validation: all 56,382 matrix records match and preserve the 52,447 canonical
prefix; 56,727 focused tests / 68,632 assertions (283.43 seconds); 7,096 fast tests /
1,866,374 assertions with 32 existing skips (48.43 seconds); builds pass; the same
584 normalized type diagnostics remain. All 417 production hashes are frozen.
Fresh coverage replaces whole edited-file records: wrapper 320/322 emitted lines
(the unreachable post-null Gram return and loop delimiter remain counted), stages
817/819 (two existing closing-call delimiters), qfb 1,584/1,651. Combined execution
is 104464/128162 (81.51%); this is not full branch or behavioral coverage. Ideal intersection
routing, coercion, fractional construction and the broader audit continue.


## Ideal intersection and construction — 2026-09-12

The Sage ideal factory now delegates to the fractional constructor, preserves a
same-field fractional ideal's identity, and catches ValueError to construct a fresh
base-class zero ideal. Direct construction flattens the native singleton generator
list boundary, coerces generators through the field, preserves duplicate generators,
and distinguishes empty from all-zero fractional constructor errors. Nonzero
arithmetic results use the fractional class. Base-ideal objects do not take the
fractional identity shortcut; Sage's conversion failure is preserved. Class-aware
string prefixes cover zero and base principal ideals, while full reduced-generator
display remains explicitly incomplete. Foreign abstract-field rational constants
coerce; nonrational elements still require compatible natural embeddings.

The new base4.idealintersect follows bundled base4.c:2534–2559: remove denominators,
cross-scale the integral matrices, request the kernel from ZM_lll, truncate it to
the first ideal's coordinates, multiply, and normalize via modular HNF. The former
Sage-layer I*J/(I+J) shortcut is removed. The typed adapter accepts canonical ideal
HNFs in zero-indexed columns with positive denominators, preserves inputs, and
returns numerator columns with the least common denominator. Backend shape misuse
is separately tested as an adapter RangeError.

Permanent comparative additions total 5,126: 1,110 input/class/arithmetic cases,
3,120 factory/direct-constructor numeric and layout cases, 864 bundled-native
intersection cases, and 32 Sage-layer post-call random-state controls. Native
backend cases reach degree 16, ideal exponents four, 320-bit HNF scaling and
redundant denominator factors beyond 500 bits. Both layers compare the next
128-bit PARI draw. The old Sage-layer intersection also passes the 32 random-state
controls: no RNG discrepancy is claimed. Captured pre-fix outputs differ on 3,478
constructor/input cases (1,018 + 2,460), including input forms previously absent
from the declared TypeScript domain. These are regression counts, not distinct
root causes. Installed Sage and bundled PARI can choose different integral bases;
Sage comparisons therefore normalize exact power-basis lattices. Raw basis-choice
differences were excluded from the bug count.

All 26,768 live records match and retain the 21,642-record canonical prefix.
Frozen validation passes 27,416 focused tests, 7,102 fast tests with 32
existing skips, all package builds and the executed LLM example. The same 584
normalized type diagnostics remain. All 417 production hashes are frozen; edited
files replace entire previous LCOV records. Combined execution is 104535/128028 (81.65%).
The next 220-case Sage probe finds 170 discrepancies in membership, divisibility,
coprimality and addition input handling. Those fixes and full coverage remain open.


## Ideal operator coercion and centered bases — 2026-09-12

Membership now coerces through the ambient field before checking lattice coordinates
and catches only TypeError. This prevents foreign nonrational elements from being
silently interpreted in the wrong field. Exact-length field coefficient vectors
coerce through rational semantics and retain native length/nonfinite errors.
Addition, multiplication, divisibility and coprimality follow their different
factory/ideal identity rules. Division instead follows the monoid coercion path,
including unsupported lists, foreign parents, nonzero native floats, numeric-zero
coercion and Python-int TypeError masking for a base zero ideal.

Bundled base2.c:462–516 revealed an omitted final ZM_hnfcenter call. Centering the
maximal-order basis fixes 24 of 92 direct basis probes, including quadratic half
coefficients and a degree-eight negative-half tie. That last case required the
actual gen3.c:1048 diviiround rule: ties round toward positive infinity, not away
from zero. The new shared helper and centered-HNF adapter are compared directly
with bundled C. The rational idealmul matrix route now removes primitive content,
checks field dimensions, delegates two-generator multiplication and restores
rational content. Native coordinate interpretation for cross-field multi-generator
products is preserved, even where it differs from field-element coercion.

The basis change exposed a bounded-search regression in the existing partial
class-number certificate. It now searches nonnegative and centered HNFs in fixed
power-basis coordinates, retaining the original per-box limit and exact norm and
membership checks. Forty-eight Sage comparisons preserve the covered class-number
and class-group results under equation scaling. This is still a partial certificate,
not an implementation of bnfinit. Two old coprimality tests now construct proper
fractional ideals through the factory; 36 new comparisons separately preserve
Sage's conversion behavior for explicitly constructed base ideals.

Permanent additions: 4,395 initial method/vector cases, 1,944 multiplication/division
cases, 216 foreign-field cases, 2,537 native quotient/HNF/basis/product comparisons,
48 certificate controls and 36 base-ideal coercion controls: 9,176 total. Captured
pre-fix input/operator outputs differ on 5,025 cases; the basis probes add 24.
These are discrepancy records, not distinct root causes. Native kernel tests include
quotients through 8,192 bits, signed half ties and zero divisors, centered HNFs
through dimension 16, integral bases through degree eight, and rational products
through degree five with post-call random-state checks. Existing higher-degree
smooth-part approximation remains an explicit architectural/RNG gap.

Frozen validation: all 35,944 live records match and preserve the 26,768 canonical
prefix. The focused suite passes 36,720 tests; fast passes 7,230 with 32 existing
skips; package builds and API examples pass. The same 584 normalized type diagnostics
remain. All 417 production hashes are frozen; fresh LCOV replaces whole edited-file
records. Combined execution is 104657/128068 (81.72%); full behavioral coverage remains open.

A follow-up native probe already records 84 mismatches in 960 Gram-LLL, HNF and SNF
calls (46, 3 and 35 respectively). Existing buch/qfrep quotient rounding and the
qfrep Gram reducer's missing delegation are under review next. Direct nonzero
base-class ideal methods and the wider audit remain open.


## Native Gram/HNF/SNF and downstream lattice callers — 2026-09-12

The initial 960 bundled-C probes exposed 84 discrepancy records: 46 Gram
transformations, 35 Smith representatives and three HNF transformations. The Gram
wrapper now delegates to ZM_lll with native image/Gram flags, including singular
image widths and indefinite outputs. HNF remove=true now preserves the full
transformation, with the leading kernel columns retained. Smith group generators
use nonnegative residues for diagonal HNFs; general HNFs use native centered
rounding. The word-reduction helper compares 64-bit limb lengths, not coefficient

Boundary coverage adds 1,090 rectangular, rank-zero, 63–257-bit, and custom-delta

Caller review found that algebraic dependencies built a Gram matrix in place of
PARI's direct relation basis. The direct basis route matches all 10,386 existing

Those 17 inputs now also have dedicated shared regressions and unit fixtures.

cannot serve as the oracle for the bundled source version.

the original source's -36 + j + k example replaces an installed-10.3 expectation.
The 847 new shared quaternion checks include exact representatives, error types,
quadratic forms and theta-series callers. Together with the matrix and relation
cases, this adds 2,938 shared comparisons and 2,298 unit/example checks.

Twenty-seven indefinite-form probes exhaust the original 256 MB PARI stack during

Two hundred sixty native fixtures and an executed LLM example retain regressions.

The next audit remains active. A bounded process reproduces the fractional valuation
hang: the old implementation truncates a fractional norm to zero, then repeatedly

## Ideal valuations and prime finalization — 2026-09-12

The old port reversed Sage's public receiver and argument: Sage uses I.valuation(P).
It rejected zero instead of returning infinity, omitted negative fractional valuations,
capped repeated division after 1,001 steps, and truncated norms in its nonprincipal
route. For (a+1)/2 in Q(sqrt(2)), truncation produces zero and the old divisibility
loop never terminates. A second hang comes from prime_below trial-dividing the norm
of an ideal above a 65-bit prime. Both old processes are captured with three-second
bounds; a permanent bounded child test checks the corrected native results. The
prime below P is read directly from P's intersection with ZZ.

Version 24.0.0 corrects the public valuation signature and the Galois ramification
caller. Input validation follows native nonzero, prime and same-field order,
including the bundled Python format-expression TypeError on foreign prime ideals.
Direct base-class ideals remain valid receivers/prime arguments where Sage permits
them. The old implementation differs on 2,201 of 2,240 initial public value rows and
3,560 of 3,570 supported-input rows (5,761 discrepancy records, not distinct bugs).

PARI base4.idealval now removes primitive fractional content and runs the original
idealHNF_val anti-uniformizer multiplication, norm/intersection bounds and limb-size
modular reduction. Base3.ZC_nfval preserves periodic content extraction. Base2's
matrix-route primedec_end retains prefix/suffix intersections, native uniformizer
search, first finite-field dependency and ramification calculation. Exact resultant
norms replace the complex-embedding estimate as a documented arithmetic adapter.
Existing Sage-layer decomposition supplies certified HNFs; its complete relocation
and native Kummer dispatch remain the next dependency work. NfIdealData now retains
the polynomial/basis/denominator/discriminant needed by these routines.

The new shared set has 8,753 cases: 90 exact native prime-data comparisons, 3,570
supported coercions, 3,360 initial value checks, 1,187 larger exponent/vector checks,
96 rational-prime lookups, 360 scaled-polynomial cases and 90 degree-one boundaries.
They cover degrees 1–16, inessential discriminant divisors, split/inert/ramified
primes, principal and multi-generator ideals, 127-bit primes, and exponents beyond
the old cap. Native matrix-route prime generators/e/f/tau match in the same nfbasis
coordinates using nf_NOLLL; public ideal comparisons use canonical power-basis HNF.

One randomly generated zero vector hit native ZC_nfval's nonterminating precondition
violation. That 30-second native timeout and the port's RangeError are retained as a
separate resource control, not an exact match. Public zero ideals correctly produce
infinity. The initial input grid also records 324 pre-existing generator-display
error discrepancies and 210 unsupported string-input discrepancies. Multi-generator
ideal text alone is normalized to its canonical lattice for the supported error
comparisons; the error class and remaining text stay exact. All 534 raw pairs remain
in the permanent audit artifact; the underlying BNF/string gaps are still open.

Validation: 46,555 full native rows plus 90 final degree-one boundaries, all matched,
with the 37,892-record native prefix unchanged. Full replay/callers/examples pass
49,961 tests; the isolated fast suite passes 12,282 with 32 existing skips. There are
2,493 new unit/example checks. Builds and executable API docs pass; 584 baseline
type diagnostics are unchanged. The 418-file production freeze is preserved, and
coverage replaces whole records for all six changed/new production files. Combined
execution is 104754/128227 (81.69%); 556 throw-only and 248 partial unimplemented callables remain.

The next 294 ideal-factorization probes contain 273 discrepancies: 251 missing
successful results, 14 wrong zero-ideal errors through NumberField.factor, and eight
false empty factorizations. In Q(i), (3+4i)/5 has norm exactly one but factors as a
quotient of distinct primes above five; in Q(sqrt(2)), (6/5) has norm 36/25, which
also triggered the old integer-truncation unit shortcut. These original outputs
have been captured for the next permanent comparative tests. The wider audit stays

## Full prime decomposition, factorization and bounded ideal construction — 2026-09-12

Version 24.1.0 replaces the field-factor stub and limited ideal-factor shortcut with
PARI's rational-HNF factorization. The initial 294 comparisons exposed 273 old
mismatches: 251 successful native factorizations rejected by stubs, 14 zero-input
error mismatches and eight false empty factorizations. In Q(i), (3+4i)/5 has norm
one but two prime factors of norm five, with exponents +1 and -1. Neither an exact
norm of one nor a truncated fractional norm identifies the unit ideal. The raw
before/native records are retained in nf-factorization-baseline.json.

Base2 now ports native Dedekind--Kummer, partial-Kummer and Buchmann--Lenstra
splitting, Frobenius radicals, quotient minimal polynomials, prime finalization,
residue-degree limits and prime comparison. Limits skip uniformizer work while
retaining the complete get_LV context. The index-free adapter derives the integral
index from the exact basis determinant; all 140 fallback comparisons match. The
native norm-estimate shortcut retains the previously documented exact resultant
fallback. Base4 implements pr_hnf, coprime idealnumden, primitive/content HNF
factorization, and idealismaximal. Sage e/f methods read cached prime metadata.
Sage factorization uses its own row-wise HNF matrix ordering, separately from
PARI's p/residue-degree/generator order.

The higher-degree expansion found ten failing calls on two degree-16 generator
sets: the old Sage-local HNF elimination grows until Bun throws a BigInt memory
RangeError. The original Sage pari_hnf method instead constructs each principal
HNF then combines them with idealadd. The port now follows that sequence. Base3
zkmultable_inv/capZ delegate to modular ZM_gauss; base4 principal construction uses
the resulting intersection bound for modular HNF. The first captured failure now
completes in an ordinary short invocation. Native results and old errors remain
in nf-ideal-construction-growth.json, and both inputs are permanent comparisons.
No host memory-limit increase or arbitrary search budget was introduced.

Matrix dependencies preserve binary/word/generic dispatch, CUP recursion and pivot
preferences, unreduced independent image columns, supplementary unit columns,
rectangular inverses and prime-modulus HNF. Inputs use the documented packed or
column-array adapters. Native checks compile bundled PARI with its current bit
precision ABI and nf_NOLLL. Nonmonic polynomials use original ZX_to_monic and the
corresponding generator substitution; an initial test-adapter error that passed
nfinit's [nf,c] wrapper directly was corrected before accepting those results.

The 7,137 new shared cases comprise 1,952 matrix comparisons, 840 exact prime-data
checks (including index fallback), 679 initial factor/order/backend checks, 2,145
expanded factorization checks, 330 cache/recomposition/prime-metadata checks and
1,191 HNF/inverse checks. They span degrees 1–16, nonmonic fields, inessential
index divisors, split/inert/ramified primes, fractional and multiple generators,
large exponents and 127-bit primes. Public factor values are canonical power-basis
lattices. For ordered factors, original Sage Factorization.sort and ideal _richcmp_
run with cached HNFs replaced by the same bundled nf_NOLLL matrices used in TS;
this preserves the ordering test without claiming unrelated basis equivalence.
All new cases also have colocated native fixtures; LLM.md's scalar/fractional
factorization example is executed rather than inferred.

rows are freshly generated (5,045 together plus 140 fallback cases). The full area
now has 51,830 comparisons. Matrix replay covers all 60,408 native-backed rows.
The combined area/caller/unit/docs run passes 122,642 tests and 129,039 assertions
in 627 seconds; the final fallback cases are included in the colocated unit run.
The separate fast suite passes 19,420 tests with 32 existing skips and 1,880,949

throw-only stubs and 247 partially unimplemented callables. BNF generator display,

remaining field/MPQS dependencies remain open. The audit continues with field
trace/norm and characteristic/minimal polynomial routing.


## Absolute trace/norm and rational quotient dependencies — 2026-09-12

Version 24.2.0 replaces NumberFieldElement.trace/norm's dense Faddeev--LeVerrier
characteristic-polynomial calculation with the original dedicated routes. Sage's
quadratic element implements direct formulas; the port expresses those in the
original generator's power basis, normalizing the defining equation first. Other
absolute elements delegate to PARI's gtrace/gnorm reduced-polynomial-modulus paths.
Scalar inputs use degree multiplication or powering. RgXQ_trace uses the defining
polynomial derivative, quotient multiplication and leading remainder coefficient;
RgXQ_norm uses the resultant and leading-coefficient correction. QXQ_norm removes
rational content and calls the existing modular integer-resultant backend.

The original rational dependencies are ported as QQ coefficient/denominator
adapters: ZX_rem, QX_mul, QX_ZX_rem, QXQ_mul, QXQ_norm, RgX_rem, RgXQ_mul,
RgXQ_trace, RgXQ_norm and the two polynomial-modulus generic entries. Monic integer
moduli follow the native fast route; other rational moduli use quotient-first
rational elimination. The adapter contracts and remaining non-QQ GEN domains are
explicit in DEVIATIONS.md. Input coefficients are tested before normalization,
including signed denominators, common content and trailing zeros.

Twelve native timing/value cases isolate trace/norm on certified x^n-2 fields at
degrees 32, 64 and 128. The old committed method bodies, installed verbatim on the
prototype in separate children, hit a three-second limit five times: norm(1+a)
at degree 64 and all four degree-128 operations. Degree-64 scalar trace/norm each
took about 2.2 seconds. The repaired values all match Sage, with the slowest whole
cold child taking 0.173 seconds in the captured run. Permanent comparative child
tests retain a three-second bound. Both constructors disable the independent
irreducibility check for these operation-only measurements. Initial constructor-
inclusive results are retained separately in the audit evidence and do not serve
as norm-only timing claims. Default degree-128 constructor validation still took
more than 47 seconds in a separate observed run; that factorization route remains
an active, distinct audit item.

The first adapter experiment had 31 invalid C-oracle outputs for constant-modulus
scalar results. These were rejected, the C scalar serializer was corrected, and
the Python wrapper now parses every JSON result before accepting it. The same
comparison also caught 30 errors in the initial new quadratic formula, caused by
using the original nonmonic defining equation without normalization. All affected
inputs remain permanent regressions. The final fresh native run has all 3,561
rows matching TypeScript, with no accepted partial native output.

The new cases comprise 2,145 PARI rational-kernel comparisons and 1,416 public
trace/norm/alias comparisons. They cover zero/scalar/general elements, rational
and nonmonic equations, degrees through 512 for scalar/linear inputs, nine zero-
divisor errors and twelve degree-one coefficient-list errors. Public wrappers
use check=False on both sides to isolate these operations; several deliberately
unchecked reducible equations compare formal quotient behavior and are not
claimed to define fields. Existing port-only trace aliases compare Sage trace;
relative_norm on absolute fields compares Sage norm. All 3,561 cases also have
colocated native fixtures, plus twelve isolated bound checks and one executed
LLM example (3,574 added unit/example checks).

Validation: the entire 55,391-row number-field transcript matches, preserving the
old 51,830-row prefix. Dependency replay covers 17,428 polynomial/composition/
extension callers and 10,445 native real-linear callers. The combined area,
caller, number-field unit and example run passes 95,430 tests and
101,832 assertions in 462.18s. The separate fast suite passes
22,994 tests with 32 existing skips and 1,884,528 assertions in
114.05s. All package builds and the bundled PARI pol suite pass. The 584
baseline TypeScript diagnostics remain identical after source-line and union-order
normalization. All 419 production files were frozen before final coverage; whole
stale records for the six changed/new files were excluded. Combined execution is
105490/128950 (81.81%), not branch or behavioral completeness. Optional-base trace/norm,
characteristic/minimal-polynomial options and routing, irreducibility and the
remaining repository gaps are still open.


## Trace/norm base arguments and return parents — 2026-09-12

Version 24.3.0 fixes the base arguments that the old TypeScript methods silently
ignored. The initial 420 paired observations contained 276 discrepancies in
values, return parents or errors; the before/native records are retained in
nf-trace-base-baseline.json. For an element of degree greater than one, norm(K)
returns the element of K itself. For degree-one K, Sage deliberately returns the
absolute Rational norm, even when K is the parent. Generic trace(K) returns an
element of the specified field for the own-field and degree-one cases. QQ/null
and omitted bases retain Rational results. The new overloads preserve the existing
no-argument Rational type while exposing those field-valued results accurately.

Sage's quadratic trace override has no optional base argument: even explicit None
raises its exact TypeError. Generic trace/norm allow one argument, rejecting two
with the native message. Invalid primitive inputs fail when the source tries to
read domain(); comparisons cover integers, floats, strings, booleans, lists,
dictionaries and explicit Integer/Rational wrappers. A field whose degree cannot
divide the parent's degree has no embedding, so the port raises Sage's ValueError.
Other number-field bases and morphisms still require the original embedding and
relative-matrix dependencies. The public TypeScript signature does not yet expose
morphisms. This batch does not claim the full relative interface is complete.

The 672 permanent shared/colocated comparisons combine the initial 420 with 252
additional primitive-type checks. They span degrees 1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 6 and 8; zero,
constant and general rational elements; omitted/null/QQ/self/degree-one/impossible
bases; return-parent identity; and argument errors. A separate executed LLM example
checks exact QQ results and own/degree-one result parents (673 added unit/example
checks). The full native-backed number-field area now has 56,063 comparisons,
retaining the 55,391-row old prefix unchanged. All 672 new native rows were freshly
generated and match TypeScript.

Validation: all 66,757 full-area, number-field unit and example checks pass,
with 73,164 assertions in 345.24s. The separate fast suite passes
23,667 tests, with 32 existing skips and 1,885,206 assertions in
120.73s. Package builds pass. All 584 baseline type diagnostics remain unchanged
after source-line and union-order normalization. All 419 production files were
frozen; the stale NumberField source record was excluded before coverage merging.
Combined execution is 105557/129017 (81.82%). The inventory's partially unimplemented count now
also identifies trace/norm's explicit remaining other-field branches, rather than
silently reporting an absolute value for those bases.

Source tracing for the next task confirms that Sage rational irreducibility
checks the primitive integer polynomial and its factorization; integer polynomials
use NTL below degree 30 or above 300 and PARI in between. The local port instead
selects a large prime from a coefficient bound, performs finite-field factorization
and enumerates modular-factor subsets. Constructor probes found both slow cases
and deterministic NotImplementedError results at degrees 200 and above, including
irreducible Eisenstein polynomials accepted by Sage. The audit proceeds through the
native factorization and lattice dependencies, alongside remaining relative
matrices and characteristic/minimal-polynomial routing.

GRAM/INPLACE combination; matching those records does not expand the input contract.

The complete matrix and 56,063-row number-field areas, existing LLL/dependent units,

The separate fast suite passes 25,896 tests, 32 existing skips and

## NTL multifactor Hensel lifting — 2026-09-12

Version 24.10.0 adds MultiLift to the mirrored NTL factor module and mul/sqr to

reduce signed coefficients modulo a sufficiently large power of two, multiply

trade-offs are explicit in DEVIATIONS.md; native FFT/SS dispatch is not claimed.

## NTL word polynomial quotients and powers — 2026-09-13

Version 24.14.0 adds the mirrored lzz_pX.ts module, with zz_pXModulus, build,

The initial adapter constructed reciprocals above degree 32; native construction
uses context-dependent cutovers 45/90/180 plus one. On composite moduli this
caused 135 early errors when native construction, an empty remainder or a zero
power succeeds. Matching the native construction threshold fixes every observed
mismatch. The baseline explicitly identifies these as an uncommitted prototype
regression, rather than an observation of the pre-audit code. All reproductions
are in the permanent shared case list and native unit fixtures.

## NTL deterministic byte streams — 2026-09-13

Version 24.21.0 ports SHA-256, HMAC-SHA256, DeriveKey and the generic RandomStream
backend in ZZ.ts. Historical salsa20_init/core/apply names expose the native
ChaCha20 kernel for direct state comparisons. SHA uses fixed-size streaming
workspace and exact 32-bit round arithmetic. DeriveKey retains the native
HMAC counter construction. Copy construction duplicates current state; assign
updates the existing receiver so aliases retain native copy-assignment behavior.
Nonce resets restart the counter and unsigned conversion wraps modulo 2^64.

Source review found that the draft HMAC processed the full data length while
native sha256_update receives its low 32 bits. The port now retains that conversion.
The permanent suite adds 216 probes around length wraps: the native caller provides
a long length over short backing storage, of which only the low-32-bit prefix is
read. The TypeScript adapter reports the same length on a short typed-array view.
These avoid multi-gigabyte allocations and compare the actual native and ported
conversion paths. They are not claimed to be full-size allocation stress tests.

All 4,038 defined native records match the exact extracted bundled NTL 11.6.0
SHA/HMAC/derivation, ChaCha and stream implementation bodies. The public stream
class is extracted from the bundled header too; primitive types come from installed
NTL 11.4.3. The build verifies generic acceleration and word-width prerequisites
and hashes original source, compiler, version/configuration/machine headers.
Another 28 records verify explicitly documented short-key/state guards for native
pointer preconditions; no native out-of-bounds reads are executed or claimed as
matching exceptions. Every produced byte is compared, including long derivation
counter carries, block/padding boundaries, aliases and repeated chunked reads.

Three storage/identity checks and two executed examples bring additions to 4,071.
Final focused validation passes 5,352 tests and 6,172 assertions in 3.25 seconds.

The rand_stats canonical transcript retains its 5,848-record prefix and adds

## NTL ordered word-factor recovery — 2026-09-13

Version 24.25.0 ports FindRoots, FindRoot, RootEDF, FindFactors, EDFSplit, EDF,
SFCanZass2 and SFCanZass from the bundled NTL 11.6.0 source. These retain native
random splitting, recursive factor order, smaller-factor root selection, balanced
root preimages and baby/giant distinct-degree factorization. They delegate to the
ported composition, minimum-polynomial, polynomial and sampler dependencies.

A baseline run against commit 13ccc28 found 70 mismatches: word-polynomial division
by zero leaked `ZZ_pX: division by zero` from the shared capital-modulus helper,
where bundled word arithmetic emits `zz_pX: division by zero`. The fix translates
that error at the word boundary. All 70 failing records and 20 passing controls
remain in the committed baseline/native fixtures and shared comparative cases.
The rerun matches all 90, including unchanged subsequent stream bytes.

Another 4,463 comparisons match, covering exhaustive small split root sets,
large word moduli, recursive equal-degree families through degree 128, mixed
factor degrees, composite early returns and errors after earlier groups consumed
randomness. The 4,553 records comprise 4,433 defined native comparisons and 120
individually labeled zero-degree adapter guards for native undefined division.

## NTL cold word-context and small-prime state — 2026-09-13

limit. Their captured state survives nested polynomial operations and factor
recovery. Reusing generated tables consumes no new random bytes. Mathematical
operations omitting explicit state retain their earlier warmed-context adapter.

adapter returns [[1,1],[2,1]]. The next stream bytes differ as well. The new shared
state fixes that source-order discrepancy and preserves initialization effects on
error. Negative maxroot and invalid modulus validation still precede cache mutation.

## NTL transposed word projection after quotient rebuilds — 2026-09-13

Version 24.44.0 fixes UpdateMap's use of current public f/b in place of independently
retained FFT representations. A native pilot identified four discrepancies: after
48-to-1 and 100-to-5 degree plain rebuilds, native projection returns [2] where the
port returned [3] and [0,3]. After a failed 48-to-100 nonunit-leading rebuild, native
raises FFT rep mismatch where the port returned [0,3]. A failed 201-to-200 rebuild
returns nonzero coefficients at indices zero and 56, whereas the old port placed
its nonzero value at index one. The permanent baseline records these complete values.
The expanded first 598 traces exposed 322 differing traces and 404 differing steps:
226 traces contain defined native discrepancies, while 96 differ only in explicit
buffer-guard handling. These are trace counts, not independent bug counts.

The port now mirrors RevTofftRep, multiplication by FRep, RevFromfftRep, negation,
shifted RevTofftRep, multiplication by B1/B2, AddExpand and final RevFromfftRep.
Intermediate reconstruction uses the native word CRT helper. Selection depends on
B.UseFFT even after a successful plain F rebuild; empty input retains native checks.
PlainUpdateMap now accepts the defined m=-1 shift when deg(b)=n. Inputs with plain
deg(b)>n would index before an unchecked native Vec and receive an explicit guard,
after native projection-input validation. They are never executed as native UB.

FFTFwd_trans and FFTRev1_trans use the original flipped FFT recurrences through
exchanged root tables and native scaling. BigInt butterflies preserve O(N log N)
work. Lazy root-power tables are keyed by prime-info identity, and lazily reconstructed
forward representations are keyed by retained coefficient-array identity, prime-info
identity and transform size. Builds replace those arrays at native overwrite points.
Returning arrays cannot mutate either cache. The internal transform adapter keeps
the native projection algorithm in the mirrored lzz_pX1 module.

## NTL constant-modulus remainder nontermination — 2026-09-13

Version 24.44.1 fixes the next confirmed discrepancy immediately after the projection
checkpoint. With p=6, build 1+x^48, then attempt a constant [1] modulus. The failed
build retains n=0 and earlier FFT selection. Native rem(x^48,F) constructs a buffer
of length 2*n-1 and throws negative length in vector::SetLength. The port instead
entered a loop with amount=-1, increasing its remaining input length indefinitely.
The fix preserves the native error immediately before that loop. It follows the
existing small/plain dispatch so smaller inputs still return normally or raise the
correct scalar inverse error.

A three-second pilot watchdog first confirmed nontermination and killed/reaped the
owned child. The permanent subprocess comparison then failed against the old code
at its fixed five-second watchdog (5.37s total). After the fix the same 240 native
traces matched in 127ms (250ms including startup), with no timeout or assertion
changes. Final instrumentation repeats one already-validated trace in the parent
only after the entire watched cohort has completed and matched. The test therefore
continues to fail safely if the original infinite loop returns.

The shared native controls vary old plain/FFT moduli, zero/unit/nonunit/reduced
constants, normalized vector degrees immediately below/at/above the native 45/90/180
cutoffs, and one through four FFT primes. They contain 112 successful reductions,
96 uninitialized-modulus errors, 24 negative-buffer errors and eight inverse errors.
Every complete result includes public f/n, FFT roots/reciprocals/inverse powers and
the next 64 bytes. All are native operations, with no substituted undefined-input
guards. Both runners agree and the normalized area case prefix remains unchanged;
the canonical native matrix cache grows from 241,632 to 241,872 records.

Final focused validation passes 18,048 tests and 19,238 assertions
in 30.57s, covering quotient construction/arithmetic, all previous rebuild and

## NTL cached-word multiplier fallback FFT validation — 2026-09-13

Version 24.44.2 fixes another discrepancy found while preparing the arbitrary-modulus
dependency. A failed oversized multiplier build replaces public B.b but retains its
old plain flag. Native cached MulMod then calls ordinary mul before rem; that mul
must still choose its FFT path from operand degrees and validate the transform size.
The port's coefficient-only fallback skipped that check. With p=6, maxroot=9,
F=1+x^200, a previous constant multiplier and a failed rebuild to x^4000, multiplying
x^199 must raise Polynomial too big for FFT. The old port returned a polynomial.
When the operands are 2*x^199 and 3*x^4000 it returned zero, also incorrectly
suppressing the FFT error. These native pilot cases motivated the permanent controls.

The fix uses the original 150/300/500/500 multiplication crossover table for the
ordinary fallback and the existing raw product/square paths. It validates the full
unreduced product length before coefficient arithmetic and reduction. Thus a later
inverse error or missing reciprocal cannot replace the earlier multiplication error.
Existing exact coefficient-product kernels remain unchanged.

The 309 shared native traces cover one through four FFT-prime contexts, operand
and multiplier-degree boundaries, transform lengths immediately on either side of
powers of two, invalid first operands, zero operands, coefficient cancellation,
nonunit-leading modulus rebuilds and recovery. All are defined native operations.
Before the fix, 36 traces differed in 68 operations, including successful values
and error precedence. The permanent unit suite reproduces all 36 failures before
the correction (273 pass, 36 fail, 309 assertions in 22.92s) and passes all 309
unchanged afterward (21.75s). Both live runners match full outputs/errors, queried
multiplier state, public modulus state, FFT tables and following random bytes.
The canonical case prefix is preserved and the normalizer retains all 309 rows;
the native matrix cache grows from 241,872 to 242,181 records.

Final focused validation passes 18,357 tests and 19,547 assertions in 51.61s,
including earlier quotient/rebuild/projection fixtures, the bounded constant-modulus
regression, API examples and canonical formatting checks. Eight builds pass and all
584 normalized baseline type diagnostics are unchanged. The version 24.44.0 broad
run predates this correction; it is not presented as final-source validation.

## Multiplier context reuse and retained FFT coefficients (24.45.1)

The adjacent word-context audit finds the same allocated-prime-count invariant in
`lzz_pX.cpp::fftRep::DoSetSize`. The first **432 native traces expose 200 missing
errors**. The permanent test reports **232 pass / 200 fail** before the repair and
**432 pass / 0 fail** after retaining the allocated count across plain/failed builds.
The guard runs at B2 resizing, after copying the new public polynomial and setting
the FFT flag, before changing B2, B1 or the port's derived coefficient caches.
The error spelling is native lower-case `fftRep: inconsistent use`.

Another **268 word traces** check use after rejected rebuilds, recovery, and
successful same-count rebuilds with identical allocated transform dimensions.
They expose **28 coefficient discrepancies**; the extended permanent test reports
**672 pass / 28 fail** after the count guard alone, then **700 pass / 0 fail** after
the numeric repairs. The corresponding **48 arbitrary-modulus traces** report
**20 pass / 28 fail** before their repair and **48 pass / 0 fail** afterward.

Two numerical assumptions were wrong. First, the packed complete-product kernel
assumes coefficients are normalized under its supplied modulus; retained B1/B2
coefficients can have been built under a different, larger modulus. Complete
products now normalize those operands before packing. Second, an incomplete inverse
FFT must use the original retained FFT-prime residues. Normalizing that cached
representation under the current coefficient modulus before forward transformation
changes the interpolated result. The TFT path now reads the retained coefficients
without that intervening conversion. The discarded complete product formerly
computed before taking the TFT branch is also removed. Native projection controls
match without a projection change.

The context probes keep native prime-row counts, transform dimensions and accessed
prefixes consistent; failed changed-count probes use the preserved old buffers.
They do not read incompatible or undersized native transform arrays. Forty further
word and forty arbitrary-modulus controls cover larger coefficients and multiple
prime rows, including coefficients exceeding individual FFT primes. All match.

Permanent coverage totals **740 word + 88 arbitrary-modulus = 828 native traces**,
all distinct shared rows. Both shared language runners compare every output,
exception, retained public polynomial/flag, prime/root/reciprocal table and following
64 random bytes: **828/828 match**. Canonical matrix_extended now has **234,569

## Word ordinary products, extended GCD and inverse context (24.46.0)

The word inverse entries delegated to `ZZ_pX1.XGCD`, whose crossover schedule
and transform checks differ from the native `lzz_pX1.cpp` implementation. They
also discarded the context captured by quotient powering. Restoring that old
inverse delegation makes **23 of 716 permanent trace tests fail**: 10 initial
traces contain 10 InvMod and 10 InvModStatus discrepancies; 13 additional traces
contain 26 PowerXMod, 26 PowerXPlusAMod and 13 PowerMod discrepancies. These
are missing native FFT-size errors, including after failed modulus construction.
The baseline JSON records operation counts separately from trace counts.

The port now exposes word `mul`, `sqr` and `XGCD`. Ordinary products preserve
alias-to-square dispatch and the strict 150/300/500 degree cutoffs before product
cancellation. Extended GCD follows native XHalfGCD, including the distinct
untruncated extended recursion, equal-degree quotient and final Bezout adjustment,
90/180/350 cutoffs, FFT division and both matrix transforms. Euclidean matrix
updates now use the word ordinary product. Inverses use this word implementation
and accept optional context settings; negative powers retain F.options. Complete
coefficient products continue to use the documented portable NTL product boundary.

The expanded bundled-NTL word state oracle adds operations 16–25 for these entries,
inverse/status, negative powers and GCD. **780 distinct shared/native traces**
cover all four prime counts, degrees across the half-GCD/multiplication/division
cutoffs, large degree gaps through 2254, zero/equal/swapped operands, nonmonic and
composite coefficients, exact Bezout coefficients, common factors, failed builds,
FFT caps and complete products whose leading terms or all coefficients cancel.
Every trace compares results, errors, retained quotient fields, all FFT-prime
information and the next 64 random bytes. The new functions were stubbed first;
missing APIs are not counted as existing inverse bugs.

Validation: the actual shared Python/TypeScript runners match **780/780 complete
records**. Focused callers, native fixtures and documentation checks report
**72,026 pass, zero fail, 73,269 assertions across 18 files (220.98 seconds)**.
This includes one ownership check and two executed API examples. All eight builds
pass and all 584 baseline type diagnostics are unchanged after source-position
normalization. The latest broad run is the recorded 24.45.0 run; it predates these
changes. Current-source LCOV replaces the two edited modules' prior measurements.
The canonical matrix_extended suite has 235,349 configured executions; the local
native transcript cache has 245,288 records. The whole-port audit remains open.

Current word modules execute **506/506** and **454/454** instrumented lines.
Aggregate recorded execution is **111086/135104 (82.22%)**; this is not branch or
behavioral completeness.


## Stateful Hensel lifting and native error order (24.47.0)

MultiLift bypassed the native word factor-tree operations and arbitrary-modulus
quotient/multiplier caches. It also omitted shared state from its integer products.
AdditionalLifting skipped degree-dependent word-context initialization. The repair
uses word mul/XGCD for the minimum-degree tree, stateful ZZX1.mul for integer
products and ZZ_pXModulus/ZZ_pXMultiplier operations in the original Hensel order.
Both quotient moduli are built before either multiplier; the final stage uses
raw products and skips inverse caches. Exact scalar division remains before any
quotient construction. AdditionalLifting normalizes its leading coefficient first,
then uses NextPowerOfTwo(deg(f))+1 for the word context.

The new `ntl_hensel_state` area adapter extracts seven original ZZXFactoring.cpp
bodies and links the complete matching bundled NTL 11.6.0 library. Every trace
starts in a fresh native child, retains its cache across operations and compares
full results/errors, all FFT-prime information and the next 64 random bytes.
A zero initial-context selector is an oracle convention for cold AdditionalLifting
calls. Returned independent arrays preserve the already documented mapping of
native mutable output parameters; native destructive vector clearing is not added.

**362 distinct shared/native traces** cover degrees around 21/22, 45, 90 and 150,
all four ordinary word prime counts, mixed-degree trees through 16 factors,
exponents through 128, cold and prewarmed caches, constants, non-coprime factors,
nonunit coefficient inverses, exact-division errors, invalid native word moduli,
target normalization and early exponent validation. The old implementation differs
on **267 traces / 882 operations**: 198 result/error operations and 702 state
operations, with 18 operations belonging to both categories. Error discrepancies:
42 missing FFT-limit errors, 36 missing constant-quotient errors, 48 incorrect word
context errors and 72 incorrect scalar-inverse messages. The first permanent test
run reports 95 pass / 243 fail; all 24 added scalar-error regressions also fail with
the old source restored. Baseline counts are saved alongside the native fixtures.

The real shared Python and TypeScript runners match **362/362 complete records**.
The canonical matrix_extended suite has 235,711 configured executions; the local
native transcript cache has 245,650 records. All eight builds pass and all 584
baseline type diagnostics remain unchanged. Two executed API examples and one
ownership check cover the added optional state/context arguments.

The full fast tier with coverage reports **223,231 pass, 32 existing skips and
two timeouts**, with 2,085,822 assertions across 343 files (899.87 seconds). Every
new Hensel regression and documentation example passes. The two unchanged tests
pass separately without changed assertions or timeouts: word projection 6282 in

(5-second limit). Their broad-run durations were 31,811.12 ms and 12,580.31 ms.
The preceding word checkpoint's focused run also passed both. This is recorded
as a broad run with two isolated timeout rechecks, not an entirely green broad run.

## FFT-prime coefficient contexts and integer GCD state (24.48.0)

The word polynomial adapter treated an FFT-prime coefficient modulus as an
ordinary CRT context. Native FFTInit selects PrimeCnt=0, NumPrimes=1 and the
chosen prime's own root table. The repair separates algorithm selector from
transform row count, preserves that context through nested calls, directly
reconstructs coefficient residues and restores the sequence crossover at 400.
The explicit fftPrime option validates consistency with p. Native NTL prohibits
nontrivial arithmetic on operands/caches from a different coefficient context;
those undefined inputs are not claimed as supported comparative cases.

Integer GCD previously generated primes without native root-table/random effects
and used arbitrary-modulus extended GCD. It now initializes each FFT prime before
leading-coefficient rejection, delegates to word GCD in the selected FFT context,
and passes shared state to both exact-division certifications. SquareFreeDecomp
retains the same state through all GCD and division stages. Signed content,
balanced CRT, degree-reset/skip rules and coefficient normalization are preserved.
NTL's primitive, positive-leading-coefficient squarefree input contract is explicit.

There are **519 distinct new native/shared traces**: 278 FFT-context traces and
241 integer GCD/squarefree traces. The old implementation differs on **491 traces**:
276 word traces (1,464 operations, including 372 result/error/profile differences)
and 215 integer traces (430 state differences, no coefficient discrepancies).
The permanent pre-fix test run reports 28 pass / 491 fail. Traces cover selected
prime indices through 11, native index errors, quotient/cache rebuilds, products,
projection, GCD/XGCD, inverses, negative powers and sequence boundaries through
481. Integer cases include zeros, signed contents, degrees through 801, 2,000-bit
coefficients, cold/prewarmed caches and repeated operations.

A separate diagnostic build of the original integer GCD and squarefree bodies
preserves all 241 native output/state records. All twelve monitored outcomes occur:
zero-a 16, zero-b 4, skipped prime 96, constant gcd 186, first CRT 520, degree drop
52, degree increase 52, changed CRT 268, stable CRT 544, first certification rejected
20, second certification rejected 4, and certified result 520. These counters
establish execution of the targeted reconstruction branches, not whole-port
branch completeness. They are not installed in production.

The real shared Python and TypeScript runners match **519/519 full records**.
All 843 initial new/regression/documentation tests pass, including constructor
adapter guards, coefficient ownership and two executed API examples. Eight builds
pass and all 584 baseline type diagnostics are unchanged. The canonical
matrix_extended suite has 236,230 configured executions; the local native cache
has 246,169 records.

The broader NTL/docs/case-format run passes **156,758 tests**, zero failures,
161,169 assertions across 50 files in 606.59 seconds. Sage polynomial callers pass

## Number-field automorphism order (24.49.2)

NumberField.automorphisms inherited a string sort from the local conjugate helper.
For unembedded fields, Sage retains PARI's polynomial order (degree first, then
exact coefficients from highest degree down) and swaps the natural embedding
with the first entry. It does not move the identity while shifting other entries.
The port now reconstructs that order in Sage's signed integral model; a negative
original leading coefficient reverses even-index coefficient comparisons. The
local positive-scale arithmetic model and certified conjugate search are retained.
The method's old claim that every non-Galois field has only one automorphism is
also corrected; examples such as Q(2^(1/4)) have two.

The 190 shared cases include degree-one/two controls, cyclic cubics, cyclotomic
fields through degree eight, non-Galois fields, translations, rational scalings,
negative generator scalings and negative defining-equation content. Permanent
pre-fix tests report **96 pass / 94 fail**; every failure permutes an otherwise
correct set of images. The repaired tests pass **190/190** and compare every
coefficient in its exact output position, also checking that the first map is
identity. The complete live Python/TypeScript records match.

Installed Sage 10.3 still uses polredbest in _pari_absolute_structure, unlike the
bundled source's signed leading-coefficient map. Seventeen initial oracle records
differed for that reason, including six installed-Sage degree-one errors. These
were not installed as expected port errors. The final oracle executes the bundled
method directly, applies its beta conversion explicitly to bypass installed
Sage's stale model, and uses the complete bundled PARI galoisconj kernel. All
190 final records agree with the independent installed kernel using the corrected
conversion. The canonical number_fields area contains 56,096 executions and its
local native cache 56,739 records. Distinguished real embeddings, immutable
Sequence/cache semantics and full flag-zero conjugate delegation remain open;
flag four's identity fallback cannot replace the nfroots fallback.

The broader number-field fast files, documentation and case-format checks pass
**11,372 tests / 17,507 assertions** across 25 files in 131.74 seconds, without
changed deadlines or assertions. All eight builds pass and all 584 baseline
type diagnostics are unchanged. Fresh LCOV replaces the complete edited
number_field.ts record: **1544/1661** measured lines. Unchanged files retain their
previous measurements; aggregate execution is **111298/135150 (82.35%)**.
The whole-port audit remains open; line execution is not branch completeness.

### Integer and rational polynomial roots — 24.50.1 (2026-09-14)

Bundled `integer_ring.pyx:1233–1460` uses factorization through degree 100 and
sparse exponent gaps/block GCD above it. The port instead factored coefficient
integers and globally sorted roots. Root factor subproblems now select NTL below
30 or above 300 and PARI otherwise; QQ roots delegate to PARI QX_factor. Sparse
valuation, dense-after-shift, no-gap and block-GCD branches retain Sage's order,
including initial zero and subsequent +1/-1 roots.

The oracle executes the actual bundled integer and generic polynomial methods,
with only Cython declarations and private-parent access adapted to Python.
Installed Sage 10.3's QQ zero ArithmeticError differs from the bundled method's
NotImplementedError; the latter is the comparison target. ZZ retains its distinct
ValueError and exact message.

There are 699 new shared rows, each with distinct arguments and a unique seed.
They cover degrees through 309, coefficients through 297 bits, signed content,
nonmonic and repeated factors, denominators, valuation, gap-size boundaries,
constant/nonconstant block GCD, and roots of modulus greater than one. Eighteen
large-coefficient rows use external 10-second watchdogs; timeouts fail the tests.
The old safe run has 13 mismatches in 573 cases: six zero errors and seven ordered
root permutations. The old linear `x-C`, with `C=(2^127-1)*(2^107-1)`, times out.
These are permanent comparative regressions, checking order, multiplicity,
coefficient classes and errors rather than unordered root sets.

  reports 14,003.42 ms against its 10,000-ms deadline. The exact unchanged test,

  No assertions or deadlines were relaxed; the full run is not reported green.

  recorded lines. Current polynomial-suite/full-fast LCOV union replaces the

### Public integer/rational factorization and irreducibility — 24.50.2 (2026-09-14)

Bundled integer polynomial factorization factors integer content before selecting
NTL below degree 30 or above 300, and PARI otherwise. QQ factorization always
uses PARI QX_factor; QQ irreducibility instead tests the positive-leading primitive
integer numerator through ZZ's degree-selected backend. Public callers now follow
these routes. The previous local FLINT-style factor driver remains reachable only
through its existing internal test hooks.

ZZ constant factors now contain their prime factors, the unit constant one has
an empty factor array, and zero ZZ factorization raises the bundled
ZeroDivisionError('division by zero'). Other supported fields retain ArithmeticError.
ZZ irreducibility counts integer content factors; successful ZZ/QQ predicate
results, including false, are cached per polynomial object as in Sage. A module
WeakMap supports both constructor and Object.create-based instances without
changing the exported surface or structural type diagnostics.

The 1,118 new shared/native cases comprise 814 ordered factor/predicate values
and 304 PARI-state traces. They cover zero, signed constants and content,
nonmonic/repeated polynomials, rational denominators, five base-ring controls,
degree boundaries 29/30/31 and 299/300/301, and 234-bit coefficients. State traces
compare first and repeated cached calls against fresh objects; the bundled C
oracle factors content before the polynomial and records full PARI state.
Native Sage produces the factor and predicate values. The installed Sage 10.3
word-field zero factor behavior differs from bundled Sage, so the oracle executes
the bundled word factor wrapper and delegates only its nonzero backend calls.

The old implementation differs in 14 of 480 safe value cases and 30 of 200 safe
state cases. Selected permanent regressions fail before repair. Degree-above-100
comparisons run in externally watched subprocesses at the unchanged ten-second
deadline; a timeout fails the test and is never an expected native result.
The final 1,118 live TypeScript transcripts match the independent native records.
The canonical polynomial area has 279,843 configured cases and its local native
cache has 281,314 records.

### Finite-field roots, distinct-root callers and native extension comparison — 24.51.0 (2026-09-14)

Bundled finite_field_base.pyx:2182 factors even small-field polynomials when
multiplicities are requested, then extracts linear roots in Factorization order.
The port instead enumerated fields of order at most 10,000 and globally sorted
numeric root representatives. It now uses the factorization route, propagates
the field factor backend's zero error, and preserves factor order. The unused
_linearFactor method and field iterator helper have been removed.

This also exposed an independent extension factor-order bug. Sage's
PARI-backed elements call gen2.c:cmp_universal; the port sorted their base-p
integer representations. The new gen2.ts finite-field adapter compares backend
tags, raw values, defining moduli and characteristics in native order. Raw
coefficients are compared low first after length, with binary coefficients packed
into 64-bit words and native signed long comparison for packed/word values.
Polynomial factor comparison delegates to that adapter. Its exported signature,
input profile and three executed examples are documented in LLM.md.

There are 6,007 new shared/native records: 987 default root lists, 987 full factor
lists, 236 root-state traces, 1,686 distinct-root lists, 211 elliptic division-point
lists and 1,900 direct comparisons against the complete
bundled PARI 2.18 kernel. Values cover both prime-field implementations, GF2,
eight small extension profiles, binary dimensions 63/64/65/127/128/129, prime
characteristics through 89 bits, explicit defining moduli, signed word slots,
zero/constants, nonmonic/repeated factors and inseparable powers. Direct
comparisons include different fields and backend tags, normalized zero padding,
and scalar prime-field representations. Tests check input ownership and root
parent identity. The extension oracle selects the documented PARI element
backend; it does not substitute Givaro's iteration or comparison order.

The finite root oracle executes the actual bundled field method. For word
polynomials it executes the bundled factor wrapper too, preserving its zero
guard that installed Sage 10.3 lacks. The state oracle uses bundled PARI's finite
factorization backend and complete random state; no value sorting hides output
permutations. Every new live TypeScript result agrees with its native record.
The polynomial area now has 285,639 configured cases; its local native cache
has 287,110 records. The elliptic area and its cache each have 262 records.

The old implementation differs on 137 root lists, 20 factor lists and 15 root-
state traces. Thirty-seven root differences are zero errors; the other 100 are
ordered-value differences. Six state traces have identical root values but
expose the skipped factorization call. Selected permanent root and factor tests
fail against the pre-repair implementation. The additional 304 boundary cases
account for 22 old root differences and 14 old factor permutations.

The port also ignored `multiplicities:false`. The new overload returns flat
roots and, over finite fields, factors gcd(f, x^q-x) to discard nonlinear and
repeated factors first. Of the 1,686 new distinct-root cases, the old code differs
on 1,097; 699 cover ZZ/QQ and 987 cover finite fields. Another 118 traces compare
PARI state for the distinct route. The native oracle executes the bundled field
method with Sage's independent modular-power implementation because installed
10.3 predates the bundled wrappers' zero guards and large-exponent handling.
It executes the bundled zero guard for binary/extension fields and native first
reduction for word/large prime fields. This adaptation does not compare ambient
NTL state. All recorded errors are actual zero-polynomial cases.

Elliptic division_points now requests distinct roots, as the bundled caller does.
The existing exhaustive GF(11)/GF(13) caller test exceeded its unchanged five-second
deadline twice before repair and passes in 2.36 seconds afterward. The 211 shared
point-list cases include a degree-5,100 division polynomial (m=101 over GF(7)):
with the corrected factor-based default roots, the old caller exceeds a ten-second
subprocess watch, while the repaired caller takes about 0.10 seconds. The permanent regression enforces that watch;

### Modular polynomial root parents and CRT/Hensel dispatch — 24.52.0 (2026-09-14)

The bundled residue-ring method has a different contract from finite-field
roots. For a nonzero polynomial over Zmod(p), default roots belong to the cached
GF(p) parent. Composite rings reject multiplicities even for constant inputs.
Distinct zero roots enumerate all residues; unit/nonunit linear cases are base
cases. Higher degrees use finite-field distinct roots or factor the modulus,
lift roots through each prime power and combine in CRT Cartesian-product order.
The port formerly used its generic finite-field route or rejected composites.

IntegerModRing now exposes the cached field and factored-order methods, its
root hook and the static residue-field lifting helper. Newton doubles precision;
singular lifting preserves root-major, then digit-major ordering. Polynomial's
modular-coefficient overloads expose the changed default root element class and
retain IntegerMod for distinct roots. The arithmetic delegates to existing
factorization, polynomial and CRT ports.

There are 5,126 new shared native comparisons: 3,990 initial root cases, 216
field-cache cases, 216 factor-cache cases, 464 direct lifts, 238 direct root-hook
cases and two watched large-composite root cases. The initial port differs on
3,132 roots (1,811 default and 1,321 distinct). Native results are generated by
executing the bundled modular/generic/finite-field methods with native Sage
polynomials, including the existing bundled zero-wrapper adapter. Direct hook
cases include null/self/unsupported ring arguments and ignored algorithms.
The two large-composite cases (1000036000099 and 10028029413722401) require
factorization and lifting to fit the permanent ten-second subprocess watches.

Independent mathematical checks found an upstream error: the bundled nonunit
linear branch computes a/g but recurses without dividing f by g. Fifteen cases
return nonroots; for example 2*x+2 modulo 4 gives [0,2] rather than [1,3]. Those
are preserved and explicitly documented for compatibility. All other successful
small-modulus distinct lists match exhaustive root sets. A separate fresh-parent
adapter initializes the native generic parent with its factory category; the
raw generic constructor's missing-factory-metadata TypeError is outside the
port's existing category-free construction profile. No such framework errors
are accepted as comparison expectations.

All 5,126 new live comparisons and the complete 9,199-case modular area match.
The full fast suite passes 239,012 tests (32 existing skips); affected slow
polynomial/elliptic/hyperelliptic callers pass 434 tests. Final focused helper,
root, runner, documentation and case-format checks pass 5,496 tests. Eight
package builds pass. The same 584 primary type errors remain: import discovery
changes union/abbreviated type rendering and one existing union failure's
illustrative candidate, without introducing a new primary diagnostic.

### Weierstrass isomorphism root equations — 24.52.1 (2026-09-14)

The generator used local square/cube/fourth/sixth-root routines based on the
characteristic rather than the field order. Its characteristic-two j=0 branch
also solved a quadratic where the bundled method requires a quartic. Extension
root sorting compared strings, some returned tuples failed the curve
transformation equation, and QQ calls failed with BigInt/type errors.

Every branch now constructs the source polynomial and delegates to distinct
polynomial roots, preserving native order without a secondary sort. The source
elliptic-curve argument guard is restored. The obsolete local root helpers are
removed. This fixes 479 of 1,632 initial finite-field comparisons (including six
invalid tuples), 74 of 80 QQ comparisons and all 24 invalid-argument cases.
One additional valid-argument control and 22 boundary cases give 1,759 new
shared comparisons. Boundaries include 31/61/89-bit prime fields and GF(256),
with ten-second subprocess watches. All 1,759 match live native records, as do
32 existing Weierstrass comparisons.

The oracle executes the bundled generator with native curves and polynomial

An initial unit replay rounded six large transformation inputs when parsing

356/422 (84.36%). The edited file's LCOV is replaced from the final passing caller
