### Generic curve isomorphism ordering and parents — 24.52.2 (2026-09-14)

The generic curve APIs duplicated the obsolete generator and sorted scalar
values by residue or string. They now share the repaired Weierstrass generator;
first-isomorphism and boolean queries stop after its first result. Sorted lists
and automorphisms use the bundled morphism comparison key (identity first,
then the smaller of a morphism and its negative, sign preference, and tuple).
QQ comparison uses rational order and PARI extension comparison delegates to
cmp_universal. Six operators, unequal-domain/codomain errors and unsupported
operand NotImplemented/null behavior follow the bundled comparison body.

Curve equality now checks parent definitions before comparing coefficients.
Supported equivalent prime-field implementations coerce into a common parent;
extensions require matching characteristic, degree, name and modulus. Custom
prime generators distinguish native linear-modulus parents, while explicit
x-1 agrees with the default generator. Noncurve is_isomorphic inputs return
False. Global factory metadata, optional common-field embeddings and unknown
field comparison remain open fidelity gaps, explicitly documented.

### Extension scalar square roots — 24.53.0 (2026-09-14)

Extension elements lacked sqrt. The added method follows the bundled
pari_ffelt wrapper: extend=false by default, unsupported extend=true, all=true
lists in [r,-r] order, one root for zero/binary fields, and exact nonsquare and
unknown-keyword errors. The method delegates to a PARI FF_issquareall adapter
with its root output requested; null represents the native failed-root status.

The dependency specializes FF_ispower to n=2. Odd extensions use the bundled
Doliskani–Schost algorithm with native random polynomials, Frobenius composition
and logarithmic sumautsum folding. Binary fields use the generic group
inverse-exponent power; linear fields retain the Shanks Sylow search. Existing
PARI multiplication, inversion, composition and scalar-square-root ports supply
the arithmetic. No prime-subfield nonresidue enumeration or polynomial root
factorization is substituted. Word and large-prime backends retain their own
sampling and comparison shortcuts.

The first implementation incorrectly short-circuited word-extension sqrt(1).
Fifteen initial native comparisons caught changed random state and, sometimes,
changed root sign. The source uses gequal1 on a word polynomial stored as
VECSMALL, which is false even for one; its randomized path is now preserved.
Large-prime polynomial-one and binary/linear field-specific shortcuts remain.
The permanent comparisons include those cases and full retained PARI state.

There are 4,324 distinct shared comparisons: 1,398 dependency calls and 2,926
extension API calls. Removing the newly added method from the otherwise current
prototype reproduces all 2,926 old API failures. The C oracle calls the bundled
FF_issquareall directly; Python also executes actual Sage sqrt option errors,
checks successful root sets and parent identity, and uses the bundled C result
for version-specific root order and state. Profiles include all small-field
elements, odd extensions through degree 17, binary degrees 65 and 130, word
primes on both sides of 2^63 and 89-bit primes. Four subprocess watches enforce
a ten-second boundary; an ownership test checks roots and input preservation.

### Generic curve coordinate roots — 24.54.1 (2026-09-14)

The curve-local square-root helper searched only prime-subfield constants for
a nonresidue. Every such nonzero constant is a square in an even-degree
extension, so the search failed or ran up to the full field size. Its field-size
assumptions also failed over QQ. The curve now calls the coefficient field's
square predicate and sqrt(all=True), with Sage's predicate guard. QQ and PARI
extension y-coordinate sorting follows native scalar order instead of strings.
Characteristic-two lift_x requests distinct polynomial roots; is_x_coord retains
the source's default multiplicity mode. Montgomery construction keeps both
native distinct-root lists, evaluates all candidate predicates and selects the
first maximum. The obsolete local field-size/Tonelli code is removed.

Comparisons additionally exposed missing _coeff_repr parentheses and whitespace
handling for extension coefficients. The curve equation now preserves scalar
coefficient hooks and follows Element's non-atomic extension formatting. Both
direct representations and error messages are compared.

There are 5,822 new shared comparisons: 5,461 initial caller cases, 240 large
boundary cases, 115 direct representations and six Curve25519 Montgomery cases.
The initial old implementation differs on 1,537 values/errors and 5,258 call
traces; these overlap, with 5,274 differing records overall. After the first
arithmetic/ordering fixes, 201 initial and one boundary error-message differences
identified the coefficient representation issue. All new comparisons match.
Small-field profiles enumerate coordinates across general and special forms;
QQ includes negative and fractional coordinates. Boundaries include 61/89-bit
primes, primes above 2^63, degree-17 odd extensions and degree-130 binary fields.
Subprocess watches cover large-field lifting and Curve25519 model construction.

The Python oracle executes the bundled generic method bodies. Scalar dependency
calls are instrumented through AST binding replacements without changing caller
branches or arithmetic. Polynomial calls use the previously verified bundled
root dispatcher; extension scalar roots/predicates use bundled PARI C oracles.
Native Sage confirms root sets. The comparisons include point parent identity,
exact errors, dependency arguments/multiplicity modes and odd-extension scalar
random state. Polynomial dependency streams retain their separate state oracles.

The first broader run caught two assertions outside the new fixtures. The API
example had selected the separate prime-field curve factory, while the repaired

algorithm selects -486662 from its distinct-root order. That assertion now

The final broader field/elliptic/hyperelliptic/docs/case-format run passes

All 10,202 advanced-area comparisons match native records. Eight builds pass.

## 2026-09-18: lift_x parent promotion and base change (24.57.2)

rational_field.py:344. Canonical parent maps, rather than individual successful
conversions, decide which field receives the coordinate and the curve. In

is constant. Isomorphic differently named explicit fields do not acquire an

called elllift_x, ignored extend, and could not promote coordinates. Share the
existing generic implementation through a cached short-model curve. Adapt points
back to the optimized class when their curve is unchanged; retain generic points
when promoted or extended. Public overloads and examples expose both result APIs.

controls explicitly; no production behavior or comparison assertion was weakened.

wrapped integers, parent promotion, extend, and dependency-root calls. The trace
profile varies primes, coefficients, coordinates, operations and seeds, comparing
native scalar predicate/root calls as well as values, parents and errors. All
oracles execute the same bundled generic Sage method bodies used by its subclasses.

and the existing native polynomial dispatch. Scalar extension roots use bundled

only dependency bindings, traces top-level calls and supplies the native module
namespace for relative imports. Nested calls inside dependencies are deliberately
outside the caller trace and have their own dependency tests.

## 2026-09-29: finite-extension trace dependency routing (24.60.0)

Read element_base.pyx:632-687, element_pari_ffelt.pyx:982-1003, FF.c:983-1042,
FpX.c:3028-3051, Flx.c:3848-3870 and F2x.c:1212-1218. The public trace calls
FF_trace, which dispatches to binary, word or arbitrary-prime kernels. These
multiply the input by the modulus derivative, reduce in the quotient and extract
one coefficient, rather than accumulating Frobenius conjugates. Port those
kernels through the existing native polynomial arithmetic and restore the existing
prime-subfield parent on the Sage-facing result.

The low-level profile calls bundled PARI through the existing compiled helper.
Domains include p=2, 3, 5, 17, 65537 and 2^127-1, degree 1 through 129, zero,
nonmonic moduli and quotient-reduction crossover sizes. Direct FpXQ_trace keeps
native derivative-degree indexing; Flxq_trace indexes from the modulus degree.
The distinction matters in small characteristic and is tested, not normalized away.
The FF wrapper selects the native backend and also covers scalar record encoding.

The caller oracle executes the bundled Python-compatible trace method body with
an observed native PARI trace binding, then independently checks its result against
Sage. A port export spy checks the FF_trace call, not just numerical equality.
With the low-level kernels in place but the old public method retained, 300/300
kernel comparisons passed and all 303 caller comparisons failed due to missing
backend delegation (seed 290929). After the caller repair, all 603 passed.
Eight compact rows preserve caller routing and derivative-index, nonmonic, binary
and zero controls; all other inputs are freshly generated. No corpus is retained.

Final validation:
- 908/908 live kernel/caller/scalar-record comparisons, seed 3002380440, runs 300.
- 538 existing extension, native field-predicate, executed-doc and case/storage
  tests pass; native predicate seed 3446416018, runs 8.
- Final canonicalization checks pass after expanding the scalar-record generator.
- Eight builds pass. All 570 full TypeScript diagnostic messages and continuations
  match the compressed baseline after normalizing line/column positions.

Norm remains open. Sage extracts the signed charpoly constant coefficient, while
our public method multiplies Frobenius conjugates. Native FF_charpoly dispatches
to bivariate resultants; the existing ffinit.ts implementation instead uses a
monic-only multiplication matrix/Bareiss determinant. Read polarit3.c:1917-1987
and record its interpolation/subresultant branches as prerequisites. Correct the
existing source comment that claimed exact native algorithm fidelity. Do not route
norm directly through FF_norm or the current determinant and call that complete.
No new whole-repository coverage measurement or full-equivalence claim is made.


## 2026-09-29: finite-extension norm and characteristic polynomials (24.61.0)

Read element_base.pyx:632-660, element_pari_ffelt.pyx:982-1003, FF.c:1026-1042,
FpX.c:1765-1851/3040, Flx.c:2639-2695/2769-2864/3861, and
polarit3.c:1687-1987. Replace public norm's Frobenius product with the signed
constant coefficient of charpoly('x'). Add charpoly through FF_charpoly, using
Flxq_charpoly for binary/word fields and FpXQ_charpoly for arbitrary primes.

The previous bivariate resultant was a monic-only multiplication-matrix/Bareiss
implementation. Replace it with native evaluation/interpolation below the word
characteristic bound and polynomial pseudo-remainder/subresultants otherwise.
Preserve degree-drop leading-coefficient corrections, sign changes, skipped-degree
scaling and Lazard's exact-division schedule. Arbitrary primes interpolate.
Product/remainder trees and batch inversion implement native interpolation.
The existing outer-eliminated-variable coefficient layout is retained explicitly.

Comparisons invoke the bundled native C kernels. The public norm oracle executes
Sage's bundled norm body with an observed native charpoly binding and checks its
value independently in Sage. With the old norm retained, its result agreed but
its calls were [] versus ['charpoly:x', 'FF_charpoly']; the retained regression
now checks that architecture as well as values, parents, variables and random state.

The expanded profile found an additional boundary: native degree-one binary
charpoly samples coincident interpolation points and raises Fl_inv. Shared generic
inversion initially reported Fp_inv and a different payload. Preserve the word
inverse backend and exact native error, retaining the minimal scalar-zero row.
This is compatibility with native behavior, not an invented mathematical restriction
on Sage's public extension fields (whose tested degree is at least two).

Twelve compact rows cover caller routing, odd/even/zero/constant elements, nonmonic
and degree-drop resultants, subresultant boundaries and native binary failure.
Fresh generators cover p=2,3,5,7,17,2^127-1, resultant degrees through nine,
interpolation sizes through sixteen, scalar records and ordinary extensions.
No generated input/output corpus is retained. FpX_composedsum's distinct native
Newton/Laplace algorithm remains open and documented.

This is an explicit intermediate architecture step, not completed finite-curve
integration: five-coefficient results use generic point x()/y() rather than the old
optimized coordinate properties. The major version and public docs identify that
runtime compatibility change. General finite-field PARI cardinality/order/group

Fresh recipes exercise QQ, prime fields and explicit extensions with bigint,

## Hybrid point order and bounded factorization — 25.3.0

Source: ell_point.py:794/4873, integer.pyx:4127, factorint.pyx:251,
factorization.py:1437 and ell_finite_field.py:316/670. Hybrid previously aliased
unbounded generic_small and additive_order discarded algorithm options. Port the
native bounded-search schedule and extract _compute_order, retaining outer point
caching. Add default prime-curve cardinality/order/cardinality_pari as the required
curve dependency; cardinality_pari leaves the Sage-facing cache unset.

Integer.factor({limit}) delegates to the new mirrored factor_trial_division,
which reuses trial_division and GMP mpz_remove. C-long bounds and unit handling
are explicit. Completeness checks test each factor base for primality or unit
status, equivalent to the bundled Factorization body, while retaining the existing
factor array API. The free factor(n) remains unchanged.

Live comparisons execute bundled base and finite _compute_order bodies with real
bounded group searches, integer factorization and bundled PARI calls. Native
factor-completeness code is bound onto the older installed Sage factorization
container (which lacks that method). The existing homset-zero binding permits
bundled generic group code to operate on installed Sage points. No search outcome
or threshold is simulated. The cardinality cache probe resets factory-shared Sage
curve state before starting, so earlier tests cannot contaminate it.

A real point on y^2=x^3+8*x+1 over GF(1000000007) has order 499981897. Its curve
cardinality is 999963794 = 2*12011*41627. Bounded searches cover 1..1024,
1025..16384, 16385..262144 and 262145..4194304. At trial bound 8192 the unresolved
cofactor is composite, so another search covers 4194305..67108864. Bound 32768
then completes the factorization and triggers PARI. Compact rows retain this
case, a direct first-factorization transition, pre-cached curve/point controls,
zero, the additive alias, factor boundaries/errors and cardinality cache behavior.

- Eight builds pass. All 568 full type diagnostics and continuation messages are

The optimized override invokes the generic point prototype for the two inherited
algorithm branches; those branches use only their common group protocol and
curve.order(). Hybrid's later _compute_order('pari') dispatches to the optimized
override. The generic implementation keeps its pre-existing unsupported-parent
default behavior explicit; moving the branches does not remove that fallback.
Neither coordinate representations nor the optimized short PARI backend changes.

caller traces for default/PARI/generic_small/hybrid, unknown options, point/curve

from 25.3.0 also runs through the optimized class. No expected-output corpus is

## Optimized scalar coercion, orders and native PARI adapter — 25.5.0

Source: ell_point.py:4402 and elliptic.c:2289/2306. Optimized point multiplication
called FpE_mul directly, accepted only bigint, discarded cached point orders and
returned infinity before checking the scalar. It now coerces through ZZ, invokes
pari.ellmul and propagates order/gcd(order,k) to a fresh result. Thus fractional
scalars also fail on infinity, and zero multiplication of a known-order point
retains the native order-one cache.

The new pari_curve method aliases the existing optimized short record cache;
__pari__/toPari retain their representation and identity. PARI ellmul adapts these
records (implicit zero a1/a2/a3) to ellinit for p > 3 before using the already
compared general-model coordinate conversion and FpE_mul. A WeakMap caches the
invariants and checks all coefficient/characteristic fields on reuse because
short records are mutable. No public record-type migration is required.

Eight compact rows and fresh recipes cover wrapped/integral numeric inputs,
fractional errors, positive/negative/zero scalars, infinity, cached orders and
all three prime-parent representations. Direct bundled PARI comparisons exercise
80-bit multipliers, 127-bit primes, repeated cached calls and a changed coefficient
on the same short record. The live scalar caller still executes the bundled Sage
body with observed PARI dependency arguments; successful runs store no transcript.

  run passes 1,628 (1627952395/runs=200), plus hybrid 823 (3164371840/runs=200)

- Final 508 comparisons pass (3569013982/runs=60) with the result-union narrowing

Source inspection: elliptic.c:779 routes prime characteristics 2/3 through

conversion. None of those elliptic extension kernels were present. Port the
binary dependencies first rather than making Sage call its local generic scalar
algorithm under a PARI name.

F2xqE.ts now mirrors F2xqE.c:43–247: changes of coordinates, addition, doubling,
negation, subtraction and signed scalar multiplication for ordinary and
supersingular models. Scalar calls reuse the existing native gen_pow_i schedule

## September 30 — binary model initialization and scalar callers (26.0.0)

The binary point kernels were implemented, but generic Sage multiplication still
used local double-and-add and binary pari_curve conversion was unavailable.
Ported ff.c:1200's ordinary/supersingular model conversion, ff.c:1363's FF_ellinit,
and ff.c:1466's FF_ellmul, together with the five-coefficient ellinit_Fq path
(elliptic.c:798 / initsmall5). The low-level initializer preserves precomputed
invariants and permits singular curves with j=0; ellinit_Fq returns null for a
singular model. Native ellmul dispatch uses the binary adapter and retains the
infinity shortcut.

FFEllipticCurve has field-valued invariants, a field descriptor and the packed
binaryModel tuple. Integer coefficients/coordinates mean prime-field constants;
packed polynomial integers never cross that boundary implicitly. Generic curves
cache this separate record for characteristic-two PrimeField/FiniteFieldPrime,
prime IntegerModRing, GF2Field and explicit FiniteFieldExtension parents. Scalar
results use the original model/parents, ZZ coercion and known-order propagation.
The public generic pari_curve/__pari__ return type is now a union; prime-only
consumers must narrow it. This TypeScript change warrants the 26.0.0 major bump;
all workspace versions and the lockfile are synchronized.

The persistent native oracle now calls bundled ellinit/FF_ellinit/ellmul/FF_ellmul
and compares all thirteen invariants, the native model and original-coordinate
results. Ordinary and supersingular generators cover GF(2), GF(4), GF(8), GF(16),
GF(32) and GF(256). Valid-curve generators solve a6 from the supplied point;
singular outcomes are compared explicitly. Binary callers execute the bundled
Sage _acted_upon_ AST against Sage parents/PARI, checking dependency arguments,
returned parents, cached model aliases, scalar errors and known point orders.
Seventeen compact rows cover model changes, singular records, negative integers,
infinity, scalar coercion and prime-parent families. No generated corpus is stored.

## September 30 — odd-extension and characteristic-three scalar backends (27.0.0)

The odd extension kernels were absent and their Sage callers still used local
scalar arithmetic. FlxqE.c:40–246 and FpE.c:1519–1686 now have mirrored exports for
addition, doubling, negation, subtraction, multiplication and coordinate changes.
Shared affine formulas select the existing word/arbitrary-prime polynomial
implementations, preserve native inverse errors and use gen_pow_i's exact window
schedule. FlxqE retains its separate characteristic-three ordinary `[a2]` model
and doubling slope; this must not be treated as short-model a4.

Ported ff.c:1145–1197's ternary ordinary/supersingular and larger odd conversion,
FF_ellinit/FF_ellmul dispatch and the five-coefficient ellinit_Fq invariant path.
Sage general models over characteristic three and explicit odd finite extensions
now cache their PARI record and delegate scalar multiplication, restoring the
original coordinates and parents and propagating known orders after ZZ coercion.
Binary model behavior remains covered. FFEllipticCurve is now a union of binary
and odd records; native model properties require narrowing, a breaking TypeScript
change reflected by 27.0.0 across all workspace versions and the lockfile.

The direct C oracle invokes bundled FlxqE/FpXQE, ellinit, FF_ellinit, ellmul and
FF_ellmul. It compares all thirteen invariants, the converted model and both
scalar entry points. Tests include degree-one fields, quadratic extensions,
cubic characteristic-three extensions, near-64-bit word primes and 127-bit
primes; scalar recipes cross 64/160/384/896-bit thresholds. Model generators solve
a6 from a point and force b2=0 for ternary supersingular coverage. The source
Sage scalar method is executed via the bundled AST to compare dependency
arguments, caches, parents, coercion errors and known-order propagation.
Forty-three compact controls and fresh shared generators are stored; compiled
oracles, transcripts and logs remain in OS temporary storage.

Validation:
- Initial kernel comparison: 190 checks (2159228023/runs=40). Expanded kernel
  stage: 817 odd plus 1,627 binary checks (1919812644/2436671711, runs=400).
- Complete integrated sweep: 1,239 odd (456176795), 1,227 binary (3624253768),
  and 2,428 existing prime-model comparisons (1078173373), all with runs=300;
  222 existing point/curve tests pass in the same run.
- Final odd sweep with all 43 compact controls: 1,243 checks
  (416900735/runs=300). Follow-up torsion and relation comparisons: 189
  (1879752453/runs=60) and 243 (2182163364/runs=120), plus 32 unit controls.
- All eight workspace builds pass.
- All 389 documentation/storage checks pass, including two executable example
  tests for the native ordinary ternary kernels and actual Sage callers.
- All 568 full type diagnostics and continuations match the baseline after
  source-position normalization; no baseline refresh is needed.

## September 30 — finite-field initializer input forms (27.0.1)

The exported ellinit_Fq adapter accepted only five coefficients and failed on
native `[j]` and `[a4,a6]` inputs. Read elliptic.c:521/798/6563–6600 and mirrored
its model selection: coerce j into the field first, distinguish zero and 1728,
use the binary reciprocal and characteristic-three ordinary/supersingular
models, and expand two coefficients into the short equation. The existing
native finite-field invariant/model path then handles nonsingularity.

Twenty-six compact controls cover these discrepancies, with fresh recipes over
binary, ternary, other word and 127-bit prime fields. The native oracle compares
all thirteen invariants and the transformed model. Missing supported inputs
failed before the fix. No stored transcript data was added. Raw GEN record
vectors and malformed/mixed-field input boundaries remain outside this typed
tuple adapter and are still open audit items.

Validation: 1,565 odd comparisons (2657995022/runs=300), 1,531 binary comparisons
(1016244589/runs=300), 390 docs/storage checks including the new executed LLM
example, and eight builds pass. All 568 complete normalized typecheck diagnostic
blocks match the frozen baseline; no refresh. Version 27.0.1 is synchronized.
Next: legacy integer-only small-prime initialization and optimized scalar paths;
finite-extension cardinality/order/group backends and the broader audit remain.
